Jason Fladlien's Hook Mastery: Complete Breakdown &

AfterHarvest Applications

🎯  THE CORE HOOK DEFINITION

A hook gains attention that is:

1\. Favorable (not just attention, but the RIGHT attention)

2\. Qualified (targets the right sub-segment)

3\. Increases purchasing (prejudices them toward buying)

The Critical Insight

"Free sex and beer" gets attention, but loses it immediately when you pivot. Your hook must PRIME them

for the sale, not just grab eyeballs.

Jason's Framework: "100% of one dollar is still one dollar. 1% of a million dollars is a lot more."

Make it as narrow as possible to a sub-segment

While having the fattest, biggest sub-segment

That produces the most profit

🔥  HOOK PSYCHOLOGY: THE ELEMENTS

Every great hook contains SOME of these elements:

Element Purpose

Novel Fresh angle they haven't seen

Shocking Breaks their pattern

Engaging Interactive, pulls them in

Controversial Takes a stand

Specific Concrete, not vague

Exciting Creates anticipation

Dramatic High stakes

Unpredictable Keeps them guessing

Bold Confident claims

Emotional Hits feelings

Urgent Time-sensitive

Topical Timely, relevant now

Key Learning: You don't need ALL elements, but you need SOME. Mix and match based on what fits

your offer.

💎  THE 7 BEST HOOKS

1\. POP QUIZ

What it is: Start with interactive questions that reveal surprising facts

Why it works:

Interaction - Gets immediate engagement

Fast-paced - Creates "can't look away" momentum

Hits objections - Makes them realize they're wrong about assumptions

Embeds benefits - Shows scale/opportunity without directly claiming it

The Speed Example (YouTube Secrets):

Q1: "How many unique visitors on YouTube each month?" → 1 billion+

Q2: "What % of traffic comes from outside US?" → 70%

Q3: "Most subscribed channel subscribers?" → 13 million (Pewdiepie)

The Psychology: Don't make all questions easy. Let them get some wrong so they think "I need to learn

this."

Jason's Insight: "The comparison is what makes numbers meaningful. '6 billion hours watched' means

nothing until you say 'there are only 7 billion people on earth.'"

AfterHarvest Application: ⬇️

2\. PITCH UP FRONT

What it is: Tell them EXACTLY what you're selling in the first 60 seconds

Why it works:

Safer - No bait and switch feeling

Easier - Sets clear expectations

Unique - Most people hide the pitch

Self-selects - Wrong people leave early (saves everyone time)

Dr. Alan Barnard's Framework: "Impossible unless..."

Frame: This seems impossible

Bridge: Unless you have X

Reveal: I have X, here's proof

Examples:

WP Monster: "WordPress sites can make serious money IF you build them right"

Launchpad: "Amazing software but nobody can have it" (creates scarcity immediately)

Copy Eclass: "I'm going to teach you copywriting in the next 90 minutes"

Jason's Note: This can be your entire frame, not just the hook.

AfterHarvest Application: ⬇️

3\. FORTUNE TELLER

What it is: Predict their future based on current path vs. new path

Why it works:

Universal - Everyone wants to know their future

Simple - Easy to understand immediately

Sets up sale - Natural bridge to solution

Hits objections - Shows cost of inaction

The Affiliorama Example: They created a quiz/diagnostic that predicted:

Where you'll be in 6 months (current path)

Where you COULD be in 6 months (their path)

The exact gap and why it exists

Framework:

1\. "Here's where you're headed..." (pain)

2\. "Here's where you could be..." (pleasure)

3\. "Here's the one thing that determines which path you take..."

4\. "Let me show you how to guarantee path \#2"

AfterHarvest Application: ⬇️

4\. HYPE BUT TRUE\!?

What it is: Make an outrageous claim that seems like hype, then prove it's actually true

Why it works:

Hype is amoral - Big claims get attention

Intrigue - "Wait, is that real?"

Clickbait that delivers - You prove it, so no disappointment

ASM (Amazing Selling Machine) Examples:

"We're going to show you 18 people's full portfolios, live, 100% transparent"

"Every single student made money" (then showed proof)

"$10M+ in sales in first year" (then documented it)

The Psychology: The bigger the claim, the more attention. But you MUST prove it or you lose all

credibility.

Jason's Story - ASM Origin: "We had 18 one-on-ones and could show ALL their portfolios 100% in

public. That's the hook. 'Let me just show you everybody's results, then you decide if you want this.'"

AfterHarvest Application: ⬇️

5\. TALL TASK

What it is: Give them a seemingly impossible challenge, then help them accomplish it

Why it works:

Dramatic - High stakes immediately

Attacks resistance - "If I can do THIS, I can do anything"

Mission-based - Creates shared goal

Everyone loves a challenge - Taps into competitive spirit

Secrets of Closing Example: "Your challenge: Close 3 sales in the next 7 days using only what we teach

you tonight. Who's in?"

Framework:

1\. State the challenge (specific, measurable, time-bound)

2\. Acknowledge it sounds hard

3\. Show how it's actually achievable

4\. Give them the roadmap to do it

5\. Celebrate when they complete it

AfterHarvest Application: ⬇️

6\. IMMEDIATE EXPECTATION

What it is: Tell them exactly what to expect RIGHT NOW in bold, direct language

Why it works:

Shared experience - Creates "we're in this together" feeling

Topical - Addresses what's on their mind NOW

Lot to cover - Creates urgency to stay tuned

Sets tone - You're in control, confident

"Sometimes it's okay to go for the jugular" - Jason's words

Genius Webinars Example: "In the next 90 minutes, you're going to learn the exact webinar framework

I've used to generate over $100M in sales. I'm not going to hold anything back. You'll get the actual

scripts, the slide templates, the psychology behind every transition. By the end, you'll have everything

you need to run your first webinar. Let's go."

Amazing.com Pro Example: "Here's what's happening: Most people selling on Amazon are doing it

wrong. In the next hour, I'm going to show you the 7 mistakes they're making and exactly how to avoid

them. Some of you will get uncomfortable because you're making these mistakes right now. Good. That

means you're about to fix them."

AfterHarvest Application: ⬇️

7\. BENEFIT, BENEFIT, BENEFIT

What it is: Stack benefits rapid-fire, creating momentum and desire

Why it works:

Done \> Better - Focus on completion/results, not just improvement

Hope then pain - Build them up, then show the gap

Urgent tone - Creates FOMO

Easy Offline Formula Example: "You're going to learn how to:

Get local clients in 72 hours

Charge $2,000+ per client

Deliver in 2 hours per week

Build recurring revenue

Never touch ads or tech

Work from anywhere"

Framework:

1\. Stack 5-7 specific benefits (not features)

2\. Each one should be tangible/measurable

3\. Build momentum with pacing

4\. Then: "But here's the problem most people face..."

5\. Transition to your solution

AfterHarvest Application: ⬇️

🎨  THE 7 "EDGE CASE" HOOKS

Jason mentioned these are less common but powerful when they fit:

1\. Pain, Pain, Pain - Opposite of benefits. Stack the pain of NOT acting

2\. Left Field - Completely unexpected angle/analogy

3\. Intriguing Question - Open loop that demands answer

4\. Pressure Cooker - Time-sensitive scenario creating urgency

5\. Recent Development - "New discovery" or "just announced"

6\. Fill in the Blanks - Interactive framework building

7\. Between Us - Insider/confidential framing

🧠  JASON'S ADVANCED PRINCIPLES

1\. ISOLATE ELEMENTS FROM APPLICATION

What it means: When studying any marketing, separate:

The specific thing they did (application)

The universal principles behind it (elements)

Example: "Pop Quiz" isn't just asking questions. The elements are:

Interaction (gets engagement)

Pattern interrupt (breaks autopilot)

Social proof through comparison (makes claims believable)

Objection handling (proves assumptions wrong)

How to apply: Take any hook and ask "What elements make this work?" Then recombine those elements

for your situation.

2\. PEOPLE BUY WHEN LIMITS ARE REMOVED

Jason's Quote:

"People buy when their limits are removed, MORE SO than when the possibility of gain is presented.

Remove somebody's limits and they're more likely to buy from you."

The Psychology:

If they think they can't make $1, showing them how to make $1M won't work

But if you remove the excuse keeping them poor, they'll get rich as a byproduct

Go after objections as fast as possible

Example:

Bad: "You'll make $10K/month\!" (when they think they can't make $100)

Good: "The reason you're not making money isn't talent—it's this one belief..."

AfterHarvest Implication: Don't just sell results. Remove the beliefs blocking them from getting results.

3\. WORK BACKWARDS: HOOK BEFORE PRODUCT

Jason's Methodology: "I try to create the hook BEFORE the product or webinar"

Why this works:

Marketing-first approach

You know it'll be easy to sell

The product is designed around what converts

No "product in search of a hook" problem

Examples Jason Gave:

The Plan: "I had a hook before I had a product. We set up the situation where we had 18 one-on-ones

and could show all their portfolios 100% in public."

Launchpad: "The hook was intentionally designed—exclusivity and a massive supply/demand

skew."

Ramp Engine: "I met Trey Lewellen who'd run more products through ClickFunnels than anyone

including Russell Brunson. I said 'I want to do a deal with this guy. I don't care what we sell. The

story IS the hook.'"

The Framework:

1\. Find proof/story/angle that's inherently compelling

2\. Build product around that hook

3\. Launch with built-in credibility

Jason's Philosophy: "Proof before product"

4\. HOOKS ARE MORE IMPORTANT FOR REPLAY

Jason's Process: "I make more money on the replays than live webinars. So we need the hook because

that first 30 seconds somebody watches a video is make or break."

His Current System:

1\. Do small talk at beginning of live webinar

2\. Then: "I'm going to take a deep breath. I'd love for you to join me right now."

3\. "Breathe in with me now. Hold it. Release. Let's begin."

4\. Everything after "Let's begin" is scripted

5\. Team cuts replay to start at "Let's begin"

6\. First 100 words are optimized to hook replay viewers

Why this matters:

Live audience is warm (they showed up, invested time)

Replay audience is cold (scrolling, distracted)

Replay needs stronger hook than live

AfterHarvest Application: Design your hook for replay viewers, not just live attendees.

5\. TWO DIFFERENT HOOKS

Jason's Principle: "The hook to get them TO the webinar doesn't have to be the same as the webinar

hook itself"

Example Structure:

Email/Ad Hook: "Former Amazon insider reveals the 7-figure product formula"

Webinar Hook: Pop quiz about Amazon statistics

Why separate:

Pre-webinar hook = curiosity, intrigue, registration

Webinar hook = proof, credibility, buying mindset

6\. CALIBRATION IS EVERYTHING

Jason's Story - Numerology List: "This is a numerology list. They've never been on a webinar before. I

can't start with a crazy hook. I'm starting with 'this is the chat box.' They're Alice in Wonderland—'where

the hell am I?' You're in a webinar, everything's gonna be okay."

His Opening:

"Welcome, thanks for attending this live web-based training \[not 'webinar'\]. This is where you can ask

questions \[shows chat\]. My name is Jason Fladlien. I'm a 5-11-1 combination, perfect for attracting

wealth and teaching others how to do the same \[took their same test, speaks their language\]."

Calibration Examples:

Options Traders (John Carter partnership): "Would you go into a market where you could only

buy and never sell? No? That's Amazon. But 57% of profits come from third-party sellers..."

Bob Proctor Audience: Used "wealth potential" not "money." Used "spiritual" language. Gave away

first edition Think and Grow Rich.

The Principle: "All you gotta do is change a couple slides."

Framework:

1\. Research your audience's language/beliefs

2\. Mirror their terminology

3\. Reference their shared experiences

4\. Meet them where they are, THEN lead them

7\. STATISTICS NEED COMPARISON

Jason's Rule: "If you're ever using statistics, they have to have a comparison. Otherwise numbers are

meaningless and are not very effective for conversion."

Bad: "YouTube has 6 billion hours watched per month" Good: "YouTube has 6 billion hours watched per

month. That's almost one hour for every person on Earth."

Bad: "70% of YouTube traffic is from outside the US"

Good: "70% of YouTube traffic is from outside the US. That means this is a GLOBAL opportunity, not

just American."

Framework:

Raw number + Human comparison = Impact

Raw percentage + Implication = Meaning

Statistics alone = Boring

Statistics + "which means" = Compelling

8\. POP QUIZ SPECIFIC TACTICS

Question Design Rules:

1\. Don't make all questions easy - Let them get some wrong

2\. Build toward a point - Each question reinforces the narrative

3\. Use escalating numbers - Show growth/scale

4\. Make them type answers - Forces participation

5\. Reveal quickly - "Boom\! The answer is..."

6\. Add comparisons - Make numbers meaningful

The Speed Reference: Started his first quiz with "Pop Quiz, Hot Shot\!" from the movie Speed (2008,

when it was timely). "Now nobody remembers Speed anymore so we don't do that."

Lesson: Even your hook references need to be calibrated to what's current/relevant to your audience

NOW.

9\. START FAST, ADD COMPLEXITY LATER

Jason's Advice: "When I started, I kept it simple. Training wheels. Then you build up and go on from

there."

The Journey:

Beginner: Use one of the 7 hooks as-is

Intermediate: Combine elements from multiple hooks

Advanced: Create hooks before products, manufacture proof

Don't try to be Jason right away. Pick ONE hook type, master it, then expand.

💰  MANUFACTURING HOOKS: ADVANCED STRATEGY

The "Proof Before Product" Methodology

Three Examples Jason Gave:

1\. The Plan (18 One-on-Ones)

Hook: "We're showing you ALL 18 people's full portfolios, live, 100% transparent"

How it was manufactured: Dan did most of the webinar, Jason just closed

Why it worked: Social proof was the hook, built in before product existed

2\. Launchpad (Artificial Scarcity)

Hook: "This is the greatest software ever, but nobody can have it"

How it was manufactured: Intentionally designed exclusivity and supply/demand skew

Why it worked: Scarcity creates desire; the limitation WAS the hook

3\. Ramp Engine (Authority by Association)

Hook: "The guy who's outsold everybody on ClickFunnels (including Russell Brunson)"

How it was manufactured: Found Trey Lewellen, partnered specifically FOR the story

Jason's Quote: "I don't care what we sell. The story is the hook."

The Principle: Don't find products and try to create hooks. Find/create hooks, THEN build products

around them.

Alex Hormozi Story: "When It's Easy, Go Hard"

Context: Jason met Alex Hormozi and Layla at an event when they'd just broken through.

Jason's Advice:

"When it's easy, that's when you go hard. It's raining money—why are you sleeping in the house? Get

a damn bucket."

Lesson from ASM: "Other affiliates: 'I'm not doing anything and making $1 million\!' Will and I: 'Dude,

it's RAINING MONEY outside. Get a bucket\!'"

Application: When you find product-market fit, SCALE AGGRESSIVELY. Don't coast.

📊  THE HOOK HIERARCHY

Jason's implicit framework for choosing hooks:

TIER 1: Universally Strong

1\. Pop Quiz - Works for almost anything

2\. Pitch Up Front - Safe, clear, effective

3\. Fortune Teller - Appeals to everyone

TIER 2: Situation-Dependent

4\. Hype But True - Need proof to back it up

5\. Tall Task - Need actionable challenge

6\. Immediate Expectation - Need confident positioning

TIER 3: Contextual

7\. Benefit Stack - Better for transactional offers

8\. Edge Cases - Use when they fit perfectly

How to Choose:

Start with Tier 1 (especially Pop Quiz)

Move to Tier 2 when you have proof/positioning

Use Tier 3 for specific situations

🎯  AFTERHARVEST: DIRECT APPLICATIONS

Now let's apply EVERYTHING to your retention/implementation business...

YOUR POSITIONING HOOK OPTIONS

Option A: Pop Quiz (Recommended First)

"The Retention Reality Check"

Q1: "What percentage of course buyers actually FINISH the course they purchased?"

A) 10% B) 25% C) 40% D) 60%

Answer: 10% → "Which means 90% of your customers feel like they wasted their money"

Q2: "What's the \#1 reason students don't finish courses?"

A) Too busy B) Lost motivation C) Poor course structure D) Lack of accountability

Answer: C - Poor course structure → "It's not them, it's YOU"

Q3: "If you doubled completion rates, what would happen to referrals?"

A) Stay same B) Increase 20% C) Increase 50% D) Increase 200%+

Answer: D - 200%+ → "Happy customers are your best marketing"

Q4: "What's the ROI of spending $10K on acquisition vs $10K on retention?"

A) Same B) 2x better C) 5x better D) 10x better

Answer: D - 10x better → "You're leaving millions on the table"

Q5: "How many course creators have a SYSTEM for student success?"

A) 80% B) 50% C) 20% D) Less than 5%

Answer: D - Less than 5% → "This is your competitive moat"

Why This Works:

Addresses their biggest pain (low completion)

Shows them they're wrong about WHY (structure, not students)

Quantifies the opportunity cost (ROI)

Positions you as the solution (the 5%)

Option B: Fortune Teller

"Your Two Possible Futures"

Path 1: Keep Doing What You're Doing

6 months: Completed 2 more launches, made $150K, lost 70% to refunds/churn

12 months: Exhausted from constant launching, still at $20K/month

18 months: Burned out, students complaining, reputation damaged

Path 2: Fix Your Backend First

6 months: Same launch revenue BUT 80% completion, 5% refunds, testimonials flooding in

12 months: $40K/month mostly from repeat buyers and referrals, working less

18 months: $100K/month, premium pricing, waitlist of dream clients

The One Difference: Implementation Systems

Then prove it with case studies.

Option C: Pitch Up Front (Bold)

"I Help Course Creators Stop Hemorrhaging Money"

"Here's what I'm selling: Implementation systems. For $15K, I'll rebuild your course backend so students

actually finish and get results. This isn't about getting more students—it's about making the students you

ALREADY HAVE worth 10x more through retention and referrals.

If you're making under $200K/year, this isn't for you yet. If you're making $200K+ and losing students to

refunds, ghosting, or lack of results, let me show you the exact system that's working for \[X clients\]."

Why This Works:

Clear offer immediately

Self-selects (filters out broke people)

Positions as premium solution

Creates authority through exclusivity

Option D: Hype But True\!?

"Nobody Failed. Zero Refunds. 97% Completion."

"I'm going to make a claim that sounds like complete BS: One of my clients went from 15% completion

to 97% completion in 90 days. Zero refunds in that period. Every single student got results.

Sounds impossible, right? Like I'm making it up?

Let me show you the exact Kajabi backend, the student testimonials, and the bank account screenshots.

Then I'll show you the 7-step implementation system that made it happen."

Then deliver proof.

Option E: Tall Task (Challenge-Based)

"The 30-Day Retention Sprint"

"Here's your challenge: Take your worst-performing course—the one with the highest refund rate, lowest

completion, most complaints—and triple its completion rate in the next 30 days.

Sounds impossible? That's what \[Client Name\] thought. Then we did it. And then we did it again with 6

more clients.

Tonight I'm going to give you the exact 30-day roadmap. Who's ready to do this?"

CALIBRATION FOR YOUR AUDIENCE

Course Creator Language:

✅  "Implementation systems"

✅  "Student success"

✅  "Completion rates"

✅  "Churn reduction"

❌  "Customer success" (too corporate)

❌  "Retention optimization" (too technical)

Pain Points to Address (Objections):

1\. "I don't have time to fix this" → Show time ROI

2\. "I need more students first" → Show math that retention \> acquisition

3\. "My students are just lazy" → Prove it's structure, not students

4\. "This sounds complicated" → Show simple implementation

5\. "I can't afford this" → Show opportunity cost of NOT doing it

Proof Elements Needed:

Before/after completion rates

Before/after revenue from same cohort

Student testimonials (social proof)

Time savings for creator

Specific numbers (not vague)

📚  LESSONS TO ALWAYS REMEMBER

1\. Hooks \> Everything Else

Jason spends "a lot of time on the hook, a lot of time." It's not an afterthought—it's the foundation.

2\. Work Backwards

Try to create the hook BEFORE the product. "Proof before product."

3\. Test, Don't Guess

Jason has created hundreds of webinars. He has data. You need to test your hooks and track what works.

4\. Calibrate Always

What works for one audience fails for another. "All you gotta do is change a couple slides."

5\. Remove Limits, Don't Just Show Possibility

People buy when objections are removed more than when dreams are presented.

6\. Replay \> Live

Optimize for replay viewers. They're cold. They need a stronger hook.

7\. Interaction \> Presentation

Webinars should be conversations, not lectures. Get them typing, answering, engaging.

8\. Start Simple, Add Complexity Later

Use the 7 proven hooks. Don't try to reinvent the wheel on day one.

9\. Comparison Makes Numbers Meaningful

Never use statistics without context. "6 billion hours" means nothing without "there are only 7 billion

people."

10\. Manufacturing Beats Discovering

You don't find hooks—you create them by designing proof, exclusivity, and stories into your business.

🎯  YOUR 30-DAY ACTION PLAN

Week 1: Hook Research

 Watch 10 webinars in your space

 Identify which hook type they use

 Note what works/doesn't work

 Choose YOUR primary hook type

Week 2: Hook Creation

 Write 3 versions of your chosen hook

 Test on 5 people (voice feedback)

 Refine based on response

 Create 5-slide deck

Week 3: Hook Deployment

 Send 20 Loom outreach videos with hook

 Track response rate

 A/B test different hooks

 Double down on winner

Week 4: Hook Optimization

 Analyze what's working

 Create hook variants for different channels

 Document your "hook playbook"

 Train Thea on your best hooks

Jason's Most Valuable Insight:

"I try to create the hook BEFORE the product or the webinar ideally. But when I started, I kept it

simple. So you know, training wheels. Then you build up and you go on from there."

What This Means for AfterHarvest:

Don't wait until you have 50 case studies to create your hook. Create a SIMPLE hook now:

"I help course creators double completion rates in 90 days."

Then prove it with 1 client. Then 2. Then 10.

The hook evolves as the proof compounds.

Start simple. Start now.

FINAL CHECKLIST: IS YOUR HOOK READY?

 Does it gain FAVORABLE attention? (not just any attention)

 Does it qualify your ideal customer? (repels wrong people)

 Does it increase purchasing likelihood? (primes them to buy)

 Does it contain 3+ hook elements? (novel, specific, urgent, etc.)

 Can you say it in 30 seconds? (for replay viewers)

 Does it address objections? (removes limits)

 Is it calibrated to your audience? (their language)

 Do you have proof to back it up? (case studies, numbers)

 Is it simple enough to test? (don't need perfection)

 Can Thea deliver it too? (systematized)

If you checked 7+, you're ready to deploy.
