"I use a lot. I use a lot of testimonials in the introduction because it sets the stage of what's at stake."

"That's some of the ways in which you could use testimonials, especially in the introduction. I use a lot."

# THE "PERSON vs PROCESS" CLOSE

Jason says this is one of his favorites and he uses it a lot.

## The Setup - Code Breaking Secret Society Example

"With that said, I've been part of a code-breaking secret society whose sole intent is to manipulate and exploit Google for profit. What's important is when you break the code, it isn't the person anymore - it's the process."

## The Logic Jason Explains

"So if it's a person, it would require skill and ability and intelligence and experience. But if it's a process, then anybody of any age or anybody of any experience or somebody with no skills or ability could do it. Because it's not the person, it's the process. So person or process?"

### Then He Shows The Proof:

Here's Daryl, he's 67, started in March 2014, six figures in debt, he's currently doing over $1,500 a day, $50,000 per month and loving it.

Here's David and Tess, they came to America not speaking a lick of English, in 90 days everything changed, they're making $17,000 in affiliate commissions.

Here's Michael, he's 19, he's already generated $42,000 since he started even though this was his first 1K day and it was not too long ago.

Here's Josh Fletcher, he was making $20,000 a year with his job, within four months he was making $10,000 per month.

"And then I show like six more. I think a lot of them, there was like baby, baby, baby, baby, baby. And then I tie it down."

### The Tie Down:

"It's not the person, it's the process. And I tie it down because if it's the person, then it means that YOU are the variable. But if it's the process, it doesn't mean so."

## Wholesale Formula Example

"In the Wholesale Formula, we use the same close. I go - we show their billion dollars in customer results. I have a picture, it has all the demographics on it - young, old, white, yellow, brown, black, and any color in between. You know, ages 17 to 77, something like that."

"And I say: 'It's not the person, it's the process.' And I said: 'And that's exciting.'"

"And I go: 'Now, as an individual, you are unique. You are a beautiful little snowflake. So as an individual, it's wonderful. But in Amazon, you're just another cog in the machine. You mean nothing to them. You are just another thing. It's not you, it's the process.'"

### The Final Close:

"So the good news is, if you just follow the process, you can achieve the profits. That's kind of the big thing that I build them up to."

"I love that close - it's not the person, it's the process - because then you're inconsequential. Yeah, you suck? OK cool, I'll take you, just follow the process. Yeah, you're great? Alright, you too, just follow the process. I have the process."

## The Critical Rule:

"But you gotta prove it. You gotta have - remember: extraordinary claims require extraordinary proof."

# IMPLEMENTATION: HOW TO EXCITE

## Jason's Core Rules

### 1\. Make sure it's in the intro

"A lot of people don't push to the edge of hype, and then you can pull it back a little bit."

### 2\. Push to the edge of hype

Don't be timid. Go harder than feels comfortable, then calibrate back if needed.

### 3\. Use at least one technique

Pick from the 7 excitement techniques and commit to using it thoroughly.

### 4\. Try to use more, then garnish the rest

"A little bit here, a little bit there, sprinkle a little bit here, a touch of it there, and then pretty soon it'll become second nature. You'll just start doing it more often naturally as a byproduct."

## Other Critical Considerations

### Excitement continues throughout

"Excitement will continue to be used throughout. You don't just use it in the intro."

### Push-Pull Strategy

"Push-pull is great because it helps you enhance excitement without becoming manic."

"If it's all like 'this is the greatest thing ever, this is the best thing ever, there's no flaws in it, it's one of a kind, million beautiful blah blah blah' - people don't believe you because it's too good to be true."

### The "Too Good to Be False" Reframe

"Although later on I could teach you the close: 'Is it too good to be true or is it too good to be false?' They say 'what do you mean?' Yeah, that's an interesting question, isn't it? Is it too good to be false?"

### Carrot and Stick

"We want to use carrot and stick so we get:

•    The pain and the gain

•    The benefit and the drawback

•    The limitation and the miracle

•       The aspiration

# JASON'S SELF-CRITIQUE & TRANSPARENCY

On the Launchpad copy:

"I look at this, I should have more outcomes in here. I have too many features and not enough benefits and outcomes. But you know, live and learn, right?"

# KEY JASON FLADLIEN QUOTES

### On Feature/Benefit/Outcome:

"Too many features, not enough benefits, and oh, so few outcomes. I want my webinars dripping with benefits and outcomes and pain (which is the reverse - it's just a benefit in different clothing)."

### On Exploit:

"This is my Moby Dick. I am looking for him all the time in the wild. I wanna find something that - yeah, my customers shouldn't be able to make money - however, there's a hack, there's an exploit, there's something, there's a glitch in the matrix that would allow ordinary people to make extraordinary income."

### On Manipulation:

"Algorithms can be manipulated. What's the implication of that? It means you can exploit it for personal gain. And that's exciting to people whether you want to admit it or not. I mean, travel enough of the world and you will see how selfishly people will act, especially the more they're backed into a corner. So we can ignore that or we can lean into it. I prefer to lean into it."

### On Results/Proof:

"Extraordinary claims require extraordinary proof."

### On Testimonials:

"There's so many testimonials, so many people don't use that they should be using. When I work with clients, I love it because I go through their testimonials and I find them all. I don't do it for myself because, you know, the cobbler's kids have no shoes. But I do it for others. I sit there and spend hours finding every testimonial I can."

### On Person vs Process:

"It's not the person, it's the process. You are inconsequential. Yeah, you suck? OK cool, I'll take you, just follow the process. Yeah, you're great? Alright, you too, just follow the process. I have the process."

### On Stack and Dimensionalize:

"Look at all the different ways that I've essentially said 'to make money' - all these turn of phrases, all these different slight variations of the same thing. This is how we stack and dimensionalize benefits. Stack and dimensionalize things."

### On Naturalizing Excitement:

"A little bit here, a little bit there, sprinkle a little bit here, a touch of it there, and then pretty soon it'll become second nature. You'll just start doing it more often naturally as a byproduct."

### On the Webinar Itself:

"I don't wanna do a damn webinar. I don't wanna do another webinar just like you don't wanna do another webinar. But we have to do the webinar to make the money - that's the benefit. And then we make the money so we don't have to worry about money - that's the outcome."

# FINAL SUMMARY

The Core Lesson:

Even with clients paying $3,125/hour, Jason finds that 9 out of 10 times, the problem is they're stuck on features instead of moving to benefits and outcomes. This isn't about intelligence - it's about discipline.

The Framework:

•    Feature = What it is

•    Benefit = What it does

•       Outcome = How it changes your life

The 7 Techniques:

•    Exploit - The Moby Dick, the glitch in the matrix

•    Results - Stack 10-20+ testimonials showing everyone winning

•    Bling - Visual success (homes, cars, travel)

•    Easy - Little effort, no experience required

•    Aspiration - Higher purpose, becoming your best self

•    Story - Jesus told stories, so should you

•    Quick - Instant gratification beats delayed bigger wins

•       Timing (bonus) - Right place, right time

The Power Close:

Person vs Process - Show diverse results proving anyone can do it, then close with "It's not the person, it's the process. You are inconsequential. Just follow the process."

The Implementation:

•    Push to the edge of hype (don't be timid)

•    Use at least one technique heavily

•    Garnish with others throughout

•    Use push-pull to avoid being manic

•    Stack and dimensionalize benefits (say it 7 different ways)

•       Use carrot and stick (pain and gain)

Jason Fladlien's Positioning Masterclass

Complete Notes & Frameworks

THE CORE CONCEPT: TWO POSITIONS

Every great webinar requires positioning in two critical areas:

1\. Position YOU

How you're uniquely qualified to solve this problem for your customers.

The mindset: "They can't solve this particular problem from anyone else. Either they solve it through you,

or they remain hopeless."

This is the frame Jason enters every webinar with. He thinks: "You could go on 100 other webinars from

100 other people and you're screwed when it comes to this specific thing. Nobody's gonna do it better

than me."

Key Philosophy: "WITHOUT me, you have no hope."

2\. Position THE OPPORTUNITY

Why it's hot, why it needs to be done NOW.

Why it's urgent and timely

Why others who try to do it the normal way will fall short

Why YOUR specific way or approach is the difference between winning or not

The frame: "You're with me in this opportunity, you win. You're not, you lose."

THE "I'M KIND OF A BIG DEAL" FRAMEWORK

Jason breaks down positioning yourself through 5 core elements:

1\. Proof

2\. Motive

3\. Analogy

4\. Story

5\. Niched Down

Each element works together to establish your authority and uniqueness.

PROOF: THE MOST CRITICAL ELEMENT

The Proof Hierarchy (Most Powerful to Least)

1\. DIRECT RESULTS

This is the gold standard. Real, tangible outcomes you've personally achieved.

Examples:

"Here's how much we sold on Amazon last month"

"Here's the money in my bank account"

For weight loss: actual pounds lost

For tax: actual dollars saved

For trauma healing: specific trauma healed

Jason's Philosophy on Direct Results:

Show it "in the wild" - not on a spreadsheet anyone can fake

Use screenshots from actual platforms

Get on Zoom calls to capture real dashboards

The more authentic, the more believable

Real Example from Jason: His $1M webinar story - he was only supposed to sell 100 spots at $10,000

each. He sold out in less than 60 minutes. He shows the text message from Will telling him to STOP

selling. This direct proof makes future scarcity claims more believable.

2\. INDIRECT RESULTS

These support direct results but aren't the primary goal.

Examples:

"Got on more podcasts" → leads to more money

"More products live on Amazon" → leads to more sales

These are stepping stones to the real result people want.

3\. SECONDARY RESULTS

Supporting metrics that validate your approach.

4\. CUSTOMER RESULTS

What your clients/students have achieved. This is proof by proxy.

5\. TESTIMONIALS

Direct quotes from satisfied customers.

6\. ACCOLADES

Awards, recognition, media features, industry honors.

7\. VISUALS

Photos, videos, screenshots that make everything more real and tangible.

The Quantity Principle

"Quantity becomes its own quality when it comes to proof."

Jason's approach: "Smear your slides with as much proof as possible."

Most people could include 10x more results in the same amount of time. The goal is overwhelming

evidence that you're the real deal.

Key Insight: There are countless ways to demonstrate proof. Don't limit yourself to one type. Layer

different forms of proof throughout your presentation.

THE TEACHING FRAMEWORK: RUSSIAN NESTING DOLLS

Jason explains his content structure methodology that Alex Hormozi learned from him:

The Three-Layer Framework

Layer 1: THE PARADIGM

The thing that changes everything

The "aha moment"

The insight that makes people say "Oh my God, this changes everything. My life will never be the

same."

Layer 2: THE VITAL FEW

Typically 3-5 major points

The first major point, second major point, third major point

These are the legs of the stool supporting the paradigm

Layer 3: THE ELEMENTS

Within each major point, there are 3 sub-components

Each sub-component can split into 3 more if needed

Like Russian nesting dolls - each concept contains smaller concepts

Example of Nesting:

POSITION (paradigm)

YOU (vital few element \#1)

Proof (element within YOU)

Direct Results (sub-element)

Indirect Results (sub-element)

Customer Results (sub-element)

This framework works for ANY teaching, not just webinars. It's an easy way to teach and have people

follow along.

MOTIVE: THE "WHY" BEHIND YOUR AUTHORITY

Motive answers: "Why are you the one teaching this?"

This goes beyond just having results. It's about your backstory, your journey, what drives you.

The Genius Behind ASM Examples

Jason references multiple Amazon courses (ASM 3, ASM 4) where he positioned himself and others

through:

Personal journey stories

Transformation narratives

The "why now" element

What's at stake

The motive creates connection and makes your authority more human and believable.

ANALOGY: MAKING COMPLEX IDEAS SIMPLE

Analogies are powerful teaching tools that help people:

Understand complex concepts quickly

Remember your teachings longer

See connections they wouldn't otherwise make

Jason's Everest Analogy:

He tells the story of Ed Viesturs, the first American to summit all 14 of the 8,000-meter peaks without

supplemental oxygen. Ed also had the \#1 grossing IMAX documentary of all time.

The marketing insight: "There used to be the first person to ever climb Everest, then the first person in

this country, then the youngest person, and now it's like the first person with one extra nipple to climb

Everest."

The Lesson: They just keep carving it up, carving it up. In marketing, you should pick a position where

you're FIRST because there's nobody else there.

Application: Carve the market up so small that you can claim "first" in something meaningful.

STORY: THE ULTIMATE PERSUASION TOOL

Stories serve multiple functions:

Illustrate your points

Create emotional connection

Make you memorable

Embed lessons in ways facts cannot

Jason weaves stories throughout every webinar - personal stories, client stories, historical stories,

analogies that become stories.

NICHING IT DOWN: BECOMING FIRST IN YOUR CATEGORY

The Core Strategy: If you can't be first overall, carve up the market until you ARE first in something

valuable.

How to Carve Your Market

Think in terms of:

Geography ("First in Colorado")

Demographics ("First for women over 50")

Methodology ("First using X approach")

Industry/Niche ("First for e-commerce sellers")

Result Speed ("First to achieve X in Y timeframe")

Unique Combination ("First to combine A + B + C")

The Goal: Find a position where you can honestly say: "I'm the only one who can solve your problem

this way."

THE 4 LEVELS OF SELF-POSITIONING

Jason reveals a hierarchy of positioning sophistication:

Level 1: MVP (Minimum Viable Positioning)

QR Control Example: Basic positioning - you have something valuable, and you present it competently.

Establishes baseline credibility.

Level 2: Quick & Great

LinkedInception Example: More sophisticated positioning where you stack multiple forms of proof and

authority quickly. You establish dominance in your space rapidly.

Level 3: Big Bopper

Product eClass Example: You're positioned as THE authority. Heavy credentials, undeniable results,

market leader status. You've "made it" and everyone knows it.

Level 4: Inception (The Ultimate Level)

The highest form of positioning: YOU position SOMEONE ELSE as the star.

This is Jason's secret weapon. When he pitches supplements for Manuel Suarez, he does it this way:

The Inception Structure:

1\. First, position yourself (the presenter) as credible

2\. Then, position the opportunity as incredible

3\. Finally, position THE STAR (the person who created the offer)

4\. You become the cheerleader, not the star

Why This Works:

"If I talk about me: blah"

"If YOU talk about me: let's go\!"

When you champion someone else, it's more believable

You get to split a bigger pie (even if you split it, the total is larger)

Jason's Manuel Suarez Example:

"The more webinars I do, like the one you're on right now, the more millionaires I make, the bigger my

rep gets. The bigger my rep, the more businesses knock on my door showing me their thing, begging me

to get behind it, partner with them and bring it to the world.

And my answer is almost always NO.

That's not to say there aren't a lot of great opportunities out there, but I'm not interested in great. I'm

interested in GREATEST.

I'm lucky if I find one thing a year that passes my BFF test."

The BFF Test (Analogy): "If my best friend in the world came to me and he was limited on money and

time, and had to absolutely make money or his whole world would collapse, what would I recommend to

him?"

Then he introduces Manuel as someone who PASSES this test.

The Two Inception Approaches

Approach A: Reveal the Star Early

Position yourself first

Then immediately elevate someone else as THE expert

You become the guide introducing the master

Used when you don't have deep credentials in that specific niche

Approach B: Reveal the Star Late

Build yourself up throughout most of the webinar

Establish YOUR Amazon credentials

Then, right before the pitch, reveal someone EVEN BETTER

"I bow in the presence of greatness of Dan and Dylan"

Used when you DO have credentials but someone else has MORE

Jason's Amazon Example: With the Wholesale Formula (Dan and Dylan), he builds up his own Amazon

credibility throughout, then reveals at the end that he bows to Dan and Dylan's greatness. This is

incredibly powerful positioning.

THE CUSTOMER JOURNEY THROUGH POSITIONING

Jason maps out the psychological progression:

Stage 1: "I'm not gonna do business with anybody at all, period."

Stage 2: "Oh my god, this opportunity is so great, I'll do business with ANYBODY who has it."

Stage 3: "Wait a second, I only do business with the LEGITIMATE folks."

Stage 4: "I only do business with YOU."

Your positioning should guide prospects through these stages systematically.

HOW TO APPLY: THE 3-PART FORMULA

1\. Find a Position You Can TAKE, HOLD, and EXTEND

Take: Claim your position at the beginning of your presentation

Hold: Maintain that frame throughout your content (don't waver or apologize for your authority)

Extend: Elevate your position further in the pitch (reveal you're even cooler than they thought)

2\. Ideally Choose a Position That:

Crushes the competition (makes competitors look inferior by comparison)

Enhances desire to do business (makes working with you irresistible)

3\. Assert That Position With Maximum Elements

Deploy as many of the positioning elements (Proof, Motive, Analogy, Story, Niching) as dramatically as

possible.

CRITICAL CONSIDERATIONS

1\. How You Speak and Carry Yourself MATTERS

The Congruence Principle: Don't be the expert who says "Today I'm here to talk to you about

happiness" in a boring, flat tone. You're lost.

You must be congruent in delivery and tone with your positioning.

Jason's Evolution:

Early days: "I'm not a guru" worked because he didn't look like one

Now: He OWNS being a guru because that's his reputation

He leans into being direct because that's what he's known for

The Ladies at the Event: Sweet ladies told Jason: "We love your webinars but we don't go on them

anymore... because we always end up buying something\!" (laughing)

Jason's response: "You're taking a break from my webinars. You'll be back one day, won't you?"

He OWNS his reputation for converting. That's his position now.

2\. Engineer the Position If Possible

Don't wait for your position to emerge naturally. Design it intentionally.

Think: "What position do I WANT to hold?" Then build toward it.

3\. The Position of the Point Is MORE IMPORTANT Than the Point Itself

This is one of Jason's most profound insights.

Example: "Losing weight is super easy. You just reduce calories. Calories in, calories out."

That's the POINT. Everyone knows it already.

But if you say it that way, nobody will care. They'll be upset. They'll gain MORE weight to spite you.

The Truth: Most of your audience already KNOWS the points you're going to make. They're not

following through on them.

What Matters: How you POSITION that point. How you set it up. The context, the frame, the journey to

that insight.

The Comedy Analogy: In a comedy routine, comedians spend FAR more time on the SETUP than on the

punchline.

But most webinars are just: "Punchline, punchline, punchline, punchline."

No setup. No positioning. Just information dumping.

The Fix: Spend your time crafting the SETUP of your points. The positioning IS the value.

If you can get leverage to set up the point properly, that is MORE IMPORTANT than the point itself.

JASON'S PERSONAL POSITIONING PRINCIPLES

The "Only I Can Solve This" Mindset

"I wanna do it on something where this is true with me: WITHOUT me, you have NO hope. You could go

on 100 other webinars from 100 other people and you're screwed when it comes to this specific thing that

I'm teaching. Nobody's gonna do it better than me."

This is the confidence (and truth) Jason operates from.

Finding What's Actually TRUE

"I try to find the things that are TRUE, and then I build the presentations around that. I don't force square

peg into round hole."

Don't fake your positioning. Find what's genuinely true about your unique ability, then amplify it.

Carving Up the Market

"How do we carve it up so we can be the first? I'm kind of a big deal, right? That's your positioning."

If you can't be first in the broad market, carve it up until you ARE first somewhere meaningful.

ADVANCED TACTICS & INSIGHTS

The Proof Multiplication Strategy

Jason shares his approach with clients like Trey:

"The most difficult thing I had to do dealing with Trey was he wouldn't give me results because he was

weird about showing off. So I had to sneak them in and then tell him later - beg forgiveness, not ask

permission.

We got on a Zoom so he could show me his stuff, and I did some screenshots. Put them in the webinar

anyway."

Lesson: Sometimes you need to be creative about extracting proof. Always be thinking: "How many

ways can I show this result?"

Demonstrating Results "In the Wild"

"Notice how I try to do it in the wild, not on a spreadsheet in Google. Cause anybody can put any number

in this."

Authentic proof from actual platforms (Amazon dashboards, bank accounts, real software) is infinitely

more believable than manufactured screenshots.

The Wholesale Formula Positioning

Jason uses different positioning approaches with different partners:

With Manuel (Supplements):

Can't claim supplement expertise himself

Positions Manuel as the star from the beginning

Becomes the trusted advisor introducing the expert

With Dan & Dylan (Wholesale Formula):

Has his own Amazon credentials

Builds himself up first

Then reveals he "bows in the presence" of Dan & Dylan

Uses mostly selling software (print-on-demand private label supplements)

Different offer structure requires different positioning

The Insight: Your positioning should adapt to your offer and your genuine relationship to the expertise.

THE LANGUAGE AND VOICE OF POSITIONING

Be Congruent With Your Reputation

Jason can pitch in ANY voice: "If you say 'hey listen, we discovered a planet where there's Klingon, Star

Trek had it right after all,' I could learn Klingon and sell that to the market. Give me a month or two and

I'll pitch in Klingon if the money's right."

But the key is authenticity to YOUR position.

Own Your Edge

If you're known for being direct, be MORE direct.

If you're known for being educational, lean into deep teaching.

If you're known for being inspirational, amplify that.

Don't try to be someone you're not.

THE THREE FUNDAMENTAL HUMAN DESIRES

Jason breaks down what people ultimately want:

The Positive Frame:

1\. More money

2\. Look sexier

3\. Better relationships

The Negative Frame (Often More Powerful):

1\. Don't wanna be broke

2\. Don't wanna be ugly

3\. Don't wanna have bad relationships

"Everything goes back to one of those things."

Application: Connect your positioning to one of these core desires, using BOTH the positive and

negative frame for maximum leverage.

FINAL FRAMEWORKS & REMINDERS

The Complete Positioning Checklist

When preparing your presentation, ensure you have:

For YOU:

 Direct results clearly demonstrated

 Indirect/secondary results supporting the direct

 Customer results and testimonials

 Accolades and credentials

 Visual proof throughout

 Your motive/why clearly articulated

 Analogies that simplify complex ideas

 Stories that illustrate key points

 Niche positioning that makes you "first"

For THE OPPORTUNITY:

 Why it's hot NOW

 Why urgency matters

 Why the normal approach fails

 Why YOUR approach is the difference

 Win/lose frame clearly established

The Positioning Hierarchy of Impact

Lowest Impact: Facts and information alone

Low Impact: Facts with some proof

Medium Impact: Proof + positioning yourself as credible

High Impact: Everything above + positioning the opportunity as urgent

Highest Impact: All of the above + positioning someone ELSE as the star (Inception level)

KEY QUOTES TO REMEMBER

On Being the Only Solution: "They can't solve this particular problem from anyone else. Either they

solve it through you, or they remain hopeless."

On Market Positioning: "Pick a position where you're first, because there's nobody else in there, and it's

still worth being in that market."

On Quantity of Proof: "Quantity becomes its own quality when it comes to proof."

On Teaching Structure: "I always think in terms of everything like Russian nesting dolls. I'm gonna

teach you one thing. That one thing has three components. Each of these components splinters off into

three more components."

On Setup vs. Point: "The position of the point is more important than the point itself. Most of you, the

points you're gonna make, the audience kind of already knows them. They're not following them. But if

we can get leverage to set up the point properly, that is more important than the point itself."

On Comedy and Webinars: "If you look at a comedy routine, they spend far more time on the setup than

they do on the punchline. But yet I see so many webinars that are just punchline, punchline, punchline."

On Ultimate Positioning: "The greatest position you could ever be in is to be somebody else's

cheerleader."

On Belief and Truth: "I try to find the things that are TRUE, and then I build the presentations around

that. I don't force square peg into round hole."

IMPLEMENTATION ROADMAP

Step 1: Audit Your Current Position

What are you already known for?

What results can you prove?

What makes you different?

What's your "Ed Viesturs moment"? (The unique achievement)

Step 2: Carve Your Niche

Where can you honestly claim "first"?

How can you combine attributes to create a unique position?

What specific problem can ONLY you solve?

Step 3: Gather Your Proof Arsenal

Direct results (prioritize these)

Indirect results

Customer results

Testimonials

Accolades

Visuals

Aim for 10x what you currently have.

Step 4: Craft Your Motive

Why are YOU the one to teach this?

What's your backstory?

What drives you?

Step 5: Develop Supporting Elements

Create 2-3 powerful analogies

Identify key stories that illustrate your points

Practice the Russian nesting doll structure for teaching

Step 6: Consider Inception

Is there someone you could position as the star?

Could you do joint ventures where you're the presenter and someone else is the expert?

Would this increase credibility and conversions?

Step 7: Engineer Your Positioning

Design your ideal position

Build content that supports this position

Stay congruent in delivery and tone

Own your edge

Step 8: Master the Setup

Spend MORE time on how you present points than on the points themselves

Think like a comedian: setup is everything

Position the point before revealing the point

CONCLUSION: THE POSITIONING PARADIGM

Positioning isn't about manipulation. It's about clarity.

It's helping your audience understand:

WHY you're uniquely qualified

WHY this opportunity matters NOW

WHY your approach is different

WHY they should choose you

When done right, positioning makes the sale feel inevitable because you've established yourself as the

ONLY logical choice.

The ultimate goal: Move your audience from "I don't need this" → "I need this from someone" → "I

need this from YOU specifically."

That's the power of world-class positioning.

JASON FLADLIEN'S PARADIGM 

MASTERCLASS: Complete Analysis & 

Applications 

CORE PHILOSOPHY LESSONS 

1\. The Essence of Paradigm 

Definition: The conclusion you want your audience to draw that makes purchasing your product 

the ONLY logical choice. 

Key Insight: A paradigm creates a mental state where NOT buying would make someone an 

idiot. It's about engineering a consciousness shift that cannot be reversed. 

Memorable Quote: "A wallet once expanded can never go back to its original size" - Marshall 

Silvers 

Jason's Version: "A consciousness expanded can never go back to the old way of doing 

things" 

Application Philosophy: 

●​ Don't just educate → Transform thinking 

●​ Don't just teach → Expand consciousness 

●​ Don't just inform → Create irreversibility 

2\. The "Aha" Moment Engineering 

Goal: Create a "THIS CHANGES EVERYTHING" moment 

The Framework: 

●​ If you expand consciousness to where something is obvious 

●​ Even if they try to resist it 

●​ They won't be able to sleep until they buy 

●​ The old limiting way becomes impossible to return to 

Critical Distinction: 

●​ Understanding alone ≠ Purchase 

●​ You can have best content and still not sell (Marlon Sanders story) 

●​ People took pictures with him holding someone else's course 

3\. Two Types of Paradigms 

A. STATED PARADIGMS (Easier, Safer) 

●​ Literally tell them what you hope to accomplish 

●​ "Here's the paradigm we're shooting for" 

●​ "Here's the specific result, let's see if we can get there together" 

●​ Lower risk, more straightforward 

●​ Best for beginners 

B. IMPLIED PARADIGMS (More Effective, Riskier) 

●​ Covert, not overt 

●​ Things are better understated 

●​ Much harder to execute 

●​ More ways it can go wrong 

●​ Jason's preferred method 

THE 7 MAJOR PARADIGM STRATEGIES 

PARADIGM \#1: SHOW THE MANUAL, SELL THE AUTOMATIC 

Jason's Favorite Paradigm 

Concept: Spend exhaustive time showing them how to do it manually → Reveal you'll do it 

automatically for them 

Why It Works: 

1.​ Appreciation Through Understanding 

○​ The more they see the manual work involved 

○​ The more they appreciate your automatic solution 

○​ They understand the true value after seeing the complexity 

2.​ The Brush vs. Spray Paint Analogy 

○​ Brushing a house: 5x slower, 1/5th as good 

○​ Spray painting: 5x faster, 5 coats at once 

○​ "Give them the brush, then sell them the sprayer" 

Classic Examples from Transcript: 

Example 1: Instant Automation 

●​ Showed: Manual math problem (14.241 x 22.06 ÷ 5 + 61 – 37 X 3.267 + 14.178 ÷ 2.734) 

●​ Message: "There IS a way to do it manually... just like solving this math problem" 

●​ Then revealed: "OR... you can use a tool that takes less than 1 minute" 

●​ Result: "NOT only can you protect yourself... but you can actually profit" 

Example 2: Website Setup 

●​ Showed all manual steps: 

○​ Install your favorite theme 

○​ CONFIGURE that theme 

○​ Set up privacy 

○​ Set up permalinks 

○​ Set up your sidebar 

○​ Install your 3 favorite plugins 

○​ Delete hello world post and comment 

○​ Fix the footer 

○​ Set up your categories 

○​ Set up your pages 

●​ Time Investment: "At least an hour to 'build' your foundation" 

●​ Then asked: "What if you could automate all that... in a couple of clicks?" 

Example 3: Keywords on Amazon 

●​ Showed: 40 different bluetooth speaker keywords 

●​ Question: "One sale per day per keyword?" 

●​ Reality Check: "Would you rather have a product that makes 40 sales a day off 1 search 

phrase OR a product that makes at least 1 sale from 40 different search phrases?" 

●​ Paradigm Shift: Why people ignore "less obvious keywords" 

○​ Reason 1: Ignorance - Don't know about long-tail opportunity 

○​ Reason 2: It's freaking hard - Would need to create 40 funnels 

The Software Solution Pattern: 

●​ Show them how to do it without software (painful, tedious) 

●​ Software becomes the magic solution 

●​ Does it faster, easier, better 

PARADIGM \#2: SPEED AS A PARADIGM 

Concept: "Is one of the easiest ways to sell - if you can cut the time down significantly people 

will pay a lot more and are easier to sell" 

Why Speed Works: 

●​ People value their time more than money 

●​ Instant results \> delayed results 

●​ Speed = modern luxury 

Application Formula: 

1.​ Show the time investment required manually 

2.​ Demonstrate the speed of your solution 

3.​ Emphasize: "The Formula: 1 problem, 1 solution, 1 sitting product creation\!" 

Example from Webinar: 

●​ Manual: 2 hours to create quality info product 

●​ Automatic: Minutes with their system 

●​ Result: High perceived value 

PARADIGM \#3: BIG FAT AGGRESSIVE PROMISE 

When to Use: "When in doubt, works well provided you have ample proof\!" 

Requirements: 

●​ Must have PROOF backing it up 

●​ Can't be empty hype 

●​ Works when you can demonstrate results 

Examples: 

1.​ "It's now undeniable - We have exclusive access to the best ranking solution in 

the world" 

2.​ Amazon Million Dollar Business: 

○​ "12 Month Amazon Million Dollar Business" 

○​ Broke it into phases: 

■​ Phase 1: $0 to $10,000/mo 

■​ Phase 2: $25,000 to $50,000/mo 

■​ Phase 3: $100,000+/mo 

3.​ "If this is FREE, the paid stuff must be INSANE\!" 

○​ Over-deliver on free content 

○​ Makes paid offer irresistible by comparison 

The Raul Story (ASM3): 

●​ Showed students doing $20K-$100K+ months 

●​ Then introduced Raul: doing $3,500/month NET PROFIT on his FIRST product 

●​ Message: "Has the most CERTAIN approach to success I've ever seen\!" 

●​ Why it worked: Relatable, achievable, proven 

PARADIGM \#4: LAUNCH IN BETA 

Jason's Claim: "Beta is the greatest thing ever" 

The Beta Strategy Components: 

A. LIMITED AVAILABILITY 

●​ Creates scarcity naturally 

●​ "Only 20 17 Spots. $3,000/month. Minimum 3 month commitment." 

●​ Makes people compete for spots 

B. QUALIFICATION PROCESS Three critical questions: 

1.​ "How much will you invest initially, beyond the $10,000 for the program?" 

2.​ "What's your risk tolerance from 1-10 (1 being most risk averse, 10 being extremely 

tolerant)?" 

3.​ "Would your life continue relatively the same if you somehow lost everything you 

invested? Yes/No?" 

Why These Questions Matter: 

●​ Filters out people who will freak out during test 

●​ Selects people with highest risk tolerance 

●​ Ensures they won't "breathe down your neck" 

●​ Makes it easier to run the actual test 

C. THE BETA POSITIONING From crypto launch example: 

●​ "I imagine you're interested in this system" 

●​ "My mentor has taken on about a dozen clients" 

●​ "Every single one has made big gains" 

●​ "And all have done better than me\!" 

●​ "It's quite hands-on at first to get you set up right and right now" 

●​ "We're looking for a few people to test-pilot this approach" 

Critical Beta Language: 

●​ "Everyone is welcome to learn more" 

●​ "But what we want is a few players with money" 

●​ "Who want a hands-on approach to getting their system set up for them" 

●​ "(like I got)" 

D. RISK REVERSAL IN BETA "Third, I do not expect you to lose money if you just stick to the 

system. I actually expect a 100% success rate for everyone who joins. But the only certainty in 

life is uncertainty. As such, the first round of clients we want to take in, I'd like - ideally - to be the 

ones that can lose everything they invested and not suffer any real life-altering consequences." 

Results from Beta: 

●​ 53 applicants for 12 spots (took 18) 

●​ Took ones with highest risk tolerance 

●​ All were successful right away 

●​ Never had to do second round 

●​ Went straight to full launch 

The Beta-to-Launch Sequence: 

1.​ Launch beta (limited, exclusive, test) 

2.​ Say "we think it can support 100" 

3.​ Sell those spots 

4.​ Say "Amazon can support more, but we'll be cautious" 

5.​ Sell few hundred more 

6.​ Eventually: "We were SO effective we got Amazon to change how they operate" 

PARADIGM \#5: COMBO PARADIGMS 

Launchpad Example (Combined Multiple Paradigms): 

Paradigm 1: Show manual, sell automatic 

●​ Taught everything about Amazon ranking 

●​ Then revealed: "Or we can do it for you" 

Paradigm 2: Big fat aggressive promise 

●​ "12 months to million dollar business" 

●​ Backed with proof and case studies 

Paradigm 3: Launch in beta 

●​ "Only taking 100 people first" 

●​ "Don't want to disrupt Amazon ecosystem" 

●​ "Need to test cautiously" 

Why Combos Work: 

●​ Addresses multiple objections 

●​ Hits different psychological triggers 

●​ Creates compounding effect 

●​ More angles of attack 

PARADIGM \#6: MEET THE MARKET WHERE IT'S AT 

Evolution of ASM (Amazing Selling Machine) Paradigms: 

ASM 3 (First Launch): 

●​ Market Awareness: Stage 1 (Don't know if it's real) 

●​ Paradigm: "Why do I do this? Because I believe in the heart of hearts this is the best 

opportunity for you to succeed. Period." 

●​ Focus: Possibility + Relatability 

●​ Hero: Raul (doing $3,500/month net profit on first product) 

●​ Message: "You'll see people making $20K-$100K... but here's who you should emulate" 

ASM 4 (6 Months Later): 

●​ Market Awareness: Stage 3 (Know it works, need specifics) 

●​ Paradigm: "Amazon is BRIBING us to help them" 

●​ Focus: Positioning as beneficiary 

●​ Message: 

○​ "Amazon only has 200M people who can 1-click purchase" 

○​ "They're bribing us to help them" 

○​ "We provide physical products, they do most selling" 

○​ "We keep most of the money" 

○​ "Do you see how we're beneficiaries?" 

●​ Promise: "The Magic: If you know the right way to private label and position your 

products... You will join the legions of customers who I have helped make millions of 

dollars in the last 18 months" 

●​ Plan: "12 months to a million dollar Amazon business" 

ASM 5 (12 Months Later): 

●​ Market Awareness: Stage 4 (Sophisticated, skeptical) 

●​ Paradigm: Address resistance head-on 

●​ Focus: "It doesn't matter..." 

●​ New Positioning: 

○​ Spent much more time on objections 

○​ Asked: "Maybe it's possible with the right approach?" 

○​ "Maybe if I do just a BIT of what they do, I can bring in a BIT of what they bring 

in?" 

○​ "Maybe it's worth me being at least OPEN MINDED to the opportunity" 

○​ Question: "If I were to show you a process that ONLY myself and my customers 

specifically follow on Amazon... And you believe it MAY be possible for you to do 

it... Then WILL you try it?" 

Key Lesson: Same product, completely different pitch decks every 6 months as market 

sophistication evolved 

PARADIGM \#7: THE PARADIGM OF OPPORTUNITY 

"Get In On The Ground Floor" 

Local Business Mobile Problem Example: 

●​ The Problem: "Here's what most local business websites look like on a mobile device" 

(broken, unusable) 

●​ Your Market: "Is used to spending thousands of dollars for similar services (high price 

point)" 

●​ The Opportunity: 

○​ "You can easily demonstrate their problem to them (just show them your phone\!)" 

○​ "They desperately need your help more so than probably ANY other local 

marketing service" 

○​ "No one is helping them right now..." 

Why "Ground Floor" Works: 

●​ Taps into FOMO 

●​ Positions them as early adopters 

●​ Suggests competitive advantage 

●​ Limited window of opportunity 

ADVANCED PARADIGM CONCEPTS 

1\. THE PRIORITIZATION PRINCIPLE 

Jason's Insight: "Prioritization is not something to undermine as a tactic" 

What Makes You Prioritize Something? 

●​ Information - But not just any information 

●​ The RIGHT information 

The Bomb Example: 

●​ Bombs go off in Middle East 

●​ We see war, chaos 

●​ Has that inspired YOU to do anything? 

●​ Sub-1% maybe bought anti-radiation medicine, became prepper, bought bunker 

●​ 99%+ did NOTHING with that information 

The Dermatologist Example: 

●​ Doctor says: "I noticed you got a little something here, let me take a look" 

●​ You're likely to take action on THAT information 

●​ Why? It's personal, immediate, and consequences are clear 

Application: The right information makes people prioritize buying. Wrong information gets 

ignored. 

2\. TEACHING PHILOSOPHY 

Jason's Core Belief: "Learning occurs when behavior changes" 

Anti-Pattern: 

●​ "You know 15 ways to do something and still don't do it = You haven't learned" 

●​ "Learn 25 more ways, now 40 total, still don't do it = You STILL haven't learned" 

Jason's Goal: 

●​ "I want you to learn NOTHING and get better" 

●​ "If I have to teach you something, I'm gonna teach you the LEAST amount of stuff I can 

teach you to get the result" 

This Is Paradigm - Minimum effective dose teaching that creates maximum behavioral change 

3\. THE CONSCIOUSNESS EXPANSION MODEL 

Three-Layer Framework: 

Layer 1: Current State 

●​ Limited way of thinking 

●​ Old paradigm 

●​ Comfortable but constraining 

Layer 2: The Expansion (The Paradigm) 

●​ New way of seeing the problem 

●​ Irreversible insight 

●​ "Aha" moment 

Layer 3: New Reality 

●​ Can't go back to old way 

●​ Must take action (buy) to step into new them 

●​ Old approach now seems foolish 

Quote: "The consciousness that caused the problem can't solve it" (attributed to Einstein) 

4\. THE STATED VS IMPLIED MASTERY 

Why Jason Prefers Implied: 

Stated Paradigm Example: 

●​ "On this webinar, I believe I'm uniquely qualified to give you the best copywriting formula 

on the planet" 

●​ "Perhaps better than any other you've learned anywhere else" 

●​ "Best of all – I can do this in under an hour" 

●​ Then offer at end 

Implied Paradigm Example: 

●​ Teach the content 

●​ Never explicitly state what you're doing 

●​ Let them draw conclusions 

●​ More elegant, more powerful 

●​ But much riskier 

The Trade-off: 

●​ Stated = Safer, still very effective 

●​ Implied = More powerful, but can fail spectacularly 

PARADIGM CASE STUDIES FROM TRANSCRIPT 

CASE STUDY 1: WP Twin (Show Manual, Sell Automatic) 

Background: 

●​ October 2010 

●​ First collaboration between Jason and Wilson Matos 

●​ Product: WordPress automation tool 

The Origin Story: 

●​ Jason in mastermind event 

●​ Saw product that was "closed during non-business hours" 

●​ They were in Sweden - time zones never matched 

●​ Jason: "I want to create this just to put these idiots out of business" 

●​ Wilson approached: "If I built that, would you do a deal?" 

●​ Jason: "Sure" (not expecting anything) 

The Manual Teaching: Spent 45-minute webinar showing: 

●​ Every tedious step to set up WordPress manually 

●​ Security configurations 

●​ Theme installations 

●​ Plugin setups 

●​ All the boring technical work 

The Automatic Reveal: 

●​ "What if you could automate all that in a couple clicks?" 

●​ Software did everything they just taught 

●​ Instant setup vs hours of manual work 

Results: 

●​ Massively successful launch 

●​ People appreciated the solution MORE after seeing manual process 

●​ Created understanding of true value 

CASE STUDY 2: Amazon Selling Machine Evolution 

The Market Sophistication Journey: 

Stage 1: ASM 3 

●​ Market belief: "Is this even real?" 

●​ Approach: Prove it's possible with relatable success story 

●​ Hero: Raul ($3,500/month first product) 

●​ Message: "This is the best opportunity for YOU" 

Stage 2: ASM 4 

●​ Market belief: "It works, but is it for me?" 

●​ Approach: Show the mechanism, position as beneficiary 

●​ Focus: "Amazon needs YOU" 

●​ Message: "If you know the right way... you'll join customers making millions" 

Stage 3: ASM 5 

●​ Market belief: "Saturated, competitive, risky" 

●​ Approach: Address objections, lower barriers 

●​ Focus: "Maybe just a little bit?" 

●​ Message: "It doesn't matter if \[objection\], will you try it?" 

Key Insight: Same product, completely different pitch every 6 months as market evolved 

CASE STUDY 3: Crypto Beta Launch 

Setup: 

●​ High-ticket offer: $10,000+ investment 

●​ Risky niche (crypto trading) 

●​ Needed proof of concept before full launch 

The Beta Approach: 

Step 1: Create Exclusivity 

●​ "Looking for few people to test-pilot" 

●​ "Hands-on approach" 

●​ Limited to 12 spots (ended up taking 18) 

Step 2: Qualify Ruthlessly Three questions that filtered for: 

●​ Capital availability 

●​ Risk tolerance (1-10 scale) 

●​ Financial stability to lose everything 

Step 3: Set Expectations 

●​ "I expect 100% success rate" 

●​ "But first round clients should be ones who can lose everything without life-altering 

consequences" 

●​ Positioned as protection for THEM, not you 

Step 4: Create Proof System Made clients agree to: 

●​ Not invest more than $25,000 initially (keeps it relatable) 

●​ Share URL access to their portfolio (for transparency) 

●​ You can show their results anytime to anyone 

Results: 

●​ 53 applicants for 12 spots 

●​ Took 18 with highest risk tolerance 

●​ All complied and succeeded immediately 

●​ Had perfect proof for main launch 

●​ Never needed to do second beta round 

The Brilliant Setup: Jason said: "I set it up so good, I didn't even have to do the webinar" 

●​ Had real results to show 

●​ Had shareable proof (portfolio URLs) 

●​ Had testimonials from relatable initial investments 

●​ Could show "people like you" succeeding 

CASE STUDY 4: Launchpad (Combo Paradigm) 

Combined Three Paradigms: 

Paradigm 1: Show Manual, Sell Automatic 

●​ Taught complete Amazon ranking strategies 

●​ Every technique, tactic, step-by-step 

●​ Then revealed: "Or we can do it for you automatically" 

Paradigm 2: Big Promise 

●​ "12 months to million dollar Amazon business" 

●​ Broke it into achievable phases 

●​ Showed proof at every stage 

Paradigm 3: Beta Launch 

●​ "Only taking 100 people" 

●​ "Don't want to disrupt Amazon ecosystem" 

●​ Artificial scarcity that felt protective 

The Escalation:

●​ "Amazon can support 100, not a big deal" 

●​ "We think it can support more, but we'll be cautious" 

●​ "Only taking few hundred more" 

●​ Those sold out 

The Prophecy Fulfillment: 

●​ "Sometimes the prophecy comes true" 

●​ "We were SO effective..." 

●​ "We got the biggest company on the internet to change how they operate" 

●​ "We so thoroughly understood that algorithm" 

Result: 

●​ Multiple successful launches 

●​ Each building on previous 

●​ Created legendary status 

●​ Actually DID impact Amazon's algorithm 

TACTICAL PARADIGM CONSTRUCTION GUIDE 

THE PARADIGM CREATION PROCESS 

Step 1: Identify The Purchase-Necessary Mindset 

Ask yourself: 

●​ "If this person had THIS mindset, they would 100% buy this product" 

●​ "What mindset makes this product so obvious to buy that they'd be an idiot NOT to 

purchase it?" 

●​ "What belief, if installed, makes NOT buying painful?" 

Example Mindsets: 

●​ Software: "Manual work is for suckers when automation exists" 

●​ Coaching: "I'm leaving money on the table every day without this" 

●​ Info Product: "This knowledge gap is costing me \[specific amount\]" 

●​ Agency: "I'm in the best position I've ever been to capitalize on this" 

Step 2: Determine Stated vs Implied 

Use STATED if: 

●​ You're new to this 

●​ High-risk offer (expensive, controversial) 

●​ Complex concept that needs explicit framing 

●​ Market is unsophisticated 

●​ You want to play it safe 

Use IMPLIED if: 

●​ You're experienced with paradigms 

●​ You can afford to test and potentially fail 

●​ The conclusion is obvious once explained 

●​ Market is sophisticated 

●​ You want maximum elegance 

Decision Framework: 

Risk Tolerance: Low → STATED 

Risk Tolerance: High → IMPLIED 

Market Sophistication: Low → STATED   

Market Sophistication: High → IMPLIED (or very sophisticated STATED) 

Your Experience: Beginner → STATED 

Your Experience: Advanced → IMPLIED 

Step 3: Choose Your Primary Paradigm Type 

The 7 Paradigm Strategies: 

Paradigm Type When To Use Complexit Risk Payoff 

y 

Show Manual, Sell You have automation/service Medium Low High 

Automatic 

Speed Your solution is faster Low Low Medium 

Big Promise You have strong proof Medium Mediu High 

m 

Beta Launch New/unproven offer High High Very High 

Ground Floor New market opportunity Medium Mediu Medium 

m 

Combo Sophisticated market High Mediu Very High 

m 

Meet Market Where It's At Existing market Medium Low High 

Step 4: Build Supporting Elements 

Every Paradigm Needs: 

A. THE BRIDGE 

●​ Current State: Where they are now 

●​ Paradigm: The "aha" moment 

●​ New State: Where they'll be after 

B. THE PROOF 

●​ Why this works 

●​ Who it's worked for 

●​ Why it'll work for them specifically 

C. THE CONTRAST 

●​ Old way (painful, slow, expensive, complex) 

●​ New way (easy, fast, affordable, simple) 

●​ What bridge connects them (your offer) 

D. THE URGENCY 

●​ Why now matters 

●​ What they lose by waiting 

●​ Why delay is dangerous 

Step 5: Test Your Paradigm 

The Three Questions: 

1.​ "After hearing this, can they go back to their old way?" 

○​ If YES → Paradigm isn't strong enough 

○​ If NO → Paradigm works 

2.​ "Will this keep them up at night until they buy?" 

○​ If YES → Strong paradigm 

○​ If NO → Need more urgency/contrast 

3.​ "Does this make understanding AND purchase likely?" 

○​ Understanding alone ≠ Sale 

○​ Must create both insight AND desire 

PARADIGM PITFALLS TO AVOID 

1\. THE MARLON SANDERS TRAP 

●​ Had the BEST content at events 

●​ Everyone said "greatest stuff I heard" 

●​ People took pictures with him 

●​ Then bought someone else's course 

Lesson: Best content ≠ Best sales. Need paradigm that creates desire for ownership, not just 

understanding. 

2\. THE "TOO MUCH TEACHING" TRAP 

Jason's Warning: "Content is important, but it's not as important as most people think" 

The Problem: 

●​ You teach 15 ways to do something 

●​ They still don't do it 

●​ You teach 25 more ways (40 total) 

●​ They STILL don't do it 

●​ They haven't "learned" (no behavior change) 

The Solution: 

●​ Teach LEAST amount possible 

●​ Focus on consciousness expansion, not information dump 

●​ Behavior change \> Knowledge accumulation 

3\. THE "STATED ONLY" TRAP 

Problem: Always explicitly stating paradigm 

Example of Over-Stating: "Today I'm going to show you the paradigm that will make you 

realize you need my product and then at the end I'll sell you my product." 

Better: Let them discover it through teaching, examples, stories 

4\. THE "NO PROOF" TRAP 

Big promises without proof = Dead paradigm 

Requirements for Big Promise Paradigm: 

●​ Case studies 

●​ Testimonials 

●​ Data/numbers 

●​ Screenshots 

●​ Before/afters 

●​ Third-party validation 

Jason's Standard: "Works well provided you have AMPLE proof" 

5\. THE "STATIC PARADIGM" TRAP 

Problem: Using same paradigm as market evolves 

Jason's ASM Example: 

●​ ASM 3: Prove it's possible 

●​ ASM 4: Show the mechanism 

●​ ASM 5: Address objections 

●​ Same product, totally different paradigms every 6 months 

Lesson: Paradigm must evolve with market sophistication 

6\. THE "TECHNICAL OVER SOUL" TRAP 

Jason's Warning: "I've noticed when I train clients, sometimes they get in the brain and out of 

the heart. They become overly focused on technique and there's no soul in the webinar. You 

gotta be careful of that." 

Must Balance: 

●​ Technique AND soul 

●​ Structure AND authenticity 

●​ Framework AND feeling 

●​ Script AND spontaneity 

Quote: "It's gotta feel organic even if it's not" 

ADVANCED PARADIGM TACTICS 

1\. THE COMBINATION PARADIGM FORMULA 

How to Stack Multiple Paradigms: 

Layer 1: FOUNDATION (Primary Paradigm) Choose your strongest paradigm as base: 

●​ Show Manual, Sell Automatic (most versatile) 

●​ OR Big Promise (if you have proof) 

●​ OR Beta (if new offer) 

Layer 2: AMPLIFIER (Secondary Paradigm)​

 Add complementary paradigm: 

●​ Speed (makes primary more attractive) 

●​ Ground Floor (adds urgency) 

●​ Meet Market Where At (adds sophistication) 

Layer 3: PROOF SYSTEM (Supporting Elements) 

●​ Case studies that reinforce paradigms 

●​ Testimonials that echo the "aha" 

●​ Data that validates the shift 

Example Combination: 

PRIMARY: Show Manual, Sell Automatic 

  ↓ 

SECONDARY: Big Promise   

  ↓ 

TERTIARY: Beta Launch 

  ↓ 

RESULT: Launchpad offer 

2\. THE MARKET SOPHISTICATION LADDER 

Stage 1: Unaware → Use "Possibility" Paradigm 

●​ Focus: "This exists and it works" 

●​ Hero: Relatable beginner success 

●​ Proof: Possibility proof (it CAN be done) 

Stage 2: Problem Aware → Use "Mechanism" Paradigm 

●​ Focus: "Here's HOW it works" 

●​ Hero: Process-driven success 

●​ Proof: Mechanism proof (the system works) 

Stage 3: Solution Aware → Use "Why You" Paradigm 

●​ Focus: "Here's why THIS solution for YOU" 

●​ Hero: Person like them succeeding 

●​ Proof: Specific proof (works for people like you) 

Stage 4: Product Aware → Use "Objection Destroying" Paradigm 

●​ Focus: "Here's why your concerns don't apply" 

●​ Hero: Skeptic who became believer 

●​ Proof: Objection-specific proof 

Stage 5: Most Aware → Use "Next Level" Paradigm 

●​ Focus: "Here's the advanced version" 

●​ Hero: Previous customer upgrading 

●​ Proof: Evolution proof (what's next) 

3\. THE BETA LAUNCH FORMULA 

Jason's Beta Blueprint: 

Component 1: THE POSITIONING 

Language Pattern: 

"I \[don't think/am not sure\] that \[statement protecting them\] 

So I \[am being cautious\] by \[limiting availability\] 

Therefore I'm \[only taking X people\]" 

Example: "I don't think that if we put this in everybody's hands it will affect things. I think we're a 

drop in the ocean of Amazon. So I think if I sold this to every single one of you, it wouldn't create 

competition, it wouldn't break stuff, it wouldn't ruin things. I think we're safe but I'm not sure. So I 

don't want to take on too many of you. I only want to take on a few of you so we can cautiously 

see how much this disrupts the ecosystem." 

Component 2: THE QUALIFICATION 

Three Questions Framework: 

1.​ Investment Capacity: "How much will you invest initially, beyond the $\[price\] for the 

program?" 

2.​ Risk Tolerance: "What's your risk tolerance from 1-10 (1 being most risk averse, 10 

being extremely tolerant of taking risk)?" 

3.​ Financial Safety: "Would your life continue relatively the same if you somehow lost 

everything you invested? Yes/No?" 

Why These Work: 

●​ Filters for serious buyers 

●​ Selects for pain tolerance 

●​ Ensures they won't panic 

●​ Creates self-selection 

Component 3: THE SETUP FOR PROOF 

Make them agree to: 

1.​ Cap their investment (keeps results relatable for later marketing) 

2.​ Share access (portfolio, dashboard, results tracking) 

3.​ You can showcase (anywhere, anytime, to anyone) 

This is Genius Because: 

●​ You're setting up your future marketing BEFORE you've proven anything 

●​ Results will be relatable (not "I invested $500K") 

●​ You'll have shareable, verifiable proof 

●​ Transparency builds trust 

Component 4: THE EXPECTATION SETTING 

"Third, I do not expect you to lose money if you just stick to the system.  

I actually expect a 100% success rate for everyone who joins.  

But the only certainty in life is uncertainty.  

As such, the first round of clients we want to take in, I'd like - ideally -  

to be the ones that can lose everything they invested and not suffer any  

real life-altering consequences." 

What This Does: 

●​ Shows confidence (expect 100% success) 

●​ Manages risk (but universe is uncertain) 

●​ Protects you legally (can lose everything) 

●​ Filters properly (self-select out if can't afford loss) 

4\. THE "SHOW MANUAL, SELL AUTOMATIC" CONSTRUCTION KIT 

Step 1: Identify What You're Automating 

Questions: 

●​ What process does your product/service complete? 

●​ What would they have to do manually without you? 

●​ What steps are involved? 

●​ How long would each step take? 

●​ What expertise is required? 

Step 2: Break Down The Manual Process 

Create exhaustive list: 

●​ Every single step 

●​ Time for each step 

●​ Complexity of each step 

●​ Expertise required 

●​ Potential mistakes 

●​ Opportunity cost 

Step 3: Teach The Manual Process 

In your content section: 

●​ Walk through each step 

●​ Show the complexity 

●​ Demonstrate the time investment 

●​ Highlight potential pitfalls 

●​ Make it thorough (this builds the contrast) 

Jason's Method: "I spend tedious amounts of time... Think about this - I'm not doing this but if I 

were doing this, think how powerful this would be: spending two days with you, meticulously 

mapping out every trick, technique, tactic and strategy I know on webinars, and then at the end, 

revealing that I'll write all of your webinars for you." 

Step 4: Reveal The Automatic Solution 

The Contrast Moment: 

"You just saw how to do \[task\] manually. 

It takes \[time investment\]. 

It requires \[expertise/tools\]. 

There's \[X\] ways to mess it up. 

OR... 

\[Your solution\] does it in \[fraction of time\]. 

Eliminates \[X\] points of failure. 

Requires \[minimal expertise\]. 

And costs \[fraction of manual cost\]." 

Step 5: Amplify The Value 

The Paint House Example: 

●​ Brushing: 5x slower + 1/5th as good = 25x worse 

●​ Spraying: 5x faster + 5x better = 25x better 

●​ Delta: 625x difference in value 

Formula: 

Manual Way: 

Time: X hours 

Quality: Y/10 

Cost: $Z (if hired) 

Automatic Way: 

Time: X/10 hours   

Quality: 9/10 

Cost: $\[Your Price\] 

Value Delta = (Time Saved × Hourly Value) + (Quality Improvement × Outcome Value) - Your 

Price 

5\. THE BIG PROMISE PARADIGM ENGINEERING 

Requirements Checklist: 

Tier 1: Minimum Viable Proof 

●​ At least 3 case studies 

●​ At least 1 video testimonial 

●​ Actual numbers/results 

●​ Before/after states 

●​ Timeline to results 

Tier 2: Strong Proof 

●​ 10+ case studies 

●​ 5+ video testimonials 

●​ Variety of customer types 

●​ Third-party verification 

●​ Public results (screenshots) 

Tier 3: Undeniable Proof 

●​ 50+ case studies 

●​ 20+ video testimonials 

●​ Live result demonstrations 

●​ Customer appearing on webinar 

●​ Real-time dashboards 

●​ Media coverage/validation 

The Promise Hierarchy: 

Level 1: Possible "You CAN achieve \[result\]" 

●​ Requires: Proof it's been done once 

●​ Example: "I made $10K in 30 days" 

Level 2: Probable "You WILL LIKELY achieve \[result\]" 

●​ Requires: Proof of pattern (multiple people) 

●​ Example: "Most students hit $10K in 90 days" 

Level 3: Certain "I GUARANTEE you'll achieve \[result\]" 

●​ Requires: Overwhelming proof + risk reversal 

●​ Example: "You'll make $10K in 90 days or we'll work with you free until you do" 

Level 4: Inevitable "You'd be an IDIOT not to achieve \[result\]" 

●​ Requires: Mechanical proof (it's systematic) 

●​ Example: "If you follow steps 1-7, you'll make $10K. It's not motivational, it's 

mathematical." 

Jason's Examples: 

Possible Level: "Amazon business is possible" (ASM 3) 

●​ Showed Raul: $3,500/mo on first product 

●​ Positioned as achievable 

Certain Level: "12 months to million dollar Amazon business" (ASM 4) 

●​ Had multiple case studies at every level 

●​ Broke it into phases 

●​ Showed the path 

Inevitable Level: "It's now undeniable - we have exclusive access to the best ranking solution 

in the world" 

●​ Positioned as mechanical advantage 

●​ Not motivational, systematic

JASON FLADLIEN'S PARADIGM 

MASTERCLASS: Complete Analysis & 

Applications 

CORE PHILOSOPHY LESSONS 

1\. The Essence of Paradigm 

Definition: The conclusion you want your audience to draw that makes purchasing your product 

the ONLY logical choice. 

Key Insight: A paradigm creates a mental state where NOT buying would make someone an 

idiot. It's about engineering a consciousness shift that cannot be reversed. 

Memorable Quote: "A wallet once expanded can never go back to its original size" - Marshall 

Silvers 

Jason's Version: "A consciousness expanded can never go back to the old way of doing 

things" 

Application Philosophy: 

●​ Don't just educate → Transform thinking 

●​ Don't just teach → Expand consciousness 

●​ Don't just inform → Create irreversibility 

2\. The "Aha" Moment Engineering 

Goal: Create a "THIS CHANGES EVERYTHING" moment 

The Framework: 

●​ If you expand consciousness to where something is obvious 

●​ Even if they try to resist it 

●​ They won't be able to sleep until they buy 

●​ The old limiting way becomes impossible to return to 

Critical Distinction: 

●​ Understanding alone ≠ Purchase 

●​ You can have best content and still not sell (Marlon Sanders story) 

●​ People took pictures with him holding someone else's course 

3\. Two Types of Paradigms 

A. STATED PARADIGMS (Easier, Safer) 

●​ Literally tell them what you hope to accomplish 

●​ "Here's the paradigm we're shooting for" 

●​ "Here's the specific result, let's see if we can get there together" 

●​ Lower risk, more straightforward 

●​ Best for beginners 

B. IMPLIED PARADIGMS (More Effective, Riskier) 

●​ Covert, not overt 

●​ Things are better understated 

●​ Much harder to execute 

●​ More ways it can go wrong 

●​ Jason's preferred method 

THE 7 MAJOR PARADIGM STRATEGIES 

PARADIGM \#1: SHOW THE MANUAL, SELL THE AUTOMATIC 

Jason's Favorite Paradigm 

Concept: Spend exhaustive time showing them how to do it manually → Reveal you'll do it 

automatically for them 

Why It Works: 

1.​ Appreciation Through Understanding 

○​ The more they see the manual work involved 

○​ The more they appreciate your automatic solution 

○​ They understand the true value after seeing the complexity 

2.​ The Brush vs. Spray Paint Analogy 

○​ Brushing a house: 5x slower, 1/5th as good 

○​ Spray painting: 5x faster, 5 coats at once 

○​ "Give them the brush, then sell them the sprayer" 

Classic Examples from Transcript: 

Example 1: Instant Automation 

●​ Showed: Manual math problem (14.241 x 22.06 ÷ 5 + 61 – 37 X 3.267 + 14.178 ÷ 2.734) 

●​ Message: "There IS a way to do it manually... just like solving this math problem" 

●​ Then revealed: "OR... you can use a tool that takes less than 1 minute" 

●​ Result: "NOT only can you protect yourself... but you can actually profit" 

Example 2: Website Setup 

●​ Showed all manual steps: 

○​ Install your favorite theme 

○​ CONFIGURE that theme 

○​ Set up privacy 

○​ Set up permalinks 

○​ Set up your sidebar 

○​ Install your 3 favorite plugins 

○​ Delete hello world post and comment 

○​ Fix the footer 

○​ Set up your categories 

○​ Set up your pages 

●​ Time Investment: "At least an hour to 'build' your foundation" 

●​ Then asked: "What if you could automate all that... in a couple of clicks?" 

Example 3: Keywords on Amazon 

●​ Showed: 40 different bluetooth speaker keywords 

●​ Question: "One sale per day per keyword?" 

●​ Reality Check: "Would you rather have a product that makes 40 sales a day off 1 search 

phrase OR a product that makes at least 1 sale from 40 different search phrases?" 

●​ Paradigm Shift: Why people ignore "less obvious keywords" 

○​ Reason 1: Ignorance - Don't know about long-tail opportunity 

○​ Reason 2: It's freaking hard - Would need to create 40 funnels 

The Software Solution Pattern: 

●​ Show them how to do it without software (painful, tedious) 

●​ Software becomes the magic solution 

●​ Does it faster, easier, better 

PARADIGM \#2: SPEED AS A PARADIGM 

Concept: "Is one of the easiest ways to sell - if you can cut the time down significantly people 

will pay a lot more and are easier to sell" 

Why Speed Works: 

●​ People value their time more than money 

●​ Instant results \> delayed results 

●​ Speed = modern luxury 

Application Formula: 

1.​ Show the time investment required manually 

2.​ Demonstrate the speed of your solution 

3.​ Emphasize: "The Formula: 1 problem, 1 solution, 1 sitting product creation\!" 

Example from Webinar: 

●​ Manual: 2 hours to create quality info product 

●​ Automatic: Minutes with their system 

●​ Result: High perceived value 

PARADIGM \#3: BIG FAT AGGRESSIVE PROMISE 

When to Use: "When in doubt, works well provided you have ample proof\!" 

Requirements: 

●​ Must have PROOF backing it up 

●​ Can't be empty hype 

●​ Works when you can demonstrate results 

Examples: 

1.​ "It's now undeniable - We have exclusive access to the best ranking solution in 

the world" 

2.​ Amazon Million Dollar Business: 

○​ "12 Month Amazon Million Dollar Business" 

○​ Broke it into phases: 

■​ Phase 1: $0 to $10,000/mo 

■​ Phase 2: $25,000 to $50,000/mo 

■​ Phase 3: $100,000+/mo 

3.​ "If this is FREE, the paid stuff must be INSANE\!" 

○​ Over-deliver on free content 

○​ Makes paid offer irresistible by comparison 

The Raul Story (ASM3): 

●​ Showed students doing $20K-$100K+ months 

●​ Then introduced Raul: doing $3,500/month NET PROFIT on his FIRST product 

●​ Message: "Has the most CERTAIN approach to success I've ever seen\!" 

●​ Why it worked: Relatable, achievable, proven 

PARADIGM \#4: LAUNCH IN BETA 

Jason's Claim: "Beta is the greatest thing ever" 

The Beta Strategy Components: 

A. LIMITED AVAILABILITY 

●​ Creates scarcity naturally 

●​ "Only 20 17 Spots. $3,000/month. Minimum 3 month commitment." 

●​ Makes people compete for spots 

B. QUALIFICATION PROCESS Three critical questions: 

1.​ "How much will you invest initially, beyond the $10,000 for the program?" 

2.​ "What's your risk tolerance from 1-10 (1 being most risk averse, 10 being extremely 

tolerant)?" 

3.​ "Would your life continue relatively the same if you somehow lost everything you 

invested? Yes/No?" 

Why These Questions Matter: 

●​ Filters out people who will freak out during test 

●​ Selects people with highest risk tolerance 

●​ Ensures they won't "breathe down your neck" 

●​ Makes it easier to run the actual test 

C. THE BETA POSITIONING From crypto launch example: 

●​ "I imagine you're interested in this system" 

●​ "My mentor has taken on about a dozen clients" 

●​ "Every single one has made big gains" 

●​ "And all have done better than me\!" 

●​ "It's quite hands-on at first to get you set up right and right now" 

●​ "We're looking for a few people to test-pilot this approach" 

Critical Beta Language: 

●​ "Everyone is welcome to learn more" 

●​ "But what we want is a few players with money" 

●​ "Who want a hands-on approach to getting their system set up for them" 

●​ "(like I got)" 

D. RISK REVERSAL IN BETA "Third, I do not expect you to lose money if you just stick to the 

system. I actually expect a 100% success rate for everyone who joins. But the only certainty in 

life is uncertainty. As such, the first round of clients we want to take in, I'd like - ideally - to be the 

ones that can lose everything they invested and not suffer any real life-altering consequences." 

Results from Beta: 

●​ 53 applicants for 12 spots (took 18) 

●​ Took ones with highest risk tolerance 

●​ All were successful right away 

●​ Never had to do second round 

●​ Went straight to full launch 

The Beta-to-Launch Sequence: 

1.​ Launch beta (limited, exclusive, test) 

2.​ Say "we think it can support 100" 

3.​ Sell those spots 

4.​ Say "Amazon can support more, but we'll be cautious" 

5.​ Sell few hundred more 

6.​ Eventually: "We were SO effective we got Amazon to change how they operate" 

PARADIGM \#5: COMBO PARADIGMS 

Launchpad Example (Combined Multiple Paradigms): 

Paradigm 1: Show manual, sell automatic 

●​ Taught everything about Amazon ranking 

●​ Then revealed: "Or we can do it for you" 

Paradigm 2: Big fat aggressive promise 

●​ "12 months to million dollar business" 

●​ Backed with proof and case studies 

Paradigm 3: Launch in beta 

●​ "Only taking 100 people first" 

●​ "Don't want to disrupt Amazon ecosystem" 

●​ "Need to test cautiously" 

Why Combos Work: 

●​ Addresses multiple objections 

●​ Hits different psychological triggers 

●​ Creates compounding effect 

●​ More angles of attack 

PARADIGM \#6: MEET THE MARKET WHERE IT'S AT 

Evolution of ASM (Amazing Selling Machine) Paradigms: 

ASM 3 (First Launch): 

●​ Market Awareness: Stage 1 (Don't know if it's real) 

●​ Paradigm: "Why do I do this? Because I believe in the heart of hearts this is the best 

opportunity for you to succeed. Period." 

●​ Focus: Possibility + Relatability 

●​ Hero: Raul (doing $3,500/month net profit on first product) 

●​ Message: "You'll see people making $20K-$100K... but here's who you should emulate" 

ASM 4 (6 Months Later): 

●​ Market Awareness: Stage 3 (Know it works, need specifics) 

●​ Paradigm: "Amazon is BRIBING us to help them" 

●​ Focus: Positioning as beneficiary 

●​ Message: 

○​ "Amazon only has 200M people who can 1-click purchase" 

○​ "They're bribing us to help them" 

○​ "We provide physical products, they do most selling" 

○​ "We keep most of the money" 

○​ "Do you see how we're beneficiaries?" 

●​ Promise: "The Magic: If you know the right way to private label and position your 

products... You will join the legions of customers who I have helped make millions of 

dollars in the last 18 months" 

●​ Plan: "12 months to a million dollar Amazon business" 

ASM 5 (12 Months Later): 

●​ Market Awareness: Stage 4 (Sophisticated, skeptical) 

●​ Paradigm: Address resistance head-on 

●​ Focus: "It doesn't matter..." 

●​ New Positioning: 

○​ Spent much more time on objections 

○​ Asked: "Maybe it's possible with the right approach?" 

○​ "Maybe if I do just a BIT of what they do, I can bring in a BIT of what they bring 

in?" 

○​ "Maybe it's worth me being at least OPEN MINDED to the opportunity" 

○​ Question: "If I were to show you a process that ONLY myself and my customers 

specifically follow on Amazon... And you believe it MAY be possible for you to do 

it... Then WILL you try it?" 

Key Lesson: Same product, completely different pitch decks every 6 months as market 

sophistication evolved 

PARADIGM \#7: THE PARADIGM OF OPPORTUNITY 

"Get In On The Ground Floor" 

Local Business Mobile Problem Example: 

●​ The Problem: "Here's what most local business websites look like on a mobile device" 

(broken, unusable) 

●​ Your Market: "Is used to spending thousands of dollars for similar services (high price 

point)" 

●​ The Opportunity: 

○​ "You can easily demonstrate their problem to them (just show them your phone\!)" 

○​ "They desperately need your help more so than probably ANY other local 

marketing service" 

○​ "No one is helping them right now..." 

Why "Ground Floor" Works: 

●​ Taps into FOMO 

●​ Positions them as early adopters 

●​ Suggests competitive advantage 

●​ Limited window of opportunity 

ADVANCED PARADIGM CONCEPTS 

1\. THE PRIORITIZATION PRINCIPLE 

Jason's Insight: "Prioritization is not something to undermine as a tactic" 

What Makes You Prioritize Something? 

●​ Information - But not just any information 

●​ The RIGHT information 

The Bomb Example: 

●​ Bombs go off in Middle East 

●​ We see war, chaos 

●​ Has that inspired YOU to do anything? 

●​ Sub-1% maybe bought anti-radiation medicine, became prepper, bought bunker 

●​ 99%+ did NOTHING with that information 

The Dermatologist Example: 

●​ Doctor says: "I noticed you got a little something here, let me take a look" 

●​ You're likely to take action on THAT information 

●​ Why? It's personal, immediate, and consequences are clear 

Application: The right information makes people prioritize buying. Wrong information gets 

ignored. 

2\. TEACHING PHILOSOPHY 

Jason's Core Belief: "Learning occurs when behavior changes" 

Anti-Pattern: 

●​ "You know 15 ways to do something and still don't do it = You haven't learned" 

●​ "Learn 25 more ways, now 40 total, still don't do it = You STILL haven't learned" 

Jason's Goal: 

●​ "I want you to learn NOTHING and get better" 

●​ "If I have to teach you something, I'm gonna teach you the LEAST amount of stuff I can 

teach you to get the result" 

This Is Paradigm - Minimum effective dose teaching that creates maximum behavioral change 

3\. THE CONSCIOUSNESS EXPANSION MODEL 

Three-Layer Framework: 

Layer 1: Current State 

●​ Limited way of thinking 

●​ Old paradigm 

●​ Comfortable but constraining 

Layer 2: The Expansion (The Paradigm) 

●​ New way of seeing the problem 

●​ Irreversible insight 

●​ "Aha" moment 

Layer 3: New Reality 

●​ Can't go back to old way 

●​ Must take action (buy) to step into new them 

●​ Old approach now seems foolish 

Quote: "The consciousness that caused the problem can't solve it" (attributed to Einstein) 

4\. THE STATED VS IMPLIED MASTERY 

Why Jason Prefers Implied: 

Stated Paradigm Example: 

●​ "On this webinar, I believe I'm uniquely qualified to give you the best copywriting formula 

on the planet" 

●​ "Perhaps better than any other you've learned anywhere else" 

●​ "Best of all – I can do this in under an hour" 

●​ Then offer at end 

Implied Paradigm Example: 

●​ Teach the content 

●​ Never explicitly state what you're doing 

●​ Let them draw conclusions 

●​ More elegant, more powerful 

●​ But much riskier 

The Trade-off: 

●​ Stated = Safer, still very effective 

●​ Implied = More powerful, but can fail spectacularly 

PARADIGM CASE STUDIES FROM TRANSCRIPT 

CASE STUDY 1: WP Twin (Show Manual, Sell Automatic) 

Background: 

●​ October 2010 

●​ First collaboration between Jason and Wilson Matos 

●​ Product: WordPress automation tool 

The Origin Story: 

●​ Jason in mastermind event 

●​ Saw product that was "closed during non-business hours" 

●​ They were in Sweden - time zones never matched 

●​ Jason: "I want to create this just to put these idiots out of business" 

●​ Wilson approached: "If I built that, would you do a deal?" 

●​ Jason: "Sure" (not expecting anything) 

The Manual Teaching: Spent 45-minute webinar showing: 

●​ Every tedious step to set up WordPress manually 

●​ Security configurations 

●​ Theme installations 

●​ Plugin setups 

●​ All the boring technical work 

The Automatic Reveal: 

●​ "What if you could automate all that in a couple clicks?" 

●​ Software did everything they just taught 

●​ Instant setup vs hours of manual work 

Results: 

●​ Massively successful launch 

●​ People appreciated the solution MORE after seeing manual process 

●​ Created understanding of true value 

CASE STUDY 2: Amazon Selling Machine Evolution 

The Market Sophistication Journey: 

Stage 1: ASM 3 

●​ Market belief: "Is this even real?" 

●​ Approach: Prove it's possible with relatable success story 

●​ Hero: Raul ($3,500/month first product) 

●​ Message: "This is the best opportunity for YOU" 

Stage 2: ASM 4 

●​ Market belief: "It works, but is it for me?" 

●​ Approach: Show the mechanism, position as beneficiary 

●​ Focus: "Amazon needs YOU" 

●​ Message: "If you know the right way... you'll join customers making millions" 

Stage 3: ASM 5 

●​ Market belief: "Saturated, competitive, risky" 

●​ Approach: Address objections, lower barriers 

●​ Focus: "Maybe just a little bit?" 

●​ Message: "It doesn't matter if \[objection\], will you try it?" 

Key Insight: Same product, completely different pitch every 6 months as market evolved 

CASE STUDY 3: Crypto Beta Launch 

Setup: 

●​ High-ticket offer: $10,000+ investment 

●​ Risky niche (crypto trading) 

●​ Needed proof of concept before full launch 

The Beta Approach: 

Step 1: Create Exclusivity 

●​ "Looking for few people to test-pilot" 

●​ "Hands-on approach" 

●​ Limited to 12 spots (ended up taking 18) 

Step 2: Qualify Ruthlessly Three questions that filtered for: 

●​ Capital availability 

●​ Risk tolerance (1-10 scale) 

●​ Financial stability to lose everything 

Step 3: Set Expectations 

●​ "I expect 100% success rate" 

●​ "But first round clients should be ones who can lose everything without life-altering 

consequences" 

●​ Positioned as protection for THEM, not you 

Step 4: Create Proof System Made clients agree to: 

●​ Not invest more than $25,000 initially (keeps it relatable) 

●​ Share URL access to their portfolio (for transparency) 

●​ You can show their results anytime to anyone 

Results: 

●​ 53 applicants for 12 spots 

●​ Took 18 with highest risk tolerance 

●​ All complied and succeeded immediately 

●​ Had perfect proof for main launch 

●​ Never needed to do second beta round 

The Brilliant Setup: Jason said: "I set it up so good, I didn't even have to do the webinar" 

●​ Had real results to show 

●​ Had shareable proof (portfolio URLs) 

●​ Had testimonials from relatable initial investments 

●​ Could show "people like you" succeeding

●​ "We were SO effective..."
