●​ Each building on previous 

●​ Created legendary status 

●​ Actually DID impact Amazon's algorithm 

TACTICAL PARADIGM CONSTRUCTION GUIDE 

THE PARADIGM CREATION PROCESS 

Step 1: Identify The Purchase-Necessary Mindset 

Ask yourself: 

●​ "If this person had THIS mindset, they would 100% buy this product" 

●​ "What mindset makes this product so obvious to buy that they'd be an idiot NOT to 

purchase it?" 

●​ "What belief, if installed, makes NOT buying painful?" 

Example Mindsets: 

●​ Software: "Manual work is for suckers when automation exists" 

●​ Coaching: "I'm leaving money on the table every day without this" 

●​ Info Product: "This knowledge gap is costing me \[specific amount\]" 

●​ Agency: "I'm in the best position I've ever been to capitalize on this" 

Step 2: Determine Stated vs Implied 

Use STATED if: 

●​ You're new to this 

●​ High-risk offer (expensive, controversial) 

●​ Complex concept that needs explicit framing 

●​ Market is unsophisticated 

●​ You want to play it safe 

Use IMPLIED if: 

●​ You're experienced with paradigms 

●​ You can afford to test and potentially fail 

●​ The conclusion is obvious once explained 

●​ Market is sophisticated 

●​ You want maximum elegance 

Decision Framework: 

Risk Tolerance: Low → STATED 

Risk Tolerance: High → IMPLIED 

Market Sophistication: Low → STATED   

Market Sophistication: High → IMPLIED (or very sophisticated STATED) 

Your Experience: Beginner → STATED 

Your Experience: Advanced → IMPLIED 

Step 3: Choose Your Primary Paradigm Type 

The 7 Paradigm Strategies: 

Paradigm Type When To Use Complexit Risk Payoff 

y 

Show Manual, Sell You have automation/service Medium Low High 

Automatic 

Speed Your solution is faster Low Low Medium 

Big Promise You have strong proof Medium Mediu High 

m 

Beta Launch New/unproven offer High High Very High 

Ground Floor New market opportunity Medium Mediu Medium 

m 

Combo Sophisticated market High Mediu Very High 

m 

Meet Market Where It's At Existing market Medium Low High 

Step 4: Build Supporting Elements 

Every Paradigm Needs: 

A. THE BRIDGE 

●​ Current State: Where they are now 

●​ Paradigm: The "aha" moment 

●​ New State: Where they'll be after 

B. THE PROOF 

●​ Why this works 

●​ Who it's worked for 

●​ Why it'll work for them specifically 

C. THE CONTRAST 

●​ Old way (painful, slow, expensive, complex) 

●​ New way (easy, fast, affordable, simple) 

●​ What bridge connects them (your offer) 

D. THE URGENCY 

●​ Why now matters 

●​ What they lose by waiting 

●​ Why delay is dangerous 

Step 5: Test Your Paradigm 

The Three Questions: 

1.​ "After hearing this, can they go back to their old way?" 

○​ If YES → Paradigm isn't strong enough 

○​ If NO → Paradigm works 

2.​ "Will this keep them up at night until they buy?" 

○​ If YES → Strong paradigm 

○​ If NO → Need more urgency/contrast 

3.​ "Does this make understanding AND purchase likely?" 

○​ Understanding alone ≠ Sale 

○​ Must create both insight AND desire 

PARADIGM PITFALLS TO AVOID 

1\. THE MARLON SANDERS TRAP 

●​ Had the BEST content at events 

●​ Everyone said "greatest stuff I heard" 

●​ People took pictures with him 

●​ Then bought someone else's course 

Lesson: Best content ≠ Best sales. Need paradigm that creates desire for ownership, not just 

understanding. 

2\. THE "TOO MUCH TEACHING" TRAP 

Jason's Warning: "Content is important, but it's not as important as most people think" 

The Problem: 

●​ You teach 15 ways to do something 

●​ They still don't do it 

●​ You teach 25 more ways (40 total) 

●​ They STILL don't do it 

●​ They haven't "learned" (no behavior change) 

The Solution: 

●​ Teach LEAST amount possible 

●​ Focus on consciousness expansion, not information dump 

●​ Behavior change \> Knowledge accumulation 

3\. THE "STATED ONLY" TRAP 

Problem: Always explicitly stating paradigm 

Example of Over-Stating: "Today I'm going to show you the paradigm that will make you 

realize you need my product and then at the end I'll sell you my product." 

Better: Let them discover it through teaching, examples, stories 

4\. THE "NO PROOF" TRAP 

Big promises without proof = Dead paradigm 

Requirements for Big Promise Paradigm: 

●​ Case studies 

●​ Testimonials 

●​ Data/numbers 

●​ Screenshots 

●​ Before/afters 

●​ Third-party validation 

Jason's Standard: "Works well provided you have AMPLE proof" 

5\. THE "STATIC PARADIGM" TRAP 

Problem: Using same paradigm as market evolves 

Jason's ASM Example: 

●​ ASM 3: Prove it's possible 

●​ ASM 4: Show the mechanism 

●​ ASM 5: Address objections 

●​ Same product, totally different paradigms every 6 months 

Lesson: Paradigm must evolve with market sophistication 

6\. THE "TECHNICAL OVER SOUL" TRAP 

Jason's Warning: "I've noticed when I train clients, sometimes they get in the brain and out of 

the heart. They become overly focused on technique and there's no soul in the webinar. You 

gotta be careful of that." 

Must Balance: 

●​ Technique AND soul 

●​ Structure AND authenticity 

●​ Framework AND feeling 

●​ Script AND spontaneity 

Quote: "It's gotta feel organic even if it's not" 

ADVANCED PARADIGM TACTICS 

1\. THE COMBINATION PARADIGM FORMULA 

How to Stack Multiple Paradigms: 

Layer 1: FOUNDATION (Primary Paradigm) Choose your strongest paradigm as base: 

●​ Show Manual, Sell Automatic (most versatile) 

●​ OR Big Promise (if you have proof) 

●​ OR Beta (if new offer) 

Layer 2: AMPLIFIER (Secondary Paradigm)​

 Add complementary paradigm: 

●​ Speed (makes primary more attractive) 

●​ Ground Floor (adds urgency) 

●​ Meet Market Where At (adds sophistication) 

Layer 3: PROOF SYSTEM (Supporting Elements) 

●​ Case studies that reinforce paradigms 

●​ Testimonials that echo the "aha" 

●​ Data that validates the shift 

Example Combination: 

PRIMARY: Show Manual, Sell Automatic 

  ↓ 

SECONDARY: Big Promise   

  ↓ 

TERTIARY: Beta Launch 

  ↓ 

RESULT: Launchpad offer 

2\. THE MARKET SOPHISTICATION LADDER 

Stage 1: Unaware → Use "Possibility" Paradigm 

●​ Focus: "This exists and it works" 

●​ Hero: Relatable beginner success 

●​ Proof: Possibility proof (it CAN be done) 

Stage 2: Problem Aware → Use "Mechanism" Paradigm 

●​ Focus: "Here's HOW it works" 

●​ Hero: Process-driven success 

●​ Proof: Mechanism proof (the system works) 

Stage 3: Solution Aware → Use "Why You" Paradigm 

●​ Focus: "Here's why THIS solution for YOU" 

●​ Hero: Person like them succeeding 

●​ Proof: Specific proof (works for people like you) 

Stage 4: Product Aware → Use "Objection Destroying" Paradigm 

●​ Focus: "Here's why your concerns don't apply" 

●​ Hero: Skeptic who became believer 

●​ Proof: Objection-specific proof 

Stage 5: Most Aware → Use "Next Level" Paradigm 

●​ Focus: "Here's the advanced version" 

●​ Hero: Previous customer upgrading 

●​ Proof: Evolution proof (what's next) 

3\. THE BETA LAUNCH FORMULA 

Jason's Beta Blueprint: 

Component 1: THE POSITIONING 

Language Pattern: 

"I \[don't think/am not sure\] that \[statement protecting them\] 

So I \[am being cautious\] by \[limiting availability\] 

Therefore I'm \[only taking X people\]" 

Example: "I don't think that if we put this in everybody's hands it will affect things. I think we're a 

drop in the ocean of Amazon. So I think if I sold this to every single one of you, it wouldn't create 

competition, it wouldn't break stuff, it wouldn't ruin things. I think we're safe but I'm not sure. So I 

don't want to take on too many of you. I only want to take on a few of you so we can cautiously 

see how much this disrupts the ecosystem." 

Component 2: THE QUALIFICATION 

Three Questions Framework: 

1.​ Investment Capacity: "How much will you invest initially, beyond the $\[price\] for the 

program?" 

2.​ Risk Tolerance: "What's your risk tolerance from 1-10 (1 being most risk averse, 10 

being extremely tolerant of taking risk)?" 

3.​ Financial Safety: "Would your life continue relatively the same if you somehow lost 

everything you invested? Yes/No?" 

Why These Work: 

●​ Filters for serious buyers 

●​ Selects for pain tolerance 

●​ Ensures they won't panic 

●​ Creates self-selection 

Component 3: THE SETUP FOR PROOF 

Make them agree to: 

1.​ Cap their investment (keeps results relatable for later marketing) 

2.​ Share access (portfolio, dashboard, results tracking) 

3.​ You can showcase (anywhere, anytime, to anyone) 

This is Genius Because: 

●​ You're setting up your future marketing BEFORE you've proven anything 

●​ Results will be relatable (not "I invested $500K") 

●​ You'll have shareable, verifiable proof 

●​ Transparency builds trust 

Component 4: THE EXPECTATION SETTING 

"Third, I do not expect you to lose money if you just stick to the system.  

I actually expect a 100% success rate for everyone who joins.  

But the only certainty in life is uncertainty.  

As such, the first round of clients we want to take in, I'd like - ideally -  

to be the ones that can lose everything they invested and not suffer any  

real life-altering consequences." 

What This Does: 

●​ Shows confidence (expect 100% success) 

●​ Manages risk (but universe is uncertain) 

●​ Protects you legally (can lose everything) 

●​ Filters properly (self-select out if can't afford loss) 

4\. THE "SHOW MANUAL, SELL AUTOMATIC" CONSTRUCTION KIT 

Step 1: Identify What You're Automating 

Questions: 

●​ What process does your product/service complete? 

●​ What would they have to do manually without you? 

●​ What steps are involved? 

●​ How long would each step take? 

●​ What expertise is required? 

Step 2: Break Down The Manual Process 

Create exhaustive list: 

●​ Every single step 

●​ Time for each step 

●​ Complexity of each step 

●​ Expertise required 

●​ Potential mistakes 

●​ Opportunity cost 

Step 3: Teach The Manual Process 

In your content section: 

●​ Walk through each step 

●​ Show the complexity 

●​ Demonstrate the time investment 

●​ Highlight potential pitfalls 

●​ Make it thorough (this builds the contrast) 

Jason's Method: "I spend tedious amounts of time... Think about this - I'm not doing this but if I 

were doing this, think how powerful this would be: spending two days with you, meticulously 

mapping out every trick, technique, tactic and strategy I know on webinars, and then at the end, 

revealing that I'll write all of your webinars for you." 

Step 4: Reveal The Automatic Solution 

The Contrast Moment: 

"You just saw how to do \[task\] manually. 

It takes \[time investment\]. 

It requires \[expertise/tools\]. 

There's \[X\] ways to mess it up. 

OR... 

\[Your solution\] does it in \[fraction of time\]. 

Eliminates \[X\] points of failure. 

Requires \[minimal expertise\]. 

And costs \[fraction of manual cost\]." 

Step 5: Amplify The Value 

The Paint House Example: 

●​ Brushing: 5x slower + 1/5th as good = 25x worse 

●​ Spraying: 5x faster + 5x better = 25x better 

●​ Delta: 625x difference in value 

Formula: 

Manual Way: 

Time: X hours 

Quality: Y/10 

Cost: $Z (if hired) 

Automatic Way: 

Time: X/10 hours   

Quality: 9/10 

Cost: $\[Your Price\] 

Value Delta = (Time Saved × Hourly Value) + (Quality Improvement × Outcome Value) - Your 

Price 

5\. THE BIG PROMISE PARADIGM ENGINEERING 

Requirements Checklist: 

Tier 1: Minimum Viable Proof 

●​ At least 3 case studies 

●​ At least 1 video testimonial 

●​ Actual numbers/results 

●​ Before/after states 

●​ Timeline to results 

Tier 2: Strong Proof 

●​ 10+ case studies 

●​ 5+ video testimonials 

●​ Variety of customer types 

●​ Third-party verification 

●​ Public results (screenshots) 

Tier 3: Undeniable Proof 

●​ 50+ case studies 

●​ 20+ video testimonials 

●​ Live result demonstrations 

●​ Customer appearing on webinar 

●​ Real-time dashboards 

●​ Media coverage/validation 

The Promise Hierarchy: 

Level 1: Possible "You CAN achieve \[result\]" 

●​ Requires: Proof it's been done once 

●​ Example: "I made $10K in 30 days" 

Level 2: Probable "You WILL LIKELY achieve \[result\]" 

●​ Requires: Proof of pattern (multiple people) 

●​ Example: "Most students hit $10K in 90 days" 

Level 3: Certain "I GUARANTEE you'll achieve \[result\]" 

●​ Requires: Overwhelming proof + risk reversal 

●​ Example: "You'll make $10K in 90 days or we'll work with you free until you do" 

Level 4: Inevitable "You'd be an IDIOT not to achieve \[result\]" 

●​ Requires: Mechanical proof (it's systematic) 

●​ Example: "If you follow steps 1-7, you'll make $10K. It's not motivational, it's 

mathematical." 

Jason's Examples: 

Possible Level: "Amazon business is possible" (ASM 3) 

●​ Showed Raul: $3,500/mo on first product 

●​ Positioned as achievable 

Certain Level: "12 months to million dollar Amazon business" (ASM 4) 

●​ Had multiple case studies at every level 

●​ Broke it into phases 

●​ Showed the path 

Inevitable Level: "It's now undeniable - we have exclusive access to the best ranking solution 

in the world" 

●​ Positioned as mechanical advantage 

●​ Not motivational, systematic

Jason Fladlien's Webinar Commitment Framework

Complete Study Notes & Analysis

TABLE OF CONTENTS

1\. Core Concept: What is Commitment?

2\. The Five Types of Commitments

3\. Intro Commitments (Detailed Breakdown)

4\. Commitment Before Content

5\. Commitments Throughout Content

6\. Advanced Commitment Techniques

7\. Between Content and Offer Commitment

8\. Key Principles & Patterns

9\. Critical Quotes & Insights

10\. Putting It All Together

11\. Final Insights

1\. CORE CONCEPT: WHAT IS COMMITMENT?

Definition

Commitment in webinars is the strategic practice of getting your audience to make small agreements,

acknowledgments, and mental/verbal commitments throughout your presentation. It's not about securing

one massive "yes" at the end—it's about building unstoppable momentum through hundreds of micro-

commitments that naturally lead to the final purchase decision.

The Psychology Behind It

Every time someone agrees with you on a small point, they're:

Taking a tiny step toward the sale

Building consistency with their previous agreements

Reducing cognitive dissonance

Creating momentum that makes the final decision feel like the next logical step (not a leap)

Why It Works

Jason explains: "There are commitments within the mechanisms, commitments before a point is made,

commitments to tie a point down, and commitments in the intro as well."

The key insight: Multiple small commitments accumulate to create a state where saying "yes" to your

offer feels natural and inevitable.

2\. THE FIVE TYPES OF COMMITMENTS

Jason identifies commitments that appear in different sections of a webinar:

1\. Intro Commitments - Set the stage and frame

2\. Commitment Before Content - Prime the audience before teaching

3\. Commitments Throughout Content - Tie down each point as you teach

4\. Between Content and Offer - Bridge the gap strategically

5\. Advanced Commitment - High-level techniques for maximum impact

3\. INTRO COMMITMENTS (DETAILED BREAKDOWN)

A. "WHAT'S YOUR WHY?" EXERCISE

Purpose: Get people emotionally invested from the start by connecting to their deepest motivations.

The Three Elements (Make Your Why More Powerful):

1\. SPECIFIC (Not Vague)

Bad: "I want to make money"

Good: "I want to have $500 additional in my bank account tomorrow"

Why it works: Specificity = Reality. Vague goals are abstract; specific goals are tangible.

2\. SENSORY (Engage Multiple Senses)

Ask: What will you see, hear, feel, taste, smell?

Example: Not just "I want a car" but "I want to feel the leather seats, smell the new car smell,

hear the engine purr"

Why it works: The brain processes sensory information as REAL. The more senses involved, the

more real it becomes emotionally.

3\. INVOLVES OTHERS (Beyond Yourself)

Ask: How does achieving this affect people you care about?

Example: Not "I'll have more money" but "I'll be able to take my kids on that vacation they've

been asking about"

Why it works: We're more motivated for others than ourselves. Social motivation \> Personal

motivation.

Implementation Example from Slides:

Slide shows: "What's your why? The more specific the better, The more senses the better, The more it

involves others the better"

B. "ON THE SURFACE" COMMITMENT

Purpose: Address skepticism head-on and get permission to prove yourself.

The Pattern:

1\. Acknowledge what people are thinking ("This doesn't look that impressive...")

2\. Challenge their initial assumption ("Looks are often deceiving")

3\. Ask for a small commitment ("Do me a favor: just listen unbiased. Can you do that for me?")

Example from Slides:

"The opportunity doesn't seem 'that' incredible..."

"Looks are often deceiving"

"Do me a favor: just listen unbiased. Can you do that for me?"

Why This Works:

It demonstrates awareness (you know what they're thinking)

It prevents them from dismissing you early

It gets them to commit to fairness/open-mindedness

Once they commit to listening, they're more likely to actually listen

C. "MAYBE" FRAMEWORK

Purpose: Lower resistance by not asking for full commitment yet.

The Psychology: Jason doesn't ask "Will you buy?" He asks "Will you consider?" or "Can you see how

this MIGHT work?"

Key Phrases:

"That's ALL I'm asking of you - just be open minded"

"Let me show you the process"

"THEN make your own conclusions - cool?"

Why "Maybe" Works:

Removes pressure

People resist being pushed, but they'll willingly consider

"Maybe" feels safe; "definitely" feels risky

Once they're open to "maybe," they start convincing themselves

Example from Slides:

"That's ALL I'm asking of you - just be open minded...Let me show you the process...THEN make

your own conclusions - cool?"

D. THE "WITH THAT SAID" TRANSITION

Purpose: Create a smooth bridge from setup to teaching while maintaining commitment.

The Pattern:

1\. Acknowledge current state: "Suppose there was a way to do it that..."

2\. List benefits (uses bullet points for clarity)

3\. Transition: "With that said..."

4\. Ask simple question: "Then would you make a valid effort to take advantage to this opportunity?"

Example from Slides:

"Suppose there was a way to do it that...

Could get immediate, measurable profits

Didn't require any more expertise than operating a smart phone

Only required a moderate amount of effort and capital

And could scale infinitely

With that said...

Then would you make a valid effort to take advantage to this opportunity?"

Why This Works:

The list format makes it easy to agree with

Each bullet is a mini-commitment

"With that said" signals a shift while maintaining momentum

The question at the end locks in the commitment

E. MY AGENDA COMMITMENT

Purpose: State your intentions transparently to build trust.

Example from Slides:

"My agenda: Is to give you the best 60 minutes of copywriting you've ever heard to prove to you that

my offer at the end is an incredible deal..."

"Sound good?"

The Power of This:

1\. Transparency builds trust - You're upfront about selling

2\. Sets expectation of value - "Best 60 minutes you've ever heard"

3\. Frames the offer positively - It's an "incredible deal," not just a pitch

4\. Gets explicit agreement - "Sound good?" requires a mental (or actual) "yes"

Key Insight: Jason isn't hiding that he'll sell. He's saying: "I'm going to give you so much value that

you'll WANT to buy." This reframes the sale as a reward, not a manipulation.

4\. COMMITMENT BEFORE CONTENT

THE "ASM 5" EXAMPLE (Amazon Selling Machine)

The Setup: Instead of jumping straight into teaching, Jason first gets a commitment about whether they'd

WANT to try it IF certain conditions were met.

The Exact Pattern from Slides:

Question Slide:

"If I were to show you a process that ONLY myself and my customers specifically follow on

Amazon..."

"And you believe it MAY be possible for YOU to do it..."

"Then WILL you try it?"

Why This Sequence Works:

1\. "If I were to show you..."

Conditional language lowers resistance

Not claiming it works yet, just asking if they'd be interested

2\. "Only myself and my customers..."

Exclusivity creates value

Social proof (other customers are doing it)

3\. "MAY be possible for YOU..."

Again, not demanding certainty

Just asking if they can see possibility

Personal ("for YOU") makes it relevant

4\. "Then WILL you try it?"

Now asking for commitment

But it's based on conditions they've already agreed to

The "if-then" structure makes saying "yes" feel logical

GENIUS WEBINARS EXAMPLE

The "Commitments Throughout" Principle:

From the slides: "Commitments throughout"

Jason shows a specific slide titled "WHAT I NEED FROM YOU" with the text:

"Implementation nothing pains me more than giving you a solution yet run into years later still clinging 

hopelessly to your problem"

Breaking This Down:

1\. Emotional Appeal: "nothing pains me more"

Shows he cares

Creates guilt/responsibility in the audience

2\. Contrast: "giving you a solution" vs. "clinging hopelessly to your problem"

Solution = Good (what he's offering)

Problem = Bad (what they're experiencing)

The word "hopelessly" makes the problem feel worse

"Clinging" implies they're choosing to stay stuck

3\. The Implicit Commitment:

If you agree this would be painful/bad...

Then you're committing to NOT be that person

Which means you're committing to implement

THE "DEAL?" SLIDE

From the slides, there's a simple but powerful commitment:

"DEAL?"

Accompanied by question marks and boxes labeled:

"Offer, Price, Bonus, Guarantee, Objections"

Why This Works:

It's asking for agreement to the PROCESS, not the purchase

"Deal?" is casual, low-pressure language

It's preceded by showing them what they'll get (the boxes)

Once they agree to hear about these elements, they're more invested

5\. COMMITMENTS THROUGHOUT CONTENT

THE "I WAS ONLY SUPPOSED TO SELL 100 TOTAL SPOTS\!" EXAMPLE

The Setup (from transcript):

Social proof of scarcity

Screenshot of text messages showing rapid sales

Creates urgency naturally

The Commitment Technique:

From the slides: "I WAS ONLY SUPPOSED TO SELL 100 TOTAL SPOTS\!"

Then Jason asks: "Would you like to know exactly how we did it?"

Why This Pattern Is Genius:

1\. Proof before promise: He shows RESULTS first (sold out fast)

2\. Creates curiosity: How did they do it?

3\. Asks for commitment: "Would you like to know...?"

4\. If they say "yes" (even mentally), they're now invested in learning

This is a commitment to pay attention, which makes them more likely to value what comes next.

THE "TOUGHER QUESTION" TECHNIQUE

From the slides:

First Question: "If you did this and nothing else, do you think it would dramatically improve your

webinar effectiveness?"

Then... TOUGHER QUESTION: "Do you think your audience deserves it and every moment you deny

it to them is a moment you're actively participating in their despair?"

Analysis:

1\. Question 1 is easy to agree with

Yes, this would help

Simple, logical

2\. Question 2 is MUCH heavier

Moral weight: "deserves it"

Guilt: "deny it to them"

Urgency: "every moment"

Strong language: "actively participating in their despair"

The Psychology:

Once they agree to Question 1, they're primed for Question 2

Question 2 makes NOT implementing feel morally wrong

This isn't just about business success anymore—it's about responsibility

THE NDA EXAMPLE (Non-Disclosure Agreement)

From the slides, Jason shows an actual NDA document with the heading:

"NDA - Please Read Carefully"

Then asks:

"Do you think this increases the number of people who show up to your webinar?"

"Do you also think that it makes them more committed from the get go?"

"And will that lead to more sales?"

The Three-Question Ladder:

1\. First question: Simple, obvious benefit (more people show up)

2\. Second question: Deeper benefit (they're MORE committed)

3\. Third question: Final outcome (more sales)

Why This Works:

Each "yes" leads naturally to the next

By the time you reach "more sales," they've already agreed twice

The progression feels logical, not pushy

THE "HOW VALUABLE WOULD THAT BE?" TECHNIQUE

Jason repeatedly asks variations of:

"How valuable would that be?"

"Would you pay money for it?"

"Would you agree?"

Example from Slides:

After showing a feature: "HOW VALUABLE WOULD THAT BE?"

Then: "AND THE REAL QUESTION" / "WOULD YOU PAY MONEY FOR IT?"

The Pattern:

1\. Show something useful

2\. Ask them to evaluate its value

3\. Ask if they'd pay for it

Why This Works:

They're pricing your solution in their mind

Once they think "yes, I'd pay for that," the actual price becomes easier

You're having them convince themselves, not you convincing them

6\. ADVANCED COMMITMENT TECHNIQUES

THE "OVERVIEW + HOLD UP\!" PATTERN

From the slides:

"OVERVIEW" Slide: "12 Month Amazon Million Dollar Business"

Phase 1: $0 to $10,000/mo \[✓\]

Phase 2: $25,000 to $50,000/mo \[✓\]

Phase 3: $100,000+/mo

Then immediately...

"HOLD UP\!" Slide: "Before we go any further..." "I want you to go here and put your name and email

in to enter for $1,000" "We will draw two entries at the end of the webinar (must be present to win)"

"http://rapidcrush.com/believe"

Why This Sequence Is Brilliant:

1\. Overview creates excitement

Shows the path clearly

Checkmarks = social proof (others have done it)

Phase 3 is aspirational

2\. "Hold Up\!" creates a pattern interrupt

Just when they're getting interested...

You stop them

This creates tension and curiosity

3\. The contest commitment

Gets them to take action (enter email)

"Must be present to win" = commitment to stay until the end

$1,000 is significant enough to matter

The URL (rapidcrush.com/believe) even subtly reinforces belief

THE "AFFILIORAMA" BRIDGE COMMITMENT

From the slides:

"Yep" Slide: "That's 'all'" "Even though we've simplified it" "And made it completely outsourceable,

from A to Z" "You work that model and it WILL work for you... do you agree?"

Then... "Agree?" Slide: "A 90+, 365 follow up sequence would go a long ways to making money?" "A

simple website with content that is easily 'assembled' by low-skilled outsourced workers can, over time,

produce profit?" "It doesn't take a perfect squeeze page and free gift and your own product to make

money if you approach it correctly, right?"

Analysis:

1\. First Commitment ("Yep"):

Summarizes what they've learned

Uses "will" confidently

Asks for agreement on the model

2\. Second Commitment ("Agree?"):

Breaks down specific objections

Each point addresses a common fear:

"I don't want to write 365 emails"

"I don't have content creation skills"

"I don't have my own product"

Ends each with "right?" to prompt agreement

The Bridge: These aren't just random commitments. They're addressing OBJECTIONS before they even

come up, and getting people to commit that these objections don't matter.

THE "HOWEVER... THE FACTS..." PATTERN

From the slides:

"However..." Slide: "We're fooling each other if you are actually going to go out and do this..." "First, it's

NOT sexy whatsoever" "Second, it's not 'low downside, high upside'"

Then... "The facts..." Slide: "90 emails at 500 words each will cost you at the very least $500... if not

more\!" "Your free report will cost you $250 to create... if not more\!" "Heck, even setting up the website

will cost $100 or more to configure right... if not more\!"

What's Happening Here:

1\. "However" introduces reality

Breaks the hype

Shows honesty (which builds trust)

Addresses the "too good to be true" feeling

2\. "The facts" shows the COST of DIY

Actual dollar amounts

"If not more\!" emphasizes it could be worse

Creates pain around doing it themselves

The Implicit Commitment: By showing how hard/expensive it is to do alone, you're priming them to

value your done-for-you solution. They're committing (mentally) that they DON'T want to do this

themselves.

ASM 5 - THE ULTIMATE COMMITMENT LADDER

From the slides:

"Question" Slide: "Isn't it time you cash in on what can only be described as the single greatest

opportunity in the digital age to create your own fortune?"

Then... "Facts" Slide: "With Amazon..."

"Anyone, anywhere in the world can sell their own private labeled physical product"

"Amazon picks, packs, ships and fulfills ALL your orders for you without you ever having to touch

inventory"

"Customers with Amazon Prime or on purchases over $35 get free shipping when they buy your

products\!"

"Like clockwork, you get paid direct to your bank account every 14 days"

The Final Question: "Do you think it would make your audience more eager buying from you?" "Do

you also think that it makes them more committed from the get go?" "And will that lead to more sales?"

Why This Is Masterful:

1\. Big emotional question first

"Greatest opportunity in the digital age"

"Create your own fortune"

Gets them excited

2\. Then concrete facts

Each bullet removes an objection

Each bullet is easy to verify and agree with

3\. Then commitment questions

Three escalating questions

Each builds on the previous

Final question is about SALES (the real goal)

7\. BETWEEN CONTENT AND OFFER COMMITMENT

THE BRIDGE VISUAL

From the slides, Jason shows a graphic:

CONTENT (blue box) on the left

OFFER (orange box) on the right

A bridge connecting them with an arrow labeled "COMMITMENT"

The Insight: Most people think: Content → Offer

Jason's teaching: Content → Commitment → Offer

Why This Matters:

You can't jump straight from teaching to selling

There must be a bridge

The bridge is made of commitments

Without it, people feel the pitch is "coming out of nowhere"

EXAMPLE: "OMG PROJECT ASSIMILATION"

From the slides:

"Safe cracker society" Slide: "If such a society exists..." "Would you rather try to, on your own effort

with your own time attempt to crack the safe yourself?" "Or INVEST for access to the society?"

Then... "Most importantly" Slide: "Is the investment worth it?"

The Commitment Pattern:

1\. Metaphor makes it abstract (safe cracker society)

Abstract = Less threatening

Easier to agree with logically

2\. Present two options (DIY vs. invest in access)

DIY sounds hard

Investment sounds smart

3\. Ask which they'd choose

Most will choose the investment option

They're now committed to valuing "access"

4\. Then ask about value

"Is the investment worth it?"

They've already said they'd choose it, so this is easier to answer "yes"

EXAMPLE: "OMG PROJECT BREAKTHROUGH"

From the slides:

"Secret Society" Slide: "Imagine if there was a GROUP of like-minded individuals... guided by a

SHEER GENIUS..." "Who, across the 1,000,000+ web properties they control..." "Constantly CRACK

the code... no matter when it changes or how often it changes"

Then... "Secret Society" (Second Slide): "What would it take for you to believe that such a society

exists..." "Who is bold enough to assert that they can ALWAYS uncover the existing, easiest path to get

all the traffic you could ever want from Google?" "Well how about someone who constantly produces

results\!"

The Commitment Building:

1\. Creates desire ("Imagine if there was...")

Group of smart people

Led by genius

Controlling huge properties

Always solving the problem

2\. Creates belief ("What would it take...?")

Asks what evidence they'd need

Then provides that evidence (results)

3\. The implicit commitment:

If such a thing existed, would you want in?

What would convince you it's real?

Here's proof it's real

So... do you want in?

EXAMPLE: "PRODUCT ECLASS"

From the slides:

"THE RESULT?" Slide: "HE TRANSFORMED AN ABANDONED IDEA INTO $19,000 IN ABOUT

A WEEKS TIME\!"

Then... "I COULD GO ON..." Slide: "I think you get it" "Other who are JUST LIKE YOU did it..."

"Does that mean you CAN?"

The Three-Part Commitment:

1\. Show incredible result ($19,000 in a week)

Specific number

Specific timeframe

Real person (implied)

2\. Connect to audience ("just like you")

Not special people

Regular people

People they can identify with

3\. Ask if they can do it

"Does that mean you CAN?"

This is THE key commitment

They're saying "yes, I can do this"

EXAMPLE: "RAMP ENGINE"

From the slides:

"TESTED ACROSS THE BOARD" Slide: Shows a graph of orders over time (234,182 New Orders,

234,182 Orders Shipped) "For dozens of products in dozens of different niches"

Then... "WHAT WOULD IT MEAN TO YOU" Slide: "If I give you this exact sequence that you can

simply, in minutes, point and click your way of putting in your own product(s) into place?" "So you could

be in a position by the end of this very week to start making some real money from your ecommerce

business... while improving your presence and profits on Amazon?"

The Commitment Flow:

1\. Proof (the graph + "dozens of products")

Not just one success

Proven across many niches

Visual proof is powerful

2\. Make it easy ("point and click")

Removes skill barrier

"In minutes" removes time barrier

3\. Paint the outcome ("by end of this week")

Specific timeline

"Real money" (not just theory)

"Improving presence AND profits" (two benefits)

4\. The implicit commitment:

This works (you've seen proof)

It's easy (point and click)

It's fast (minutes to set up, results this week)

So... would you want this?

8\. KEY PRINCIPLES & PATTERNS

PRINCIPLE 1: The Commitment Ladder

Always build from smaller to bigger commitments:

1\. Will you listen? (smallest)

2\. Does this make sense?

3\. Can you see how this would help?

4\. Would you do this if you could?

5\. Would you pay for this? (biggest before the ask)

Why It Works: Each "yes" makes the next one easier. You're building momentum, not asking for a huge

leap.

PRINCIPLE 2: Address Objections AS Commitments

Don't wait for objections—get people to commit that objections don't matter.

Example from Affiliorama:

"It doesn't take a perfect squeeze page and free gift and your own product to make money if you

approach it correctly, right?"

This gets them to commit that they DON'T need these things before they even think to object about not

having them.

PRINCIPLE 3: Use "With That Said" or "However" as Transition Commitments

These phrases signal a shift while maintaining momentum.

"With that said..." = We've established X, now let's discuss Y

"However..." = Here's the reality / Here's what you might not know

Both keep people engaged through the transition.

PRINCIPLE 4: The Question → Facts → Question Pattern

1\. Ask a big emotional question (gets them excited)

2\. Present logical facts (satisfies their rational brain)

3\. Ask a commitment question (they say yes because emotion + logic are aligned)

PRINCIPLE 5: Make Them Evaluate Value

Don't tell them your offer is valuable—ask them if they think it's valuable.

Questions like:

"How valuable would that be?"

"Would you pay money for it?"

"Is the investment worth it?"

When THEY conclude it's valuable, it's more powerful than you claiming it.

PRINCIPLE 6: Create Desire for the Category Before Selling Your Specific Solution

Example: The "Secret Society" metaphor

First, get them to want access to ANY solution like this

Then reveal yours is that solution

They've already committed to wanting it before knowing it's yours

PRINCIPLE 7: Use Social Proof as Commitment

"Others just like you did it" is a commitment primer.

If others like them could do it, then they can too. Once they agree with this logic, they're more likely to

commit.

PRINCIPLE 8: The "Must Be Present to Win" Technique

Any contest/prize that requires staying until the end is a commitment device.

They've:

1\. Given you their email (micro-commitment)

2\. Committed to staying (time commitment)

3\. Invested attention (sunk cost)

By the time you pitch, they've already invested too much to leave.

9\. CRITICAL QUOTES & INSIGHTS

On Why Commitment Matters

"There are commitments within the mechanisms, commitments before a point is made,

commitments to tie a point down, and commitments in the intro as well."

Translation: Commitments aren't just one thing—they're woven throughout the entire presentation. Every

section has its own commitment strategy.

On Specificity

"The more specific the better, The more senses the better, The more it involves others the better"

This applies to:

Getting people to define their "why"

Painting the outcome they want

Making your offer tangible

Vague = Forgettable. Specific = Real.

On Implementation

"Implementation nothing pains me more than giving you a solution yet run into years later still

clinging hopelessly to your problem"

This isn't just a commitment—it's a powerful guilt/responsibility trigger. It says:

I care about your success

NOT implementing is a choice

That choice means you're choosing to stay stuck

That would pain me (so it should pain you too)

On Honesty Building Trust

"We're fooling each other if you are actually going to go out and do this... First, it's NOT sexy

whatsoever. Second, it's not 'low downside, high upside'"

By admitting the DOWNSIDES of doing it yourself, you:

1\. Build credibility (you're honest)

2\. Set up the value of your solution (which solves these problems)

3\. Get them to commit that they DON'T want to DIY

On Framing the Sale

"My agenda: Is to give you the best 60 minutes of copywriting you've ever heard to prove to you

that my offer at the end is an incredible deal... Sound good?"

This reframes the entire presentation:

You're not tricking them

You're giving massive value FIRST

The offer is a REWARD for paying attention

They agree to this frame upfront

On Removing Pressure

"That's ALL I'm asking of you - just be open minded...Let me show you the process...THEN make

your own conclusions - cool?"

The word "just" minimizes the ask. "Then make your own conclusions" removes pressure. They're not

committing to buy—just to listen and decide for themselves. This lowers resistance.

On Building Urgency Without Hype

"I WAS ONLY SUPPOSED TO SELL 100 TOTAL SPOTS\!"

This isn't manufactured scarcity—it's real. By showing actual evidence (text messages, sold-out proof), he

creates genuine urgency without being "salesy."

10\. PUTTING IT ALL TOGETHER: THE COMPLETE COMMITMENT

FLOW

Here's how all these pieces work together in a typical Jason Fladlien webinar:

PHASE 1: INTRO (First 5-10 minutes)

1\. What's Your Why? - Get them emotionally connected

2\. Address Surface Skepticism - "It doesn't look incredible, but listen unbiased"

3\. Maybe Framework - Lower pressure, ask for open-mindedness

4\. My Agenda - Transparent about the pitch, frame value first

5\. With That Said - Transition with commitment question

Goal: By the end of intro, they've committed to listening with an open mind and evaluating fairly.

PHASE 2: BEFORE CONTENT (Transition into teaching)

1\. Commitment to the Possibility - "If I showed you X, would you try it?"

2\. Set Implementation Expectation - "I need you to actually use this"

3\. Contest/Prize - "Enter to win, but must be present"

Goal: They're committed to the idea of trying it IF it works, and they're staying until the end.

PHASE 3: THROUGHOUT CONTENT (The teaching section)

1\. After Each Major Point: "Does this make sense?"

2\. After Showing Proof: "Would you like to know how we did it?"

3\. After Removing Objection: "Would you agree that \[objection\] doesn't matter?"

4\. Value Evaluation: "How valuable would that be?"

Goal: Each point is "locked in" with agreement. By the end, they've said "yes" dozens of times.

PHASE 4: BETWEEN CONTENT AND OFFER (The Bridge)

1\. Metaphor/Story - Make it about a principle, not your product yet

2\. Get Them to Value the Category - "Would you want access to something like this?"

3\. Show It Exists and Works - Proof that the solution is real

4\. Connect It to Them - "Others like you did it, can you?"

Goal: They want the solution before you even reveal it's yours to sell.

PHASE 5: THE OFFER (Finally, the pitch)

At this point, if you've done the above correctly:

They've committed to listening

They've committed that the problem matters

They've committed that your solution makes sense

They've committed that others like them have succeeded

They've committed that they CAN do it too

They've committed that something like this would be valuable

Now the offer feels like the natural next step, not a pitch.

11\. FINAL INSIGHTS

What Makes Jason's Approach Different

Most webinar presenters think in terms of:

1\. Hook them

2\. Teach them

3\. Pitch them

Jason thinks in terms of:

1\. Hook them with commitment

2\. Teach them while getting commitment

3\. Bridge with more commitment

4\. Pitch them as the natural conclusion of all those commitments

The Psychological Foundation

Commitment and Consistency Principle (Robert Cialdini): Once people commit to something

(especially publicly or in writing), they're psychologically driven to remain consistent with that

commitment.

Jason leverages this by getting hundreds of tiny commitments. Each one makes the next easier, and by the

time he asks for the sale, saying "yes" is just being consistent with everything they've already agreed to.

The Most Important Takeaway

Commitment isn't manipulation—it's alignment.

You're not tricking people into buying something they don't want. You're helping them:

1\. Clarify what they want

2\. See that your solution delivers it

3\. Commit to taking action (because they've already committed to the smaller pieces)

When done ethically, commitment makes the sale EASIER for both parties. The prospect feels confident

because they've thoroughly evaluated each piece. You feel confident because you know they're truly

ready.

12\. STUDY RECOMMENDATIONS

To truly master this material:

1\. Watch the webinar multiple times - Once for content, once specifically tracking commitments

2\. Count the commitments - How many times does Jason get agreement? You'll be surprised

3\. Identify the patterns - Notice where commitments appear (intro, before points, after points,

transitions)

4\. Analyze your own presentations - Where are you missing commitment opportunities?

5\. Practice the language - Get comfortable with "Can you see...?", "Would you agree...?", "Does that

make sense?"

CONCLUSION

Jason Fladlien's commitment framework isn't a "trick" or a "hack." It's a sophisticated understanding of

how people make decisions. By guiding them through progressive commitments, you're helping them:

Clarify their thinking

Evaluate options logically

Build confidence in the decision

Align their actions with their goals

The most powerful insight: People don't resist buying—they resist making big decisions without

confidence. Commitments build that confidence, step by step, until the final "yes" feels natural and right.

Master this framework, and you won't need to "convince" anyone. They'll convince themselves—with

your guidance.

End of Study Notes

These notes are based on careful analysis of Jason Fladlien's Step 8: Commitment webinar transcript and

slides. For best results, review this document alongside the original materials and practice implementing

these principles in real presentations.

The Complete Webinar Close Masterclass

Jason Fladlien's Transition & Close Framework - Full Analysis

Table of Contents

1\. Executive Summary

2\. Core Philosophy

3\. The Transition Framework

4\. Psychological Principles

5\. Language Patterns & Scripts

6\. The Close Structure

7\. Objection Handling

8\. Advanced Techniques

9\. Implementation Guide

10\. Key Takeaways

Executive Summary

This document contains a complete analysis of Jason Fladlien's webinar transition and close methodology,

widely regarded as one of the most effective sales presentation frameworks in digital marketing. The

system generates millions in sales through psychological positioning, strategic objection handling, and

customer-centric closing sequences.

Core Insight: The transition isn't about manipulation—it's about creating a clear decision point where

prospects feel empowered to choose the path that serves them best.

Core Philosophy

The "Notes Graveyard" Concept

Problem Identified:

People attend webinars and take extensive notes

Notes go "somewhere" (home, office, attic, garage, trunk of old cars)

These notes become part of the "notes graveyard"—where knowledge goes to die

The webinar content never gets implemented

Psychological Truth:

"So who's fooling who? If the notes from this webinar are going to go to the notes graveyard as well,

then what's the point?"

Key Insight: Attending a webinar alone doesn't create change. Action creates change. The transition must

bridge the gap between learning and implementation.

Bridging the Gap Philosophy

Three Requirements for Success:

1\. The Right Tools - You need proper systems and resources

2\. The Right Training - You need step-by-step guidance

3\. The Right Turnkey Solution - You need done-for-you components to remove implementation

barriers

Jason's Positioning:

"We can't do it with 1 simple webinar. You need more than that. You need ongoing mentoring, special

tools, accountability, resources, and more\!"

This frames the webinar as valuable but insufficient—creating natural demand for the complete solution.

The Transition Framework

Phase 1: The Momentum Recap

Purpose: Summarize value delivered and build "yes momentum"

Structure:

1\. Question 1: "Has our time on the webinar thus far been well spent?"

2\. Question 2: "Do you now feel more confident with using webinars?"

3\. Question 3: "Can you see how even just one or two things I showed you today can help you in your

business, regardless of whether you use it in webinars or somewhere else?"

4\. Question 4: "Are you excited to make a go out of this to see what wonderful things it opens up for

you in life?"

Psychological Function:

Gets prospects in agreement state

Builds positive momentum

Transitions from information to implementation

Creates receptivity for the offer

Language Pattern:

"Thus far..." → establishes you've already delivered value

"More confident..." → plants seed of progress

"One or two things..." → low threshold, easy agreement

"Excited to..." → emotional engagement

Phase 2: The DIY Path Acknowledgment

Framework:

"You can do this on your own..."

I have absolute confidence that you could take everything we 

covered here today and on your own go out and put it into action 

and get results that you previously thought weren't possible.

I am certain just with the information we've covered on this 

webinar today, left on your own with your own resources, time 

and effort you can make a go at this in a way most people will 

never be able to.

And if that were your only option it would be a worthy ideal 

to pursue.

Why This Works:

1\. Removes pressure - You're explicitly giving permission to leave

2\. Shows respect - You believe they're capable

3\. Eliminates reactance - No hard selling triggers resistance

4\. Sets up contrast - "But what if there were another option..."

Phase 3: The "What If" Bridge

The Pivot Language:

"But what if there were another option. An option that allowed 

us to go at this together? A chance to go deeper than we can in 

one short hour on one fast moving webinar connecting together 

just this one time."

Psychological Elements:

Partnership frame: "go at this together"

Depth vs. breadth: "go deeper than we can in one short hour"

Time scarcity: "just this one time"

Opportunity frame: "what if" (not "you should")

Phase 4: The Obligation Offer

The Setup:

"What if I played an active role in your results, took a 

responsibility in your success and had an obligation to your 

outcome? How would that make things different?"

Why This Is Powerful:

1\. Role reversal - You're taking on the burden

2\. Risk reversal - You're committing to their success

3\. Accountability shift - It's now on you, not them

4\. Outcome focus - Not selling features, selling responsibility for results

Phase 5: The Empowerment Offer

The Framework:

"Do you think in that situation I could empower you with any 

and all resources at my disposal to stack the deck in your 

favor to make success all but inevitable?"

Psychological Triggers:

"Empower you" - You become more capable

"Any and all resources" - Unlimited support

"Stack the deck in your favor" - Unfair advantage

"All but inevitable" - Success becomes default outcome

Key Language Pattern: Questions rather than statements create agreement without resistance.

Psychological Principles

1\. The Principle of Consistency

Application: Once prospects agree the webinar was valuable, they're psychologically primed to continue

the journey (cognitive consistency).

Script Example:

"If you found value in the last hour, imagine what we can 

accomplish together over the next 7 weeks..."

2\. The Autonomy Preservation Principle

Core Insight: People resist when they feel forced. Preserve autonomy at all times.

Implementation:

Always acknowledge they CAN do it alone

Frame the offer as an OPTION, never a requirement

Use "What if" language instead of "You should"

Jason's Words:

"I'm not trying to convince you to do anything. I'm simply presenting an option for those who want to

go deeper."

3\. The Gap Creation Principle

Framework:

Current State → \[GAP\] → Desired State

The webinar shows them the desired state

The transition reveals the gap

The offer bridges the gap

Language Pattern:

"You now KNOW what's possible... \[desired state\]

But knowing and DOING are different... \[gap\]

That's where \[program\] comes in..." \[bridge\]

4\. The Burden Shifting Principle

Psychological Shift:

Before: "I need to convince you to buy"

After: "I'm taking responsibility for your success"

Implementation:

"I'm not asking you to figure this out alone.

I'm committing to guide you every step of the way.

Your success becomes my responsibility."

5\. The Contrast Principle

Application: Always show the DIY path BEFORE presenting your solution.

Why: Your offer looks dramatically better when contrasted with the difficult alternative.

Structure:

Option A (DIY):

\- Figure it out yourself

\- Trial and error

\- Time-consuming

\- Uncertain results

\[CONTRAST\]

Option B (Our Program):

\- Step-by-step guidance

\- Proven system

\- Fast implementation

\- Guaranteed support

6\. The Social Proof Cascade

Framework: Stack multiple types of proof:

1\. Results proof - "960 sellers generated $1.057 BILLION"

2\. Peer proof - "Just like these students did..."

3\. Authority proof - "Dan and Dylan started with just $600..."

4\. Demonstration proof - Showing actual results/dashboards

7\. The Inevitability Principle

Language Pattern:

"Make success all but inevitable"

"Stack the deck in your favor"

"Nearly impossible to fail"

Psychological Effect: Shifts perceived probability of success from uncertain to near-certain.

Language Patterns & Scripts

Pattern 1: The "Thus Far" Frame

Structure: "Thus far \[summary of value\] + \[forward-looking question\]"

Examples:

"Thus far, has our time been well spent?"

"Thus far, do you feel more confident?"

"Thus far, can you see how this could help?"

Why It Works:

Acknowledges value already delivered

Creates sense of progression

Natural setup for "what comes next"

Pattern 2: The "What If" Sequence

Structure: "What if \[possibility\] + \[benefit\] + \[question\]?"

Examples:

"What if I played an active role in your results?"

"What if I had shortcuts and support systems?"

"What if I could empower you with any and all resources?"

Psychological Function:

Opens possibility without pressure

Engages imagination

Bypasses resistance

Pattern 3: The Capability Acknowledgment

Structure: "I have \[confidence/certainty\] that you could \[desired outcome\]"

Example:

"I have absolute confidence that you could take everything we 

covered here today and on your own go out and put it into action 

and get results that you previously thought weren't possible."

Why This Works:

Shows respect

Reduces defensiveness

Makes offer seem like bonus, not necessity

Pattern 4: The Responsibility Shift

Structure: "I take \[responsibility\] for \[their outcome\]"

Examples:

"I take responsibility in your success"

"I have an obligation to your outcome"

"I commit to empowering you with any and all resources"

Psychological Effect: Reverses typical buyer/seller dynamic—now the seller is on the hook.

Pattern 5: The Contrast Stack

Structure: "\[Difficult path acknowledgment\] BUT \[easier path offer\]"

Example:

"You could spend time figuring this out through trial and error...

BUT what if there was a proven path that's already worked for 960+ others?"

Pattern 6: The Question Close

Structure: Series of yes-building questions leading to offer

Example Sequence:

1\. "Has our time been well spent?" \[Yes\]

2\. "Do you feel more confident now?" \[Yes\]

3\. "Can you see how this could help?" \[Yes\]

4\. "Would you love to hear about an option to go deeper?" \[Natural yes\]

Pattern 7: The "Imagine If" Future Pace

Structure: "Imagine if \[you had solution\] + \[emotional benefit\]"

Example:

"Imagine if you had a complete system, proven methods, and 

ongoing support. How would that change your timeline to success?"

The Close Structure

The Two Choices Close

Framework:

Choice 1: You CAN do this

\- Acknowledge their capability

\- Respect their autonomy

\- Frame DIY path as legitimate

Choice 2: What if there were another option?

\- Partnership rather than DIY

\- Deeper than one webinar

\- Active role in their results

\- Responsibility for their success

\- All resources at their disposal

Full Script:

I have absolute confidence that you could take everything we 

covered here today and on your own go out and put it into action 

and get results that you previously thought weren't possible.

I am certain just with the information we've covered on this 

webinar today, left on your own with your own resources, time 

and effort you can make a go at this in a way most people will 

never be able to.

And if that were your only option it would be a worthy ideal 

to pursue.

\[PAUSE\]

But what if there were another option. An option that allowed 

us to go at this together? A chance to go deeper than we can in 

one short hour on one fast moving webinar connecting together 

just this one time.

What if I played an active role in your results, took a 

responsibility in your success and had an obligation to your 

outcome? How would that make things different?

Do you think in that situation I could empower you with any 

and all resources at my disposal to stack the deck in your 

favor to make success all but inevitable?

If I had shortcuts and support systems and additional insights 

that go even deeper and more thorough into getting you the result 

you want, desire and quite frankly deserve.

If I had such an option for you, would you love to hear more 

about it and what it could do for you?

That's why it's with great pleasure that I introduce you to...

\[PROGRAM NAME\]

The Program Introduction

Framework: After the two choices close, introduce the program with:

1\. The Name - Clear, benefit-driven

2\. The Promise - What transformation it delivers

3\. The Proof - Social proof cascade

4\. The Components - What's included

5\. The Investment - Price and value stack

6\. The Guarantee - Risk reversal

7\. The Bonuses - Additional value

8\. The Call to Action - Clear next steps

Example: The Wholesale Formula Transition

Setup - The Amazon Authority Frame:

"Amazon is the most trusted brand in the world and by far the 

easiest place to shop online. And the formula I showed you today 

is exactly what Amazon wants its sellers to follow more so than 

any other approach."

Social Proof:

"960 sellers generated over $1.057 BILLION in sales in just a 

handful of years using this exact approach."

Origin Story:

"Dan and Dylan are two southern gentlemen from tiny Corbin, 

Kentucky, population 7261, who started their Amazon career with 

a dream and a $600 credit card limit, which they maxed out 

placing their first wholesale order.

Within 6 months that $600 had turned into $500,000 in product 

sales. They brute-forced their way to Amazon success, and soon 

felt it their duty to share their approach with others."

Community Proof:

"They have a community of customers they've helped along the way 

who support each other by exchanging best practices for 

wholesaling products from brands and selling those products on 

Amazon."

Current State:

"Every single time I talk to these guys they're showing me 

something new and cool they've discovered."

The Offer Introduction:

"Even if you never heard of Amazon before today, I'm confident 

we can have you bringing in real, life-changing money in short 

order through The Wholesale Formula and our special, exclusive 

resources we will give to you for FREE when you sign up through 

us to join The Wholesale Formula."

Objection Handling

Pre-Emptive Objection Handling

Principle: Address objections before they're raised.

Common Objections & Scripts:

Objection 1: "I don't like to sell"

Response:

"Now let's eliminate the excuses...

Is it you don't like to sell? Too much competition? 

What about Google?

\[Pause\]

Actually, the approach I'm showing you requires no selling at all.

The platform does all the selling for you."

Objection 2: "I don't have time"

Response:

"You need me in your ear for the next 7 weeks. If you TRULY are 

going to use what I showed you today..."

Reframe: Position your program as time-saving, not time-consuming.

Objection 3: "I'm not sure how to make it work"

Response:

"I understand the power of this, I'm just not quite sure how to 

make it work?

\[Acknowledge the doubt\]

That's the biggie... The biggest objection most people have. 

And that's exactly what \[program\] solves."

Objection 4: "I can figure it out myself"

Already Handled: The entire "You CAN do this" opening handles this objection.

The "Excuse Elimination" Framework

Structure:

1\. List the excuse

2\. Show understanding

3\. Provide counter-evidence

4\. Offer solution through your program

Example:

"Is it you don't like to sell?" \[Excuse\]

"I get it—most people don't." \[Understanding\]

"But here's the thing: this platform does all the selling 

for you." \[Counter-evidence\]

"And in \[program\], we'll show you exactly how to set it up 

so selling is completely automated." \[Solution\]

Advanced Techniques

Technique 1: The Origin Story Pattern

Why It Works: Origin stories create relatability and prove possibility.

Structure:

1\. Humble beginnings - "Started with just $600"

2\. The struggle - "Maxed out credit card"

3\. The breakthrough - "Within 6 months, $500,000"

4\. The duty to share - "Felt it their duty to share"

Example (Dan & Dylan):

"Dan and Dylan are two southern gentlemen from tiny Corbin, 

Kentucky, population 7261 who started their Amazon career with 

a dream and a $600 credit card limit, which they maxed out 

placing their first wholesale order.

Within 6 months that $600 had turn into $500,000 in product 

sales. Dylan and Dan did not have the benefit of experience, 

but they did have the benefit of stubbornness. They brute-forced 

their way to Amazon success, and soon felt it their duty to 

share their approach with others."

Psychological Elements:

Relatability: Small town, limited resources

Proof: Massive results from humble start

Character: "Stubbornness" = relatable trait

Mission: "Felt it their duty" = serving vs. selling

Technique 2: The Borrowed Authority

Concept: Use external validation to boost credibility.

Examples:

"This is EXACTLY what Amazon wants its sellers to do"

"Shopify Approved Program"

"Featured in \[major publication\]"

Why It Works: Third-party validation bypasses skepticism.

Technique 3: The Ongoing Discovery Frame

Language:

"Every single time I talk to these guys they're showing me 

something new and cool they've discovered."

Psychological Effect:

Creates FOMO (what else haven't you shared?)

Suggests continuous improvement

Implies you're getting cutting-edge info

Technique 4: The Community Proof

Framework:

"They have a community of customers they've helped along the 

way who support each other by exchanging best practices for 

wholesaling products from brands and selling those products 

on Amazon."

Why This Works:

Proves system works (960 students)

Shows ongoing support

Creates belonging desire

Demonstrates real implementation

Technique 5: The Specific Number Strategy

Examples:

"960 sellers" (not "hundreds")

"$1.057 BILLION" (not "over a billion")

"Population 7261" (not "small town")

"$600 credit card limit" (not "limited resources")

Psychological Effect: Specificity = credibility

Technique 6: The Confident Guarantee

Framework:

"Even if you never heard of \[topic\] before today, I'm confident 

we can have you bringing in real, life-changing money in short 

order through \[program\]."

Elements:

Even if = handles lack of experience

I'm confident = certainty transfer

Real, life-changing money = specific outcome

In short order = fast results

Technique 7: The Stakes Elevation

Language Pattern:

"Which are not making you the money that you deserve\!"

Psychological Function: Elevates the decision from "nice to have" to "deserve to have"

Case Study: The Wholesale Formula

The Complete Sequence

1\. Authority Frame:

"Amazon is the most valuable brand in the world"

"This is exactly what Amazon wants sellers to do"

2\. Social Proof:

"960 students sold $1.057 BILLION"

3\. Origin Story:

"Dan and Dylan from Corbin, Kentucky (population 7261)

Started with $600 credit card limit

Turned it into $500,000 in 6 months"

4\. Ongoing Discovery:

"Every time I talk to these guys they're showing me 

something new and cool they've discovered"

5\. Community:

"They have a community of customers who support each other 

by exchanging best practices"

6\. The Promise:

"Even if you never heard of Amazon before today, I'm confident 

we can have you bringing in real, life-changing money in short 

order through The Wholesale Formula"

Why This Works

Stacked Credibility:

1\. Amazon's authority (external)

2\. Billion-dollar proof (results)

3\. Origin story (relatability)

4\. Community (social proof)

5\. Ongoing discovery (continuous value)

Emotional Journey:

Relatability (small town beginnings)

Hope (massive results possible)

Belonging (join community)

Confidence (you can do this too)

Implementation Guide

Step 1: Craft Your Transition

Template:

\[Value Recap Questions\]

1\. Has our time been well spent?

2\. Do you feel more confident now?

3\. Can you see how this could help you?

\[DIY Path Acknowledgment\]

I have absolute confidence you could take what we covered 

and implement it on your own...

\[The Bridge\]

But what if there were another option...

\[The Offer\]

That's why I'd like to introduce you to \[PROGRAM\]

Step 2: Gather Your Proof Elements

Required Components:

1\. Student results (specific numbers)

2\. Origin story (your journey or student journey)

3\. Community proof (how many helped)

4\. Authority validation (third-party endorsement)

5\. Ongoing value (what's new/updated)

Step 3: Build Your Stack

Stack Order:

1\. Acknowledge capability (You CAN do this)

2\. Offer partnership (But what if we did this together?)

3\. Take responsibility (I commit to your success)

4\. Show proof (Others have done it)

5\. Make offer (Here's how to join)

6\. Handle objections (Remove barriers)

7\. Call to action (Here's what to do next)

Step 4: Practice the Language Patterns

Key Patterns to Master:

1\. "Thus far..." frame

2\. "What if..." questions

3\. "I have confidence that you..." respect

4\. "But what if there were another option..." bridge

5\. "Would you love to hear more about..." invitation

Step 5: Test and Refine

Metrics to Track:

Conversion rate (percentage who buy)

Cart abandonment (who starts but doesn't finish)

Most common objections

Where people drop off

Questions in chat/comments

Key Takeaways

The 10 Most Important Principles

1\. Never sacrifice respect for conversion - Always acknowledge they CAN do it alone

2\. The transition is about service, not selling - You're offering help, not forcing purchase

3\. Questions create agreement without resistance - Ask, don't tell

4\. Your confidence transfers to them - "I'm confident you can" becomes "I'm confident I can"

5\. Social proof must be specific - Numbers, names, places, amounts

6\. The origin story creates possibility - "If they did it from that starting point, I can too"

7\. Objections should be addressed before they're raised - Pre-emptive handling is most effective

8\. The partnership frame reverses typical sales dynamics - You're now on the hook for their success

9\. Community proof creates belonging desire - People want to be part of something working

10\. The close is a natural continuation, not a pivot - It should feel like the next logical step

The 5 Fatal Mistakes to Avoid

1\. The Hard Pitch

❌  "You need to buy this now\!"

✅  "Would you love to hear about an option?"

2\. The Value Dump

❌  Listing features without context

✅  Showing how each component solves a specific problem

3\. The Pressure Close

❌  Making them feel forced

✅  Preserving autonomy while presenting opportunity

4\. The Weak Proof

❌  "Many people have succeeded"

✅  "960 students generated $1.057 billion"

5\. The Unclear Call to Action

❌  "If you're interested, check out the link below"

✅  "Click the button below to secure your spot now"

Advanced Applications

For Different Market Types

1\. For Sophisticated/Skeptical Markets:

"I know you've probably seen a lot of programs promising \[result\].

And I know you're probably skeptical. Good. You should be.

That's why I'm not asking you to just trust me. I'm asking you 

to look at the 960 students who've generated over $1 billion 

using this exact approach.

I'm not asking you to believe in theory. I'm asking you to 

look at results."

2\. For Newbie/Overwhelmed Markets:

"Even if you've never \[done thing\] before, even if you're 

starting from absolute zero, even if you have no idea where 

to begin...

That's exactly who this was designed for. That's exactly who 

Dan and Dylan were when they started with just $600."

3\. For Time-Starved Markets:

"I know you're thinking 'I don't have time for this.'

That's exactly why we built it this way. You don't need 

40 hours a week. You need the RIGHT 7 hours a week with 

the RIGHT guidance."

For Different Price Points

Low Ticket ($100-$500):

Emphasize quick start

Focus on immediate value

Minimize risk with guarantee

Mid Ticket ($500-$2,000):

Balance quick wins with depth

Emphasize support and community

Show comprehensive results

High Ticket ($2,000+):

Focus on transformation

Emphasize partnership and access

Show ROI and long-term value

For Different Relationship Stages

Cold Traffic:

Heavy emphasis on proof

Clear risk reversal

Multiple proof types (results, testimonials, authority)

Warm Traffic:

Reference previous interactions

Build on existing trust

Focus on next logical step

Hot Traffic (Past Customers):

Acknowledge past success

Position as next level

VIP/priority access

The Psychology Behind Each Element

Why "Thus Far" Works

Cognitive Function:

Acknowledges progress already made

Creates sense of momentum

Sets up "what comes next" naturally

Emotional Function:

Validates time investment

Creates positive association

Reduces regret/loss aversion

Why "You CAN Do This Alone" Works

Psychological Principles:

1\. Autonomy Preservation - Humans resist when freedom is threatened

2\. Respect Recognition - Shows you value their capability

3\. Reverse Psychology - Giving permission to leave makes them want to stay

4\. Contrast Setup - Makes your offer look better by comparison

Why "What If" Works

Cognitive Function:

Engages imagination without commitment

Bypasses critical mind

Opens possibility space

Emotional Function:

Hope activation

Curiosity trigger

Low-threat exploration

Why The Obligation Offer Works

Role Reversal:

Typical: Buyer takes risk

This: Seller takes responsibility

Psychological Effect:

Reduces perceived risk

Creates partnership feel

Shifts burden of success

Why Specific Numbers Work

Cognitive Science:

Specificity signals accuracy

Round numbers seem estimated

Exact numbers seem researched

Examples:

❌  "Hundreds of students"

✅  "960 students"

❌  "Over a billion dollars"

✅  "$1.057 billion"

❌  "Small town"

✅  "Population 7,261"

Real-World Examples Analyzed

Example 1: The Wholesale Formula (Complete Analysis)

Opening Frame:

"Amazon is the most valuable brand in the world and by far 

the easiest place to shop online."

Analysis:

Establishes authority (Amazon)

Creates certainty (most valuable, easiest)

Sets up "this is the right platform" frame

Social Proof:

"960 sellers generated $1.057 billion"

Analysis:

Specific number (960, not "hundreds")

Massive result ($1.057B)

Proof of concept at scale

Origin Story:

"Dan and Dylan from Corbin, Kentucky (pop. 7261)

Started with $600 credit card

$500,000 in 6 months"

Analysis:

Small town relatability

Limited starting capital

Massive result (833x return)

Proves accessibility

Character Element:

"They did not have the benefit of experience, but they did 

have the benefit of stubbornness."

Analysis:

Relatable trait (stubbornness)

Removes "special skills" objection

Character \> credentials

Duty Frame:

"Soon felt it their duty to share their approach with others"

Analysis:

Mission-driven (not money-driven)

Service orientation

Creates trust

Community Proof:

"They have a community who support each other by exchanging 

best practices"

Analysis:

Ongoing value

Peer support

Continuous improvement

Current State:

"Every single time I talk to these guys they're showing me 

something new and cool they've discovered"

Analysis:

Cutting edge (not static)

FOMO trigger

Continuous innovation

The Promise:

"Even if you never heard of Amazon before today, I'm confident 

we can have you bringing in real, life-changing money in short 

order"

Analysis:

Handles newbie objection

Strong confidence transfer

Specific outcome (life-changing money)

Time frame (short order)

Example 2: Product Creation Offer

The Setup:

"I think you get it. Others who are JUST LIKE YOU did it...

Does that mean you CAN?"

Analysis:

Peer proof (just like you)

Capability question

Self-identification

The Vision:

"If we parted ways right now... would you immediately go out...

and set the world on fire... with your newfound product 

creation skills?"

Analysis:

Forces honest self-assessment

Creates gap awareness

Emotional language ("set world on fire")

The Doubt:

"DOUBTFUL\! I probably know you better than you know yourself.

You're a stubborn bugger who – if I let you – will quickly 

default back to your old habits..."

Analysis:

Pattern interruption (calling them out)

Creates self-awareness

Identifies the real obstacle (habits, not knowledge)

The Gap:

"which are not making you the money that you deserve\!"

Analysis:

Stakes elevation (deserve)

Creates urgency

Moral imperative

The Solution Preview:

"That's why... I won't let that happen\!

You need me in your ear for the next 7 weeks

If you TRULY are going to use what I showed you today..."

Analysis:

Takes control (I won't let that happen)

Specific timeframe (7 weeks)

Calls out true commitment

Example 3: The "Excuses?" Slide

The Frame:

"Excuses?

Now let's eliminate the excuses...

Is it you don't like to sell?

Too much competition?

What about google?"

Analysis:

Pre-emptive objection handling

Multiple objections addressed

Dismissive tone (these are excuses, not reasons)

The Complete Script Template

Part 1: The Momentum Recap

Before we go any further, I want to check in with you about 

our time together so far.

\[Question 1\]: Thus far, has our time on this \[format\] been 

well spent?

\[Question 2\]: Do you now feel more confident about \[topic\]?

\[Question 3\]: Can you see how even just one or two things I 

showed you today can help you in your \[area\], regardless of 

whether you use it for \[main application\] or somewhere else?

\[Question 4\]: Are you excited to make a go out of this to see 

what wonderful things it opens up for you in \[area/life\]?

Part 2: The DIY Acknowledgment

Now here's what I know about you...

I have absolute confidence that you could take everything we 

covered here today and on your own go out and put it into 

action and get results that you previously thought weren't 

possible.

I am certain just with the information we've covered on this 

\[format\] today, left on your own with your own resources, time 

and effort you can make a go at this in a way most people will 

never be able to.

You're smart. You're capable. You're resourceful.

And if that were your only option it would be a worthy ideal 

to pursue.

Part 3: The Bridge

But what if there were another option.

An option that allowed us to go at this together?

A chance to go deeper than we can in one \[time period\] on one 

\[fast-moving format\] connecting together just this one time.

Part 4: The Responsibility Offer

What if I played an active role in your results?

What if I took a responsibility in your success?

What if I had an obligation to your outcome?

How would that make things different?

Part 5: The Empowerment Offer

Do you think in that situation I could empower you with any 

and all resources at my disposal to stack the deck in your 

favor to make success all but inevitable?

If I had shortcuts and support systems and additional insights 

that go even deeper and more thorough into getting you the 

result you want, desire and quite frankly deserve...

If I had such an option for you, would you love to hear more 

about it and what it could do for you?

Part 6: The Program Introduction

That's why it's with great pleasure that I introduce you to...

\[PROGRAM NAME\]

\[Quick description of what it is\]

This program was created by \[name(s)\], who \[brief credibility\].

\[Specific proof\]: They've helped \[specific number\] students 

achieve \[specific result\] including \[specific dollar amount or 

metric\].

In fact, \[origin story with specific details about humble 

beginnings and transformation\].

\[Current state\]: And they're constantly discovering new methods 

and approaches. Every time I talk with them they're showing me 

something new that's working even better.

\[Community proof\]: They've built a community of \[number\] active 

members who support each other and share best practices.

\[The promise\]: Even if you're starting from zero, even if you've 

never \[done this\] before, I'm confident we can have you achieving 

\[specific result\] in \[time frame\] through \[program name\].

Part 7: The Call to Action

Here's what to do next...

Simply click the \[button/link\] below to \[specific action\].

When you do, you'll \[what happens immediately\].

Plus, as a bonus for joining through this \[format\], you'll 

also get \[special bonuses specific to this audience\].

But you need to act now because \[legitimate scarcity reason\].

Click the button below to get started now.

Final Thoughts

The Core Truth

This framework isn't about manipulation. It's about:

1\. Respecting autonomy while presenting opportunity

2\. Taking responsibility for student success

3\. Providing value first then offering to go deeper

4\. Building trust through proof and transparency

5\. Serving rather than selling

The Implementation Mindset

Wrong Approach:

"How can I get them to buy?"

"What tricks will increase conversion?"

"How do I overcome their objections?"

Right Approach:

"How can I make the decision clear?"

"How do I remove genuine barriers?"

"How do I help them choose what's best for them?"

The Long-Term View

The goal isn't just to make a sale. The goal is to:

1\. Create customers who get results

2\. Build a reputation for delivering value

3\. Generate word-of-mouth referrals

4\. Create long-term relationships

5\. Build a sustainable business

This framework achieves all of that because it's built on a foundation of genuine service.

Conclusion

The Jason Fladlien transition and close framework represents a fundamental shift from traditional "hard

selling" to what might be called "facilitated decision-making."

The Key Insight:

"People don't want to be sold. They want to make informed decisions about opportunities that could

genuinely benefit them."

This framework respects that truth while providing all the information, proof, and support needed to make

that decision confidently.

The Ultimate Goal:

Not to get everyone to buy, but to help everyone choose the path that's right for them—whether that's

going it alone with what they learned on the webinar, or partnering with you to go deeper and achieve

results faster.

When you approach the close from this perspective, selling becomes serving, and conversion becomes

natural.

The 7-Second Transition Test

Can you answer these questions clearly?

1\. What did we cover? (Value delivered)

2\. What can you do alone? (DIY path)

3\. What could we do together? (Partnership option)

4\. Why would that be better? (Unique value)

5\. What's the proof? (Social validation)

6\. What's the next step? (Clear CTA)

7\. Why now? (Urgency/scarcity)

The 5-Minute Close Formula

Minute 1: Momentum recap (3-4 questions) Minute 2: DIY acknowledgment + Bridge Minute 3:

Responsibility/Empowerment offers Minute 4: Program intro with proof Minute 5: Call to action

Red Flags to Avoid

❌  Dismissing concerns as "just excuses"

❌  Creating false urgency

❌  Overpromising results

❌  Comparing to competitors negatively

❌  Making people feel stupid for not buying

❌  Using high-pressure tactics

❌  Being unclear about what they're buying

Green Lights to Embrace

✅  Respecting their capability

✅  Genuine partnership framing

✅  Taking responsibility for outcomes

✅  Specific, verifiable proof

✅  Clear value demonstration

✅  Transparent pricing and terms

✅  Strong guarantee/risk reversal

Final Words

The most important thing to remember:

The close isn't a trick. It's a service.

When you genuinely believe your offer will help people get better results faster, presenting that offer

becomes an act of service, not an act of selling.

Master this framework not to manipulate, but to serve.

Not to extract value, but to create it.

Not to win at their expense, but to create win-win outcomes.

That's when selling becomes satisfying, sustainable, and ultimately, successful.
