OFFER LESSONS: Jason Fladlien's Step 10

The Ultimate Guide to Creating Irresistible Offers

Everything we learned from Jason's presentation and what we can apply immediately

CORE PHILOSOPHY: Every Great Offer Needs THIS Slide

The Big Idea: Your offer slide is THE most important slide in your entire presentation. This is where

everything converges.

The Critical Slide Requirements:

1\. Must be slide-based - Never just verbal

2\. Must show everything - Core offer + all bonuses

3\. Must stack value - Make the total value undeniable

4\. Must have clear CTA - Obvious next step with link

THE OFFER CREATION PROCESS

Think of Everything You COULD Offer

The Brainstorm Phase:

List every single thing you could possibly include

Don't filter yet - just get it all out

Think: training, tools, templates, community, access, coaching, done-for-you

From Slides Examples:

AFFILIORAMA: Training + Software + Templates + Traffic Methods + Community

COPY eCLASS: 6 live sessions + Guarantee + Live feedback

ONE STOP SHOP: Shopify theme + Training + Community + 5 bonuses

Assign Highest Value To...

The 8 Value Criteria (this is gold):

1\. Easy to explain and understand

If you can't explain it in one sentence, it's too complex

Simplicity = perceived value

2\. Demonstrable

Can you show it working?

Can you prove the value visually?

Screenshots \> descriptions

3\. High retail value

What would someone else charge for this?

Reference real market prices

Example: "TWF Prime usually $2,997, but 90 days FREE"

4\. High perceived value

Different from retail value

What does it FEEL worth?

Time saved, problems solved, access gained

5\. Novelty

Is it unique?

Have they seen this before?

"Exclusive," "Never-before-seen," "Only available here"

6\. Objection crushing

Does this bonus eliminate a reason not to buy?

Does it remove risk?

Does it make success more certain?

7\. Time and money cost to deliver

Can you scale it?

Lower delivery cost = can afford to add it

Digital \> Physical

8\. Scale

Can 10 people use it? 100? 10,000?

Recorded content scales infinitely

Live coaching doesn't scale (but adds value)

Right Size It

The Goldilocks Principle:

Too Small:

1-2 items feels incomplete

Lacks perceived value

Easy objection: "That's it?"

Just Right:

3-7 total components (main offer + bonuses)

Fills a slide without overwhelming

Can be consumed/absorbed visually

Too Large:

10+ items feels overwhelming

Decreases perceived value of each

Analysis paralysis kicks in

From the examples:

AFFILIORAMA: 3 main items

PRODUCT eCLASS: 7 sessions (numbered and specific)

LINKEDIN: Training + Mastermind + Guarantee + Placement = 4 components

RAMP ENGINE: Funnel + Live Coaching + Bonuses = 3 core parts

Decide Core vs. Bonus

The Psychology:

CORE = What they're actually buying

The main transformational promise

The thing you MUST deliver

Primary value proposition

BONUSES = Accelerators and insurance

Makes core easier to achieve

Removes obstacles

Adds certainty

Critical Distinction: Your core offer should be strong enough to sell alone. Bonuses should make it a

"no-brainer."

Examples from slides:

AFFILIORAMA:

Core: Jetpack (18 ready-made niches)

Bonuses: Software tools, templates, traffic methods

GENIUS WEBINARS:

Core: 2-day live workshop in Joe's office

Bonus: Shared funnel, coaching, exclusive bonuses

LINKEDIN:

Core: Training program

Bonuses: Media placement, VA service, first deal help

Figure Out Order

The Reveal Sequence Matters:

Method 1: Ascending Value

Start with smallest bonus

Build to core offer

End with highest-value bonus

Creates crescendo effect

Method 2: Descending Value

Lead with biggest/core

Add bonuses that support it

Each bonus removes objection

Creates "and there's more" effect

Method 3: Problem-Solution Pairs

State problem

Show solution (offer component)

Next problem

Next solution

Builds logical necessity

From COPY eCLASS example:

1\. States the offer (6 sessions)

2\. Shows the guarantee (removes risk)

3\. Adds better-than-money-back (increases value)

4\. States the requirement (commitment mechanism)

REAL EXAMPLES BREAKDOWN

Example 1: AFFILIORAMA - The "Ready-Made" Offer

What They Did Right:

Product Name: "Affilo JETPACK"

Clear, memorable

Suggests speed/acceleration

One word easy to remember

Story Element:

"Created by Mark Ling"

7 figures a year online credential

Underground money source positioning

Proven track record

The Makeup:

1\. Core: 18 super high-quality done-for-you niches

Specific number (18, not "many")

Quality qualifier (super high-quality)

Done-for-you (effortless)

2\. How it works:

Website setup shown visually

Autoresponders included

Affiliate products pre-selected

Free gifts ready

3\. Social Proof:

Testimonial slides

Real user results

Visual progress evidence

Key Lesson: Package information/access as a product with a name. "Access to training" is weak. "Affilo

JETPACK" is strong.

Example 2: COPY eCLASS - The "Transformation" Offer

The Offer: "6 live sessions to turn you into a world-class copywriter"

Story/Positioning: "My definition: someone who can write high 'earnings per visitor' sales letters, videos

and email copy 100x faster than the average person"

The Guarantee Structure:

Attend all 6 sessions ✓

Write ONE sales letter with my formula ✓

If you don't feel skills significantly improved...

Not only refund...

But send over $1000 extra

Key Lesson:

1\. Quantify the transformation (100x faster)

2\. Make requirements clear (attend all 6, write 1 letter)

4\. Set the bar (world-class)

Example 3: GENIUS WEBINARS - The "Experience" Offer

What They Sold: Not just information - an EXPERIENCE

Specific location (Joe's office)

Limited spots (70 people)

Live interaction (hands-on)

Premium environment (creative, healthy food, successful people)

Bonus Strategy: "I'm also going to hand you over on a silver platter some amazing 'Rapid Crush, Never

Before Seen' Exclusive bonuses designed specifically for my OMGers who sign up through my link\!"

1\. Scarcity via real limitations (venue size)

2\. Experience \> Information (being there vs learning remotely)

3\. Exclusive bonuses for this path (incentive to buy through you)

4\. Describe the environment (makes it tangible)

OTHER OFFER TACTICS (From The Slides)

Tactic 1: Schedule A Call With Me

The Slide Copy: "Go to this URL and you'll see an online scheduler with my availability. In 30 minutes

or less I'll be able to get a better understanding of what makes sense specifically for you to focus on first

to start seeing the benefits of Gender Intelligence show up immediately"

When To Use:

High-ticket offers

Service-based businesses

Need qualification

Personalization required

Key Elements:

Link to scheduler (remove friction)

Time expectation (30 minutes)

Benefit preview (see benefits immediately)

Personalization angle (specifically for you)

Tactic 2: I Can't Promise (Risk Reversal)

The Slide Copy: "That you'll end up working as a client of ours. However, if we determine after that call

that it makes sense to proceed, then we can cross that bridge when we come to it. My first and foremost

goal though is to help you truly start to enjoy the benefits that a blended, gender intelligent company

provides"

The Psychology:

Removes pressure

Consultation vs sales call framing

Priority on their benefit

Permission to say no

When To Use:

Skeptical audiences

Premium services

Multiple-touch sales processes

Trust-building required

Tactic 3: Free Book (Lead Magnet to Offer)

The Example (One To Many book): "Thanks for listening on the zoom webinar\! We have a special gift

to share with you..."

The Book Promise: "The Secrets To Creating Webinars That Impact Your Audience - And Your Bottom

Line\!"

"Inside Jason Fladlien's book, One To Many, you'll discover the formula and methodology that he has

used to generate $100 million in sales using a service in advance of sales webinar structure that is much

more valuable to the audience"

The CTA: "sign up below to get the eBook download for free\!"

Then The Scheduler: "If you'd like more information, you can schedule a free consultation right here:"

Key Elements:

Thank + gift framing

Specific promise (not vague)

Proof element ($100M in sales)

Free removes barrier

Then offer consultation

Tactic 4: Presentation (Most Important\!)

The Slide: \[Image of person holding flashlight/presentation tool\]

Text: "MOST IMPORTANT\!" "PRESENTATION"

The Lesson: Before any of these tactics work, your presentation must:

Build desire

Create urgency

Establish authority

Demonstrate value

Make the offer inevitable

The Sequence: Content → Desire → Offer

Not: Offer → Hope they buy

THE ULTIMATE OFFER FORMULA

The Essential Components

1\. Product Name

One word if possible

Suggests transformation/benefit

Easy to remember and repeat

2\. Story

Why this exists

Who created it

What makes them qualified

Personal connection/relatability

3\. Makeup (The Stack)

Core offer (clearly labeled)

Supporting bonuses (3-6 items)

Each bonus has its own value

Visual presentation

4\. Value Anchoring

"This would normally cost..."

"Compared to \[competitor\] at $X..."

"The retail value is..."

5\. Your Price

Simple (one or two options max)

Payment plans if applicable

Urgency element

6\. Guarantee

Remove all risk

Better-than-money-back when possible

Clear requirements

7\. CTA

Specific URL

What happens next

Time limitation

WHAT WE CAN APPLY TO OUR BUSINESS

Immediate Actions

1\. Name Your Offers

Don't: "Get access to our client acquisition training"

Do: "The Client Acquisition Accelerator"

Or: "The Acquisition Engine"

Or: "Deal Flow System"

2\. Stack Your Value Visually

Create one slide that shows:

Main offer (name + brief description)

Bonus 1 (value: $X)

Bonus 2 (value: $X)

Bonus 3 (value: $X)

Total value: $XX,XXX

Your investment: $X,XXX

3\. Use The 8-Point Value Checklist

For every component ask:

1\. Easy to explain? ✓/✗

2\. Demonstrable? ✓/✗

3\. High retail value? ✓/✗

4\. High perceived value? ✓/✗

5\. Novel? ✓/✗

6\. Objection-crushing? ✓/✗

7\. Low delivery cost? ✓/✗

8\. Scalable? ✓/✗

If less than 6 checkmarks, reconsider that component.

4\. Create Module/Session Numbers

Instead of: "You'll learn how to find and close deals"

Use: "7 Sessions covering the entire client acquisition process"

Then list them:

Session 1: Target Market Selection

Session 2: Outreach System Setup

Session 3: First Contact Templates

etc.

Your current: "Money-back guarantee"

Upgrade to: "Attend all sessions, implement the systems, and if you don't land at least one client in 90 

days, we'll not only refund your investment but also do the outreach FOR you for the next 30 days"

6\. Build In Social Proof

Don't: Generic testimonials

Do: Specific results with names

"John landed his first $15K client in week 3"

"Sarah closed 2 deals worth $8K each in her first month"

Include photos, numbers, screenshots

7\. Create "Done With You" Options

Instead of: Only teaching

Add tier: "Plus we'll review your first 10 outreach messages and provide feedback"

Premium tier: "We'll help you close your first deal"

8\. Use The "Normally/But/For You" Pattern

"Normally this would cost $10,000 if you hired an agency.

But because \[reason: beta, partnership, leverage, etc.\],

For you, it's just \[price\] + \[incentive structure\]"

9\. Add Exclusive Bonuses

When promoting others' products:

"Plus when you sign up through my link, you'll get:

Bonus 1 (my exclusive training)

Bonus 2 (my template library)

Bonus 3 (15-minute strategy call with me)"

10\. Leverage Specificity

Instead of: "We've helped many clients"

Use: "We've generated $847,392 for our clients using this exact system"

Instead of: "Proven system"

Use: "The same 5-step system that landed us 47 clients in the last 90 days"

Strategic Framework

For Our Black Friday Launch:

Main Offer: \[Name it something catchy\]

The Black Friday Client Surge System

The Q4 Deal Accelerator

The Holiday Revenue Rush

Core:

Complete offer creation workshop (VSL)

Funnel buildout (registration + sales pages)

Email sequence (pre + post webinar)

Bonuses:

Bonus 1: "Past Customer Reactivation Templates" (Value: $2,000)

Proven email sequences

Subject line library

Segmentation strategy

Bonus 2: "VIP Upgrade Tier Framework" (Value: $1,500)

Positioning guide

Pricing psychology

Page templates

Bonus 3: "First 30 Days Roadmap" (Value: $1,000)

Week-by-week implementation guide

Daily KPI tracking sheet

Troubleshooting checklist

Total Value: $18,000 (agency pricing) Your Investment: 15% revenue share (no upfront cost)

Guarantee: "If we don't help you generate at least $100K from this launch, you pay nothing. We only

win when you win."

MENTAL MODELS TO REMEMBER

1\. The Perception Formula

Value = (Dream Outcome × Perceived Likelihood) / (Time Delay × Effort & Sacrifice)

Every component you add should:

Increase dream outcome

Increase perceived likelihood

Decrease time delay

Decrease effort required

2\. The Irresistible Offer Test

If someone sees your offer and thinks:

"This is expensive" = Offer problem

"This is cheap" = You may have won

"This is a no-brainer" = You definitely won

3\. The Risk Reversal Hierarchy

Level 0: No guarantee Level 1: Money-back guarantee Level 2: Extended money-back (60-90 days)

Level 3: Better-than-money-back (+bonus if not satisfied) Level 4: We'll do it for you if it doesn't work

Level 5: We'll refund you AND pay you

Work toward level 3+ whenever possible.

4\. The Simplicity Principle

Each offer revision should ask:

Can I say this with fewer words?

Can I show this instead of saying it?

Can I remove a component without losing impact?

Can I combine two components into one?

Jason's evolution: 6 mechanisms → 5 → 4 → 3

Your offer should follow the same path.

5\. The Demonstrability Rule

Low Demonstrability:

"Access to our knowledge"

"Coaching and support"

"Expert guidance"

High Demonstrability:

"6 recorded workshops you can watch today"

"3 live coaching calls per month (scheduled: \[dates\])"

"Personal review of your first 5 campaigns"

THE OFFER SLIDE TEMPLATE

Structure:

\[COMPELLING HEADLINE - THE OFFER NAME\]

THE \[CORE THING\]:

→ What it is (1 line)

→ What's included (3-5 bullets)

→ What you'll achieve (outcome)

BONUS \#1: \[NAME\] (Value: $X,XXX)

→ What it is

→ Why it matters

BONUS \#2: \[NAME\] (Value: $X,XXX)

→ What it is

→ Why it matters

BONUS \#3: \[NAME\] (Value: $X,XXX)

→ What it is

→ Why it matters

TOTAL VALUE: $XX,XXX

YOUR INVESTMENT: $X,XXX

\[or: Performance-based: X% of results\]

GUARANTEE:

\[Your specific risk-reversal promise\]

→ CLICK HERE TO \[ACTION\]: \[YourURL.com\]

\[Time limitation: Expires \[date\] or Only X spots\]

FINAL LESSONS

What Jason Does Differently

1\. Everything is named

Not "training," but "JETPACK"

Not "community," but "One Stop Shop Secret Group"

Not "guarantee," but "Better Than Money Back Promise"

2\. Everything is numbered

Not "several sessions," but "6 live workshops"

Not "many bonuses," but "5 exclusive gifts"

Not "lots of value," but "$13,082.45 total value"

3\. Everything is visual

Not described, but shown

Screenshots \> text

Diagrams \> paragraphs

Before/after images

4\. Everything is specific

Not "learn Facebook ads," but "the 7-step Facebook ad system that generated $2.3M"

Not "get support," but "30-day guaranteed response time to every question"

Not "proven system," but "the system 847 students have used to generate $14.2M"

5\. Everything is reframed as investment

Not "cost," but "investment"

Not "price," but "your investment"

Not "pay," but "invest"

The Universal Truth

You're not selling a product. You're not selling a service. You're selling a TRANSFORMATION.

Your offer structure should make the transformation:

Believable (proof)

Achievable (broken down)

Fast (time compression)

Certain (risk removal)

Valuable (high ROI)

Every component, every bonus, every word should reinforce this.

Does it have a name?

Is the value stacked visually?

Are components numbered and specific?

Is there proof integrated?

2\. Run the 8-point value test

On your core offer

On each bonus

Remove or improve low-scoring items

3\. Create your offer slide

Use the template above

Make it visual

Stack the value clearly

Add guarantee

4\. Write your guarantee

Move up one level in the hierarchy

Make it specific and time-bound

Remove all risk from buyer

5\. Simplify your CTA

One clear action

Simple URL

Urgency element

Next-step clarity

This Month:

1\. Name all your offers

2\. Create visual slides for each

3\. Record testimonials with specifics

4\. Document your results/proof

5\. Test new guarantees

6\. A/B test offer presentations

CONCLUSION: The Offer Slide Changes Everything

Everything else in the webinar/presentation/pitch leads to this moment.

If your offer slide is:

Unclear → They won't buy

Weak → They'll consider but won't act

Overwhelming → Analysis paralysis

Perfect → They can't say no

Jason's offer slides are perfect because:

1\. You understand what you're getting (clarity)

2\. You see it's worth more than you're paying (value)

3\. You believe you'll achieve the outcome (proof)

4\. You feel no risk (guarantee)

5\. You must act now (urgency)

That's the model. That's the standard. That's what we build toward.

Document created: \[Date\] Review this monthly and before every major launch The principles don't

change, but your execution should constantly improve

OFFER LESSONS - Jason Fladlien's Step 11: Price

Core Philosophy

"NO MATTER THE PRICE, IT'S ALWAYS A DEAL\!"

This is the ultimate goal of every price presentation. The customer should feel they're getting incredible

value regardless of the actual investment amount.

THE 7 ESSENTIAL ELEMENTS OF PRICE PRESENTATION

1\. PREFRAME

What it is: Setting the context for why this offer exists and why it's priced this way.

Jason's Example:

"Chuck is basically in 'marketshare acquisition mode' right now"

"He's practically taking customers 'at a loss' just to shave years off his climb to the top of the email

marketing service provider game"

"As such, he's allowing people (YOU\!) in to his platform at a deal so great it's ridiculous"

Key Lessons:

Explain the business strategy behind the pricing

Make it clear this isn't normal pricing

Position it as a strategic opportunity, not desperation

The preframe reduces price resistance before you even mention numbers

Application to Our Business:

Before revealing price, explain WHY we can offer this deal

Use strategic positioning (market entry, partnership, beta testing, limited capacity)

Make it clear this pricing won't last forever

Frame it as them helping us achieve a bigger goal (market share, testimonials, case studies)

2\. DIY VS INVEST

What it is: Show what it would cost them to create the solution themselves.

Jason's Breakdown:

5 "year long" email follow ups: $2,250

15 "free reports": $3,000

Theme + year hosting: $150

8 weeks time: $2,500

DIY total cost: $7,900

Key Lessons:

Break down every component they'd need

Include time costs (their hourly rate × time needed)

Include learning curve costs

Include tools/software/resources needed

Make the DIY path look overwhelming and expensive

Application to Our Business:

List every deliverable we provide

Calculate market rate for each component

Include opportunity cost (what they could earn with their time instead)

Include the "trial and error" tax they'd pay learning

Show total DIY cost is 5-20x your asking price

Formula:

DIY Cost = (Hourly Rate × Time) + Tools + Resources + Learning Curve + Mistakes

3\. PERSONAL COST

What it is: Reveal what YOU invested to create this solution.

Jason's Example:

Crosses out $10,000

Crosses out $25,000

Crosses out $50,000

Crosses out $100,000

Lands at $159,134.92+

Key Lessons:

Show your personal investment was massive

Cross out lower numbers to build tension

Land on a specific, believable number (not round)

The "+" suggests ongoing investment

This creates massive value perception

Application to Our Business:

Calculate actual investment:

Years of experience (value of time)

Courses/training purchased

Failed attempts/tests

Tools and software

Coaching/mentorship

Travel to events

Books, resources

Present with specificity ($159,134.92 not $150,000)

Include ongoing costs

Your Story Formula:

"What do you suppose it cost just in programmers, engineers, developers, and designers 

to PERFECT \[solution\] over the course of the last several years?"

4\. ACTUAL RETAIL PRICES

What it is: Show what competitors or similar solutions charge.

Jason's Examples:

Genius Webinars: $10,000 for a 2-day event (and you have to qualify)

Various products at $3,495, $999.95, etc.

Key Lessons:

Reference real market prices for similar solutions

Show premium positioning of alternatives

Demonstrate your offer is dramatically underpriced

Use recognizable brands/authorities when possible

Application to Our Business:

Research 3-5 comparable offers

Note their prices

Highlight what they DON'T include that we do

Position our offer as "normally this would cost..."

5\. SCARCITY

What it is: Create urgency through legitimate limitations.

Jason's Example - AMZ Launchpad:

"Last time we launched this was November 3rd, 2016"

"I gave the URL to sign up on the webinar at 1:15pm"

"By 1:21 we were sold out"

"And the only people who saw it were Fast Trackers"

Types of Scarcity:

Time-based: Offer expires

Quantity-based: Limited spots (300 spots, 70 spots, etc.)

Qualification-based: Only for webinar attendees

Historical: Show past sellouts

Key Lessons:

Scarcity must be REAL

Show proof (chat logs, timestamps)

Create FOMO with past examples

Multiple layers of scarcity work better

Application to Our Business: Legitimate scarcity sources:

Limited onboarding capacity

Beta/founding member pricing

Seasonal/launch pricing

Partnership limits

Your time constraints

Software/platform limitations

Scarcity Stack:

1\. Limited to webinar attendees only

2\. Limited to X spots total

3\. Expires at end of \[timeframe\]

4\. Price increases after \[date\]

5\. Bonuses expire after \[timeframe\]

6\. REASON WHY

What it is: Give an emotional, personal reason for the great deal.

Jason's Formula:

"For YOU\!"

"Because you've taken the time to be on this webinar today..."

"To better yourself..."

"I'm going to give the best deal possible"

From Other Examples: LinkedInception:

"Well, to pay a PR firm (liked I tried to do) cost me $6,000 for three months and didn't even get into

any of the 4 major media you get access to"

Key Lessons:

Make it personal

Acknowledge their investment (time, attention, action)

Appeal to their self-improvement journey

Make them feel special/chosen

Give a strategic business reason too

Application to Our Business:

"Because you've \[specific action they took\]:

\- Attended this training

\- Been with us since \[time\]

\- Took action on \[thing\]

\- Are committed to \[outcome\]

I want to make sure you succeed, which is why..."

7\. JUXTAPOSITION

What it is: Show multiple pricing tiers to make target price look better.

Jason's Example - Comparison Tables:

Option 1: Feature-Based Tiers

STARTER ($97) vs PRO ($247) vs ULTIMATE ($497)

\[Shows features included at each level\]

\[Shows savings: $370.85 vs $835.75 vs $3,206.90\]

Option 2: Different Names

ROOKIE ($97) vs PRO ($297) vs ALL STAR ($497)

Key Lessons:

Create 3 tiers minimum

Middle tier is your target (most will choose this)

High tier makes middle look reasonable

Low tier exists to make others look valuable

Show "savings" or "value" in yellow/highlighted

Use color psychology (yellow = savings, green = best value)

Pricing Psychology:

Decoy Effect: Low tier makes middle tier look much better

Anchoring: High tier makes everything else seem cheap

Compromise Effect: People naturally gravitate to middle option

Application to Our Business:

3-Tier Structure:

TIER 1: Basic/DIY ($X)

\- Core product only

\- Self-implementation

\- Email support

TIER 2: Done-With-You ($3X) ← TARGET TIER

\- Everything in Tier 1

\- Implementation support

\- Group coaching

\- Templates/frameworks

\- Priority support

TIER 3: Done-For-You ($5-10X)

\- Everything in Tier 2

\- 1-on-1 implementation

\- Custom strategy

\- VIP access

\- Guaranteed results

Pricing Multiples:

If base is $500, tiers might be: $500 / $1,500 / $5,000

Or: $1,000 / $3,000 / $10,000

Middle tier typically 3-4x base tier

Top tier typically 5-10x base tier

ANCHORING TECHNIQUE

What it is: Establish a high reference point before revealing your price.

The Progression:

1\. DIY Cost: $7,900

2\. Your Personal Investment: $159,134.92+

3\. Comparable Market Prices: $10,000 - $25,000

4\. "What would be a fair investment for something like this?"

5\. Let them think $2,995 or $5,000

6\. Reveal actual price: $4,995 → $997

Key Lessons:

Every number before your price is an anchor

Work from high to low

Each anchor resets their expectations

By the time you reveal price, anything under $5,000 feels like a steal

Anchoring Sequence:

1\. DIY Path: $7,900

2\. Your Investment: $159,134.92+

3\. Market Comparisons: $10,000+

4\. "Bare Minimum" Price: $2,995

5\. "Magical Guru Big Launch" Price: $1,995

6\. Actual Public Price: $997

7\. Special Price Today: $497

SPECIFIC PRODUCT EXAMPLES & LESSONS

Example 1: Affiliorama

Presentation:

"Would you like to get access to this whole system..."

"Including our insider advantage resources to exploit this opportunity..."

"And get it at a special discount price that I was able to snag for you?"

Then:

DIY: $7,900

Your investment: $2,995 bare minimum

Big launch price: $1,995

Special Rapid Crush affiliate link: $747

Lessons:

Build value first

Show DIY cost

Show what YOU would charge

Show what gurus charge

Reveal special deal

Price drops from $7,900 → $747 (90% off feeling)

Example 2: CopyEClass

Presentation:

"I am giving you a MILLION-dollar education"

"So a $2,000 investment on your part would be a GREAT DEAL\!"

Then:

"However, it's not $2,000"

"It's $997 which is a smoking hot deal for you (since we haven't even discussed the bonuses yet\!)"

Special price: $497 one payment

Lessons:

State value in millions

Anchor at $2,000

Reveal $997 (50% of anchor)

Drop to $497 (25% of anchor)

Mention bonuses are still coming

Example 3: Genius Webinars

Presentation: Multiple reveals showing market context:

1\. "Genius X Members actively seek me out and hire me at obscene rates"

2\. "Putting this in front of Joe's audience, I know there is a long line of savvy entrepreneurs we'll reach

that won't bat an eye if the investment was $20,000"

3\. "Joe's two day annual event is a $10,000 investment and even then, just cutting a check doesn't get

you in the room - you have to qualify"

4\. "...is also a 2 day event so the same price tag - $10,000 - makes sense just from a supply and demand

perspective..."

5\. "That's why on the surface it might not make sense what the investment is for you to join Genius

Webinars"

Then reveals: $4,995 (with 70 limited spots)

Lessons:

Stack multiple high anchors

Use social proof of high prices

Compare to known expensive events

Build confusion about price

"Surprise" with lower price

Add scarcity (70 spots)

Example 4: Launchpad (Amazon)

Presentation:

"As a fast-tracker, you get first dibs"

"Access to this tool is $1000/mo with a 12 month commitment (or $9995 for one year + get a killer

bonus\!)"

"If this seems too much to you, don't buy it"

"Because there are plenty ASM Masters who would make many, many times this month in and month

out"

Then:

Public price: $1,000/month ($12,000/year) or $9,995/year

Fast tracker deal: Only 300 spots available

Shows urgent chat logs of people buying

Lessons:

High monthly price anchors annual price

Offer both payment options

Qualify the buyer ("if this seems too much, don't buy it")

Show it's only for serious players

Extreme scarcity (300 spots)

Real-time social proof

Example 5: OMG Project Juggernaut

Unique Approach: Uses celebrity analogies

Presentation:

"If you could work with Jeff Bezos and have him give you ALL his methods, strategies, techniques

and tools..."

"What would you pay for that opportunity?"

Similar questions for Warren Buffett (investing) and Richard Branson (branding)

"What Jeff Bezos is to eCommerce... What Warren Buffet is to investing... What Richard Branson is

to branding..."

"That's what Mike, Greg, & Mike are to Free Traffic. No joke."

Then reveals:

Investment similar to their Amazon mastermind ($25,000/year)

Or Amazon Cabo Retreats ($12,000 for 3 days)

Or Amazon software ($10,000/year)

Actual price: $12,900 is an "incredible deal" for the right person, $9,900 is "fantastic," $6,995 is

"beautiful"

Lessons:

Use celebrity/authority comparisons

Build credibility through analogy

Multiple high price anchors

Segment pricing based on buyer readiness

Qualify heavily ("for the right person")

Example 6: One Stop Shop (Ezra Firestone)

Approach: Show personal investment in creating the solution

Presentation:

"What's it worth to you to get the exact template, step-by-step training, and support of someone like

Ezra who has built several different 7 figure brands in 7 years?"

"Who is also a proven teacher and the most respected guy in the field when it comes to eCommerce"

"What do you suppose it cost Ezra just in programmers, engineers, developers, and designers to

perfect the Proven Profitable Theme over the course of the last several years?"

Shows escalating crossed-out investment numbers:

$10,000

$25,000

$50,000

$100,000

$159,134.92+

Then asks:

"What do you suppose Ezra spent in advertising to split test, heat map, optimize, and finalize the One

Stop Shop to work for digital, physical, and across all media and industries?"

$100,000

$250,000

$500,000

$1,000,000 (in red)

$1,688,097.11+

Value stack continues:

Development costs ($159,134.92+)

Optimization costs ($1,688,097.11+)

Plus step-by-step training (priceless)

Plus world class support (priceless)

Final offer:

"For the right person, $12,900 is an incredible deal"

"For the 'kind of' right person $9,900 is a fantastic deal"

"For the 'wrong' person $6,995 is a beautiful deal"

But actually:

"It's $4,995/yr to get everything you need to succeed as fast as possible"

"Even better, it's just $3,995 for a limited time if you get in on our 'first time offered to the public'

launch and just $997 per year after\!"

Lessons:

Show MASSIVE personal investment

Use very specific numbers (not round)

Cross out progressively higher numbers

Build to huge total

Create multiple "right person" price points

Dramatically discount from highest anchor

Renewable annual pricing model

Special founding member pricing

Example 7: Wholesale Formula

Approach: Binary decision framework

Question: "What's your investment to get started right now?"

Context Setting:

"Is it around what we charged for our Amazon mastermind, which was $25,000 a year and sold out

each and every time we offered it?"

"Or is it closer to our Amazon Cabo Retreats where Amazon sellers paid $12,000 for a three day

event?"

"Or is it more inline with our Amazon software, which goes for $10,000 per year?"

Answer: "Should require such an investment because it's worth many many times more than $10k, $12k

or even $25k yet if you act for the short window it will be open for this year."

Actual prices:

$874 TODAY then 3x more monthly installments of the same

OR $2997 and get a 30-day money back guarantee

Special note: "I don't care which option you pick, just that you get in right now because the timing

couldn't be better, but The Wholesale Formula is only available for a limited time so act now"

Lessons:

Frame as simple choice

Use multiple high anchors ($25k, $12k, $10k)

Justify high value

Offer payment plan option

Offer lump sum discount

Add guarantee to lump sum

Strong urgency on availability

Example 8: Product eClass

Presentation approach:

"Tons of Product eClass members have made 5+ figures, so a price tag of $9997.00 would be fair"

"I know many big famous gurus who charge more and give less"

Reality check:

"$999.95 is what Product eClass sells for to the public each and every day"

Special offer:

"For you? $499 one time —OR— $194 three monthly installments"

"OFFER EXPIRES SHORTLY\!"

Lessons:

Use student results as anchor

Compare to competitors

Show public price

Dramatic discount (50% off)

Payment plan option available

Create urgency with expiration

Unique context: For people who already have physical products

Presentation:

"Sadly though... Not everyone is going to be able to afford this, even though anybody with a single

physical product and half a brain will realize how essential this is to your business"

"Yet in business, the true best - the most cutting edge, most successful, smartest 'actually doing this'

sellers aren't easy to get access to and they don't come cheap because they can perform the greatest

magic trick in the world..."

"They can take a dollar from you and turn it into $10. Turn $10 into a $100. Turn $100 into

$100,000"

Value framework:

"What's Ramp Engine worth?"

"If we're being ultra-conservative I'll tell you what it's easily worth to you..."

"Take a look at the total revenue you've generated since since you started selling physical products"

"With this, you would've made AT LEAST double what you did - for even the most up and coming

sellers, that's easily tens of thousands of dollars more of money free and clear that you missed out

on\!"

Pricing:

"We've sold thousands of units for $3,495"

"If you already have a physical product, Ramp Engine is as valuable to you as ASM/OPP any day of

the week"

"In fact, that's the 'public' price we're going to roll this out at after we complete this training with our

Rapid Crush customers first plus a $97/month continuity to keep your funnels active"

Special deal:

"This is a world first. It's never been seen before. It's brand new."

"Here's your chance not only to get first mover advantage... but you also are going to get the best deal

we'll ever make for Ramp Engine"

"one installment $2495 zero continuity payments"

Lessons:

Pre-qualify heavily ("not everyone can afford this")

Position as essential for serious players

Use unique value calculation (2x their past revenue)

Show public price with continuity

Remove continuity for founding members

First-mover advantage positioning

One-time opportunity framing

Example 10: Prelaunch Webinar (Operation Physical Products)

Context: Selling to existing customers/community

Presentation:

"We will offer it for $3499.95"

"You never need it. You already have ASM and you will have the same level of access to our bonus

package as you do today."

"However, if you believe you'll find value in it, I am going to offer it to you for a once in a lifetime

deal of..."

Important framing:

any of your existing perks."

"This is a pre-release, one time only opportunity. Access will be granted to you at no later than the

8th of February."

"Modules will be released over time, though not necessary as long as 7 days apart."

"All sales are final."

Two comparison tables shown:

1\. STARTER ($97) vs PRO ($247) vs ULTIMATE ($497)

2\. Different format: Shows retail prices and savings

Special price: Just $349 for ASM Masters (at mybestbonus.com/opp)

Expires Monday, Jan 18th

Additional context slides:

"Chuck is basically in 'marketshare acquisition mode' right now"

"That deal goes away very, very soon"

"Hence, why I had to switch from watching on the sidelines..."

"To letting you know about this today..."

Lessons:

Acknowledge they don't "need" it

Frame as optional value-add

Deeply discount for existing customers

Set clear expectations (pre-release, module delivery)

Make sales final

Show respect for relationship ("only invest if you'll get value")

Use market share strategy as reason why

Extreme urgency

PAYMENT STRUCTURE STRATEGIES

1\. One-Time vs Payment Plan

Jason's typical structure:

One-time payment: Lower total cost, add guarantee

Payment plan: Slightly higher total, easier commitment

Examples:

Wholesale Formula: $2,997 vs $874 x 4 ($3,496)

Product eClass: $499 vs $194 x 3 ($582)

Why offer both:

Lowers barrier to entry

Payment plans often have higher completion rates

One-time payment appeals to "all-in" buyers

Both options increase total conversions

2\. Annual/Continuity Pricing

Ramp Engine example:

Public: $3,495 + $97/month ongoing

Special: $2,495 one-time (zero continuity)

Continuity adds long-term value perception

Removing continuity = powerful incentive

Founding member pricing = one-time opportunity

THE COMPLETE PRICE PRESENTATION SCRIPT STRUCTURE

Here's how to put it all together:

Part 1: BUILD VALUE (Before mentioning price)

1\. Remind them of the problem/opportunity

2\. Recap the solution and its benefits

3\. Show social proof (results, testimonials)

4\. Demonstrate authority/credibility

Part 2: PREFRAME THE PRICE

"So let's talk about investment... but before I do, 

I want to give you some context..."

\[Insert strategic reason for pricing\]

Part 3: DIY VS INVEST

"If you wanted to create this yourself, here's what it would cost you:

\- Component 1: $X

\- Component 2: $X

\- Component 3: $X

\- Your time: $X

Total DIY Cost: $X"

Part 4: PERSONAL INVESTMENT

"What do you think I've invested to create this?

\[Cross out numbers\]

I've personally invested $X to make this work"

Part 5: MARKET COMPARISONS

"Similar solutions in the market charge:

\- Competitor A: $X

\- Competitor B: $X

\- Industry standard: $X"

Part 6: ANCHORING

"So what would be fair for something like this?

$X would be reasonable...

Actually, we could charge $X and it would be a bargain...

The 'gurus' would charge $X for this..."

Part 7: SCARCITY SETUP

"Now, we can't offer this to everyone...

\[Explain limitation\]

So this is only available to \[qualifier\]

And only until \[deadline\]"

Part 8: REASON WHY

"But here's why I'm making this available to you:

\[Personal reason\]

\[Strategic reason\]

Because you \[their action\], I want to..."

Part 9: THE REVEAL

"So normally this would be $X...

But for \[reason\], we're offering it for:

\[Show tiered options\]

Basic: $X

Standard: $X (Most Popular)

Premium: $X

\[Or single option with payment choices\]

$X one-time

OR

$X x 3 payments"

Part 10: HANDLE OBJECTIONS

"Now, I know what some of you might be thinking...

\[Address common objections\]

But remember \[value recap\]"

Part 11: URGENCY & CTA

"This offer expires \[when\]

Only \[X\] spots available

Bonuses expire \[when\]

Click the button below to \[action\]"

PSYCHOLOGICAL PRINCIPLES IN ACTION

1\. Price Anchoring

Definition: The first number you hear becomes the reference point for all following numbers.

How Jason uses it:

Start with DIY cost ($7,900)

Show personal investment ($159,134)

Show market prices ($10,000 - $25,000)

Reveal "normal" price ($2,997)

Drop to actual price ($497)

By the time you see $497, it feels like practically free.

2\. Loss Aversion

Definition: People fear losing something more than they desire gaining something.

How Jason uses it:

"This price goes away..."

"Only X spots left..."

"You'll never see this price again..."

"The bonuses expire..."

Creates fear of missing out (FOMO).

3\. Social Proof

How Jason uses it:

Shows chat logs of people buying in real-time

References sold-out past launches

Mentions community members who already have it

"Fast Trackers" insider status

4\. Authority

How Jason uses it:

References his own high-dollar offers

Mentions being hired at "obscene rates"

Compares himself to industry leaders

Shows media appearances

References customer success stories

5\. Reciprocity

How Jason uses it:

Acknowledges their time investment

Thanks them for being on webinar

Positions offer as giving back

"Because you showed up..."

6\. Contrast Principle

Definition: Things appear different based on what you compare them to.

How Jason uses it:

$159,134 investment vs $497 price = incredible value

$10,000 competitor price vs $497 = amazing deal

$7,900 DIY cost vs $497 = total no-brainer

7\. Decoy Effect

Definition: Adding a third option makes the middle option more attractive.

How Jason uses it:

Basic: $97 (limited features)

Standard: $297 (best value) ← Target

Premium: $497 (full features)

Most people choose Standard because:

It's not the cheapest (quality concerns)

It's not the most expensive (budget concerns)

It seems like the "smart" middle ground

WHAT YOU CAN STEAL FOR YOUR BUSINESS

1\. The Value Ladder Formula

Your Price: $497

\= 1/15th of DIY Cost ($7,900)

\= 1/320th of Your Investment ($159,134)

\= 1/20th of Competitor Price ($10,000)

This creates a 20:1 to 320:1 perceived value ratio

Your Action: Calculate these ratios for your offer.

2\. The Tiered Pricing Template

BASIC TIER (Anchor low)

\- Core product only

\- Self-implementation

\- Price: $X

STANDARD TIER (Target - most attractive)

\- Everything in Basic

\- Implementation support

\- Group coaching

\- Bonuses

\- Price: $3X (This is what you want most people to buy)

PREMIUM TIER (Anchor high)

\- Everything in Standard

\- 1-on-1 support

\- Custom strategy

\- VIP access

\- Price: $10X

Your Action: Create your 3 tiers using this structure.

3\. The Scarcity Stack

Layer 1: Time limit (expires in X days)

Layer 2: Quantity limit (only X spots)

Layer 3: Qualifier limit (only for webinar attendees)

Layer 4: Bonus limit (bonuses expire before main offer)

Layer 5: Price increase coming (will be $X after this)

Your Action: Implement at least 3 layers of legitimate scarcity.

4\. The "Because" Framework

Research shows: Giving a reason (any reason) dramatically increases compliance.

Jason's reasons why:

"Because you showed up today..."

"Because you're committed to bettering yourself..."

"Because we're in market-share acquisition mode..."

"Because this is a first-time-ever opportunity..."

"Because we're beta testing..."

Your Action: List 5 "because" statements for your offer.

5\. The Authority Stack

Before revealing price, establish authority:

1\. Personal results ("I've generated $X...")

2\. Student results ("Our members have achieved...")

3\. Industry recognition ("As featured on...")

4\. Comparison to others ("What \[celebrity\] is to \[field\], I am to \[your field\]")

5\. Investment made ("I've spent $X developing this...")

Your Action: Document your authority in all 5 areas.

6\. The Objection Pre-Handle

Common objections and how Jason addresses them:

"It's too expensive"

Pre-handled by showing DIY cost, market prices, personal investment

"If this seems too much, don't buy it - this is for serious players"

"I need to think about it"

Pre-handled with scarcity (expires soon, limited spots)

"This won't be available for long"

"What if it doesn't work?"

Pre-handled with guarantee, social proof, results

"30-day money back guarantee"

"I don't have time"

Pre-handled by showing time cost of DIY approach

"This saves you 8 weeks of work"

Your Action: List your top 5 objections and how you'll pre-handle each.

7\. The Payment Psychology

Structure options to maximize conversions:

Option A: $997 one-time (best value)

\- Add guarantee here

\- Frame as "all-in" commitment

\- 10-15% typically choose this

Option B: $347 x 3 payments (most accessible)

\- Total: $1,041 (4% more than one-time)

\- Makes it feel affordable

\- 85-90% typically choose this

Why this works:

More people buy with payment plan

You make slightly more on payment plan

Both options increase total conversions vs single option

Your Action: Create both payment options for your offer.

8\. The Bonus Stack Strategy

DON'T mention bonuses during price reveal.

Jason's approach:

"And we haven't even discussed the bonuses yet\!"

Saves bonuses for after the price

Makes the price seem even better in hindsight

Bonus Structure:

Core Offer: $497

BONUS \#1: \[Valued at $X\]

BONUS \#2: \[Valued at $X\]

BONUS \#3: \[Valued at $X\]

Total Value: $X

Your Price Today: $497

Your Action: Create 3-5 high-value bonuses with specific valuations.

ADVANCED TACTICS

1\. The Multi-Stage Price Reveal

Don't reveal price all at once. Build to it:

Stage 1: Build value (10-15 minutes)

Stage 2: Show DIY cost

Stage 3: Show your investment

Stage 4: Show market prices

Stage 5: Anchor high ("This could easily be $5k...")

Stage 6: Reveal "normal" price ("Typically $2,997...")

Stage 7: Reveal special price ("Today only $497...")

Stage 8: Show payment options

Stage 9: Add bonuses

Stage 10: Add scarcity/urgency

This progression takes 20-30 minutes total, but every step makes the final price feel better.

2\. The Qualification Close

Make them qualify for the offer:

"This isn't for everyone. This is only for people who:

\[Qualifier 1\]

\[Qualifier 2\]

\[Qualifier 3\]

If that's you, here's what you need to do..."

Why it works:

People want what they can't have

Qualifying yourself in = self-selection

Reduces refunds (they chose to be there)

3\. The "Wrong Person" Pricing

From OMG Project Juggernaut:

"For the right person, $12,900 is an incredible deal

For the 'kind of' right person, $9,900 is a fantastic deal

For the 'wrong' person, $6,995 is a beautiful deal"

Then reveals: Actually $3,995 (or $995 founding member)

Why it works:

Everyone identifies as at least "wrong person"

$6,995 becomes the floor

Anything less than that is amazing

Creates multiple anchors simultaneously

4\. The Future Pace

Show them what happens after they buy:

"Once you click that button:

1\. You'll immediately get access to \[X\]

2\. Within 24 hours you'll receive \[Y\]

3\. By next week you'll be able to \[Z\]

4\. Within 30 days you'll have \[result\]"

Why it works:

Makes the purchase feel real

Shows clear next steps

Reduces uncertainty

Increases confidence in decision

5\. The Takeaway

Create urgency by taking something away:

"Now, I need to be honest with you...

This offer is going away soon because \[reason\].

After \[date/time\], you'll still be able to get \[product\] but:

The price will be $X (higher)

These bonuses won't be included

The payment plan won't be available

\[Other advantage removed\]"

Why it works:

Creates real urgency

Shows specific consequences of waiting

Multiple points of loss

Makes decision obvious (act now)

THE MASTER CHECKLIST

Before presenting your offer, make sure you have:

✅  VALUE BUILDING

 Clear problem/opportunity identified

 Compelling solution presented

 Benefits clearly communicated

 Social proof included (results, testimonials)

 Authority established (credentials, results, recognition)

✅  PRICE PREPARATION

 DIY cost calculated (what it costs them to do it themselves)

 Personal investment calculated (what you invested to create it)

 Market comparisons researched (what competitors charge)

 Price anchors established (high numbers before your price)

 Tiered pricing options created (3 tiers minimum)

✅  PSYCHOLOGICAL TRIGGERS

 Preframe established (strategic reason for offer)

 Reason why articulated (why this deal for them)

 Scarcity implemented (legitimate limitations)

 Urgency created (deadline or consequence)

 Authority demonstrated (credentials and proof)

 Social proof shown (others buying/succeeding)

✅  OFFER STRUCTURE

 Payment options created (one-time + payment plan)

 Bonuses identified and valued

 Guarantee included (if appropriate)

 Clear call-to-action (exactly what to do next)

 Objections pre-handled (common concerns addressed)

✅  SCARCITY LAYERS

 Time limit (expires when?)

 Quantity limit (how many spots?)

 Qualification limit (who can buy?)

 Bonus expiration (when do extras go away?)

 Price increase (what happens after?)

✅  DELIVERY & FOLLOW-UP

 Future pace described (what happens after purchase)

 Next steps clarified (immediate access, onboarding, etc.)

 Support mentioned (how you'll help them succeed)

 Risk reversal (guarantee, satisfaction promise)

"This should require an investment of \[high amount\] because it's worth many times more than that"

"This could easily be \[high price\] and it would be a bargain"

"We've sold \[thousands/hundreds\] of units at \[higher price\]"

"Similar solutions charge \[competitor prices\]"

Scarcity:

Reason Why:

"To better yourself, I'm going to give the best deal possible"

"Because \[strategic business reason\], we're able to offer..."

Qualification:

"This isn't for everyone"

"If this seems too much, don't buy it - this is for serious players"

"If you already have \[prerequisite\], this is as valuable as \[something expensive\]"

"Only invest if you feel you'll get more than \[amount\] out of this"

The Reveal:

"So what's your investment to get started right now?"

"You can get started today for just \[amount\]"

"It's just \[amount\] one-time OR \[smaller amount\] x \[number\] payments"

"And we haven't even discussed the bonuses yet\!"

Urgency:

"This offer expires \[specific date/time\]"

"The timing couldn't be better, but this is only available for a limited time"

"Act now because \[specific consequence of waiting\]"

COMMON MISTAKES TO AVOID

❌  Mistake \#1: Revealing Price Too Early

Problem: Before you've built enough value

Solution: Spend 10-15 minutes building value, showing benefits, establishing authority BEFORE

mentioning price

❌  Mistake \#2: Not Enough Anchoring

Problem: Your price seems high because there's no context

Solution: Show at least 3-4 higher price points before your reveal

❌  Mistake \#3: Weak Scarcity

Problem: "Limited time offer" with no specifics

Solution: Use specific numbers, dates, and real limitations. Show proof of past scarcity.

❌  Mistake \#4: No Reason Why

Problem: Great deal with no explanation

Solution: Always give a strategic + emotional reason for the special pricing

❌  Mistake \#5: Single Price Point

Problem: Only offering one option

Solution: Create 3 tiers or at minimum offer payment plans

❌  Mistake \#6: Forgetting Payment Plans

Problem: One-time price only

Solution: Always offer payment plan option (increases conversions 30-50%)

❌  Mistake \#7: Revealing Bonuses Too Early

Problem: Including bonuses in initial value build

Solution: Save bonuses for after price reveal to make price seem even better

❌  Mistake \#8: No Juxtaposition

Problem: Single offer with nothing to compare

Solution: Create tiers so middle option looks attractive

❌  Mistake \#9: Fake Scarcity

Problem: "Limited time" but it's always available

Solution: Only use real limitations. Fake scarcity destroys trust.

❌  Mistake \#10: No Future Pace

Problem: They buy but don't know what happens next

Solution: Describe exactly what happens after they purchase

FINAL IMPLEMENTATION PLAN

Week 1: RESEARCH & CALCULATION

Day 1-2: Calculate your numbers

DIY cost (what it costs them)

Personal investment (what it cost you)

Market comparisons (what competitors charge)

Day 3-4: Design your tiers

Create 3 pricing tiers

Assign features to each tier

Calculate pricing multiples

Day 5-7: Build your bonuses

Identify 3-5 bonuses

Calculate value of each

Ensure bonuses enhance main offer

Week 2: CRAFTING YOUR PRESENTATION

Day 8-10: Write your preframe

Strategic reason for offer

Market positioning

"Why now" explanation

Day 11-12: Create your anchors

DIY cost slide/section

Personal investment reveal

Market comparison research

"What it should cost" anchor

Day 13-14: Build scarcity layers

Identify real limitations

Create countdown/deadline

Prepare proof of scarcity

Week 3: REFINEMENT

Day 15-17: Write your scripts

Value building section

Anchor section

Price reveal section

Bonus reveal section

Urgency/CTA section

Day 18-20: Create visuals

Slides or graphics

Comparison charts

Pricing tables

Bonus stack images

Day 21: Test and refine

Read through complete presentation

Time yourself

Adjust pacing

Ensure smooth flow

Week 4: LAUNCH

Day 22-24: Prepare delivery

Practice presentation

Prepare for objections

Set up payment systems

Create order pages

Day 25-28: Execute and optimize

Launch offer

Track conversions

Gather feedback

Refine for next time

MEASURING SUCCESS

Key Metrics to Track:

1\. Show Rate: What % of registrants showed up?

2\. Stay Rate: What % stayed until price reveal?

3\. Conversion Rate: What % bought?

4\. Cart Value: Average order value

5\. Tier Distribution: Which tier sells most?

6\. Payment Preference: One-time vs payment plan?

Optimization Targets:

Show Rate: 40-50%+ is good

Stay Rate: 60-70%+ is good

Conversion Rate: 5-10%+ is good for cold traffic, 15-30%+ for warm

Payment Plan Split: Expect 70-80% to choose payment plan

A/B Testing Ideas:

Different anchor amounts

Different tier names

Different pricing multiples

Different bonus packages

Different scarcity mechanisms

Different deadline lengths

KEY TAKEAWAYS

The Big Ideas:

1\. Price is about VALUE PERCEPTION, not actual numbers

2\. Anchoring is the most powerful pricing tool

3\. The goal is to make ANY price feel like a deal

4\. Stack multiple psychological triggers, don't rely on one

5\. Legitimate scarcity beats fake urgency every time

6\. Give people a REASON WHY for special pricing

7\. Multiple options increase conversions

8\. Payment plans dramatically increase sales

The Universal Formula:

1\. Build massive value first

2\. Show high DIY cost

3\. Show your personal investment

4\. Show market comparisons

5\. Anchor at high price

6\. Reveal "normal" price

7\. Reveal special price

8\. Show tiered options

9\. Add bonuses

10\. Add scarcity

11\. Add urgency

12\. Clear CTA

Remember:

Never apologize for your price

Qualify buyers (it's not for everyone)

Make them feel special (reason why)

Give them options (tiers + payment plans)

Create real scarcity (limited capacity)

Stack psychological triggers (don't rely on one)

Always test and optimize

YOUR ACTION ITEMS (Start Today):

✅  IMMEDIATE (Do This Week):

1\. Calculate your DIY cost

2\. Calculate your personal investment

3\. Research competitor pricing

4\. Create your 3-tier structure

5\. Identify 5 bonuses

✅  SHORT-TERM (Do This Month):

1\. Write your complete price presentation script

2\. Create visual elements (slides, graphics)

3\. Build your order pages

4\. Set up payment options

5\. Prepare objection handlers

✅  ONGOING (Do Every Launch):

1\. Test different anchor amounts

2\. Experiment with tier pricing

3\. Try different scarcity mechanisms

4\. Optimize bonus stack

5\. Refine "reason why" messaging

FINAL THOUGHTS

Jason Fladlien's approach to pricing is masterful because:

1\. It's systematic - Every element serves a purpose

2\. It's psychological - Uses proven influence principles

3\. It's ethical - Based on real value, not manipulation

4\. It's repeatable - Works across different offers/markets

5\. It's testable - Each element can be optimized

The secret isn't in the specific numbers... it's in the PERCEPTION you create around those

numbers.

Your job is to make your prospect say: "How could I NOT buy this at this price?"

When you achieve that, price becomes a non-issue.

Remember Jason's core principle:

"NO MATTER THE PRICE, IT'S ALWAYS A DEAL\!"

That's not about being cheap... it's about creating MASSIVE perceived value.

Now go implement these lessons and watch your conversions soar. 🚀

JASON FLADLIEN'S BONUSES MASTERCLASS: Complete

Breakdown & AfterHarvest Applications

CORE PHILOSOPHY

The Most Important Step

Jason's Quote: "Step 12 bonuses - most important step in my opinion in the webinar process"

Key Insight: If you're already doing good but could do better, bonuses are probably where you need to

optimize.

Critical Understanding: In affiliate offers, they barely mention the course at all. Everything from the

paradigm to the mechanisms links to the offer, so they spend ALL their time on the bonuses.

The Psychological Frame

"Everything I showed you today, the very best way to do that is with this course. And when you buy this

course..." \[then immediately transition to bonuses\]

The Carrot and Stick Pattern:

Regular price: $997, no bonuses

Webinar special: $497 WITH all the bonuses

You get MORE and pay LESS

"It short circuits the mind - this is such a good deal"

BONUS BEST PRACTICES

1\. Spend MORE Time on Bonuses Than Core Offer

Jason's Standard:

1 slide for a module in the core offer

2-4 slides for a SINGLE bonus

Sometimes 3-4x as long on a single bonus than on a component of the core offer

Why This Works:

Bonuses create the "stack" effect

Bonuses remove objections

Bonuses add exclusive value

Bonuses create urgency (only available through webinar)

2\. Your Best Stuff Should Be FREE Bonuses

The Principle: "You can't buy it at any price, but you can get it for free"

Powerful Elements:

Exclusivity (can't be purchased separately)

The word "FREE" (one of the two most powerful words in advertising)

Sex and Free are the most powerful words

"Free sex" would be ultimate, but we can use "FREE bonuses"

3\. Build Around Proof

Jason's Rule: "No proof, no bonus. I mean I still do it without proof, but reluctantly"

Application:

Interview with successful client = bonus

Case study breakdown = bonus

Live demonstration = bonus

Results showcase = bonus

4\. Bonuses Are an Audition

The Process:

Put bonuses in

If they don't hold their weight, take one out

Put another one in its place

Constantly rotating bonuses for optimization

Pro-Consumer Approach:

Still give them the other bonuses

Just don't mention them in the pitch anymore

They get MORE than what they thought

But you only pitch the BEST bonuses

5\. Multiple Modalities Are Key

Jason's Observation: "This is an area that I see is very weak in most people's bonus packages"

The Problem with Single Modality:

If you're selling training, don't just offer MORE training as bonuses

"Hey you're getting a bunch of training, let me give you more training"

Creates overwhelm, not excitement

Can actually COST you sales

The Solution: Mix different types of deliverables (see section below)

6\. Bonuses Can Cost You Sales

Warning Signs:

Too many bonuses = overwhelm

Wrong bonuses = objections

Single modality = redundancy

No scarcity = no urgency

Example of BAD Bonus:

Other Amazon affiliates: "Come to Canton Fair in China with us"

Cost: Trip to China, time, logistics

Creates objection: "I don't want to go to China"

THE BONUS DELIVERABLES (What to Offer)

Deliverable \#1: TRAINING

Forms of Training:

A) Interviews

Easiest bonuses to create

Interview successful clients/partners

Built around proof elements

2 hours of questions = ready-made bonus

Example - ASM4 Bonus \#7: "From Zero to 100K from Amazon Plus Adwords - Interview with James

Watson Hayes on the dumbest approach I've ever seen with AdWords, but using this one thing to drive

traffic direct to an Amazon listing equals money"

Why This Works:

James was already mentioned multiple times in content (foreshadowing)

Proof element embedded

Specific result ($100K)

Novel approach ("dumbest approach")

Jason's Story - Pedro Adao Launch:

Interviewed Joe Polish about viral contest

Joe talked for 2 hours straight

Asked maybe 5 questions total

Turned it into a bonus

B) Recorded Training Example - TWF (The Wholesale Formula): "Listing Level Up (cool name) -

my latest training where I show you every Amazon listing optimization secret I know from title to images,

bullet points to product descriptions and more. I'll put to work for you the same insights I used to improve

Amazon listings to pull in up to 40% conversion rates"

Key Elements:

Cool name ("Listing Level Up")

Comprehensive promise (every secret)

Specific areas covered

Proof element (40% conversion rates)

C) Live Calls Example: "In this brand new training that you can attend live on Zoom, Manuel will

breakdown and teach you the strategy to create bundles to double, triple, even quadruple your profit

margins"

Why Upcoming Live Works:

Didn't have to create bonus before selling product

Creates time urgency (specific date)

Benefit: "If not, trust me, we'll record it and put it in members area"

Deliverable \#2: EBOOKS/GUIDES

General Rule: Ebooks as bonuses "essentially suck" because retail value is low ($7-10 on Kindle)

When They Work:

When tied to specific proof element

Example: "At one point we were top 1% of all Odesk clients"

Made sense for outsourcing guide

Jason's Candid Admission: "Sometimes you gotta have bonuses and you're like, I don't have time, so I'll

just look around - oh you look good, come on get in there right. And then we have a bonus."

Deliverable \#3: SOFTWARE/TOOLS

Power Move: Give them software that does what you taught manually

Framework: Show Manual, Sell Automatic (Paradigm \#1)

Teach them the hard way

Show all the steps

Make it tedious

Then reveal: Software does it in 2 clicks

Example - Website Setup: Manual Process:

Install favorite theme

Configure that theme

Set up privacy

Set up permalinks

Set up sidebar

Install 3 favorite plugins

Delete hello world post and comment

Fix footer

Set up categories

Set up pages

Time: At least an hour

Then ask: "What if you could automate all that in a couple of clicks?"

Value Perception: The more they see the manual work, the more they appreciate your automatic solution

Deliverable \#4: COMMUNITY ACCESS

The Good:

Ongoing support

Network effects

Updates and resources

Expert access

The Bad (Jason's Story):

"Lifetime access to community" can feel like homework

"Now I gotta show up?"

Can create objection rather than excitement

How to Position It Right:

Make it OPTIONAL support, not requirement

Frame as "available when you need it"

Highlight specific benefits (Q\&A calls, expert access)

Add guarantee of response time

Example Done Well - TWF Prime: "90 days of TWF Prime which includes a private FB group with

TWF experts; live Q\&A calls every month and a guaranteed response to each question you post in the

community"

Key additions:

Time-limited (90 days, not overwhelming)

Live Q\&A monthly (specific)

GUARANTEED response (removes fear of being ignored)

Deliverable \#5: PHYSICAL ITEMS

Controversial Strategy: Jason tried this, admits it can backfire

What Worked:

Books (high perceived value)

Physical products related to niche

Tangible items they can touch

What DIDN'T Work:

Anything requiring them to DO something

Travel bonuses ("come to China")

Complicated logistics

Key Principle: Physical bonuses should SIMPLIFY their life, not complicate it

Deliverable \#6: TEMPLATES/SWIPES/DONE-FOR-YOU

High Value Categories:

Email swipes

Sales page templates

Ad templates

Scripts

Checklists

SOPs

Why These Work:

Immediate usability

Removes "blank page" problem

Proven to work

Easy to demonstrate value

Deliverable \#7: SERVICES

Forms:

Done-for-you setups

Reviews/critiques

One-on-one calls

Group coaching

VA placement/hiring help

Scaling Consideration: These don't scale well but add huge value

Jason's Approach: Use services as HIGH-END bonuses or for limited beta groups

Deliverable \#8: CASE STUDY BREAKDOWNS

Format:

Take successful example

Break down every element

Show what worked and why

Make it replicable

Example: "Complete breakdown of \[successful person\]'s exact funnel with all the psychology, copy, and

strategy explained"

THE PRESENTATION STRATEGIES (How to Present Bonuses)

Strategy \#1: Name Your Bonuses

Bad: "Bonus training on X" Good: "The \[Cool Name\] System"

Examples from Jason:

"Listing Level Up"

"Affilo JETPACK"

"The Consumable System"

Naming Principles:

Memorable

Suggests benefit/speed

Easy to say

Feels like a product

Strategy \#2: Foreshadowing

The Technique: Mention bonus content BEFORE you reveal it's a bonus

Example:

Content section: Share James Watson's case study

Mention him 2-3 times throughout

Show his results

THEN in bonuses: "Interview with James Watson"

Why This Works:

They already want it before you offer it

Feels like you're reading their mind

Natural transition

Builds anticipation

Strategy \#3: Stack Them Visually

The Slide Structure:

BONUS \#1: \[Name\] (Value: $X,XXX)

→ What it is

→ Why it matters

BONUS \#2: \[Name\] (Value: $X,XXX)

→ What it is  

→ Why it matters

BONUS \#3: \[Name\] (Value: $X,XXX)

→ What it is

→ Why it matters

TOTAL VALUE: $XX,XXX

YOUR INVESTMENT: $X,XXX

Key Elements:

Number them clearly

Give each a value

Brief description

Show the stack/gap

Strategy \#4: Dramatize Each One

Jason's Method: 2-4 slides per bonus

Slide Sequence for Single Bonus:

1\. The Name + Promise

2\. What's included (breakdown)

3\. Proof element (results/testimonials)

4\. Why you can't get this anywhere else

Example Structure: Slide 1: "BONUS \#3: The Listing Level-Up System" Slide 2: "Every Amazon

listing optimization secret I know..." Slide 3: "The same insights I used to achieve 40% conversion rates"

Slide 4: "This training has never been public before..."

Strategy \#5: Use Proof Elements

Requirements:

Specific numbers

Real names

Actual results

Before/after

Timestamps

Bad: "This bonus helped many people" Good: "James used this exact interview training to go from $0 to

$100K in 90 days"

Strategy \#6: Create Natural Scarcity

Methods:

A) Webinar-Only Bonuses

Only available during webinar

Disappears after

Creates urgency

B) Limited Quantity

"First 50 people"

"While spots last"

Must be REAL scarcity

C) Time-Sensitive

"Available for next 48 hours"

"Expires when timer hits zero"

Create actual deadline

D) Qualification-Based

"Only for action-takers who sign up NOW"

"For people who stayed through whole webinar"

Makes them feel special

Strategy \#7: The Bonuses vs. Course Dynamic

The Balance:

Core offer: 60% of time

Bonuses: 40% of time

BUT bonuses might have more slides

Bonuses should feel MORE valuable than core

Jason's Insight: "In affiliate offers, we barely mention the course at all because everything we talked

about in the content, from the paradigm to the mechanisms, that's linked to the offer. So we can barely

mention the offer. Everything I showed you today, the very best way to do that is with this course. And

when you buy this course, \[then spend all time on bonuses\]"

THE MAGIC NUMBER: HOW MANY BONUSES?

Jason's Standard: 7 Bonuses

The Sweet Spot:

7 seems to be the best number

Will push to 9 when necessary

Gets scared at double digits (10+)

Usually goes with 7

Why 7 Works:

Psychological principle (7±2 items in working memory)

Enough to create stack effect

Not so many it's overwhelming

Can dedicate 2-4 slides per bonus

Total of 14-28 slides for all bonuses

Structure of 7:

4-5 "numbered" bonuses (BONUS \#1, \#2, \#3, \#4, \#5)

2-3 "additional" bonuses (not numbered, positioned as extras)

Example Progression:

BONUS \#1: Main training/interview

BONUS \#2: Software/tool  

BONUS \#3: Templates/swipes

BONUS \#4: Case study breakdown

BONUS \#5: Community access

BONUS \#6: Live coaching call

BONUS \#7: Service/done-for-you

Plus: Fast-action bonus

Plus: Webinar-only special bonus

THE ULTIMATE BONUS: THE WEBINAR-ONLY SPECIAL

What Makes This Different

Jason's Definition: "The greatest technique I've ever done"

Characteristics:

Only available DURING webinar

Disappears after webinar ends

Will NEVER be mentioned again after this slide

Not shown on sales page

Creates maximum urgency

The Psychology Behind It

The Setup:

1\. Go through all regular bonuses first

2\. After final pitch

3\. Before objection handling

4\. Pull up "one more thing" slide

The Dramatic Pause: "You may wonder why I paused just here. It's because what I'm about to say next

will be forever deleted from the annals of webinar history."

Key Elements:

Creates moment of high attention

Everyone leans in

Stakes are elevated

FOMO kicks in hard

How to Execute It (Step-by-Step)

STEP 1: The Permission-Asking Frame

Jason's exact language pattern:

"I want to offer you something additional. But I had to negotiate with \[partner/company\] to even be able

to do this. I pleaded, I did everything I could. I said please, please, please let me be able to offer this. And

they said no. They said nope, no way.

And then they said finally, we will. But we will only allow you to give it to some people. We will only put

a strict number on it.

Now I'm not gonna tell you what that number is, but if we hit that number on this webinar, I will let you

know."

Why This Works:

Scarcity is BELIEVABLE (negotiated hard for it)

Third-party validation (had to get permission)

Limited quantity (real constraint)

You're the hero (fought for them)

STEP 2: The Qualification Decision

"My dilemma is the following: Who do I give it to? Who should get it and who shouldn't get it?

So here's how I've figured out who I should give it to: If you've shown up on this webinar and you stayed

this whole time, it seems to me like if there's anybody that's gonna be successful with the program, it's

you.

So I might give it to anybody here on the webinar. But here's the catch: ONLY if you're here on the

webinar."

Why This Works:

Makes them feel special (they stayed)

Qualification = action-taker identity

Creates in-group vs. out-group

Rewards behavior you want

STEP 3: The Timestamp Urgency

"As soon as we end this webinar, we'll check the timestamps. We will know if you sign up before the

webinar. You get this bonus. If you're even a second after the webinar, you do not get this bonus."

Why This Works:

Specific deadline

Verifiable (timestamps)

Binary (yes/no)

Creates NOW pressure

STEP 4: The Invisibility Proof

"Now know this: When you go to \[URL\], you will see hide nor hair mentioned of this bonus on this page.

It's only available \[here\]. But don't worry, you will get it. Trust us, it will show up to you."

Why This Works:

Proof of exclusivity (not on page)

They can verify claim

Must trust you (relationship building)

Can't "look it up later"

STEP 5: The Stacking Technique

First time: Offer 1 webinar-only bonus

Later: "We'll also give you \[bonus \#2\]"

Even later: "And lifetime access, not just one year"

Jason's Example:

First: VA Placement for one year

Stack: "We'll also give you lifetime access, not just one year"

Stack: "You'll never pay a penny to VA Placement"

Why This Works:

Escalation of commitment

Multiple "wins"

Compounds the value

Each addition feels like gift

STEP 6: The Final Warning

Why This Works:

Last chance psychology

Invites engagement

Creates memory anchor

Reinforces scarcity

Variations of Webinar-Only Bonuses

Type 1: Exclusive Bonus Item

New bonus not available anywhere

Created specifically for this webinar

"Only webinar attendees get this"

Type 2: Upgraded Existing Bonus

Take regular bonus

Double/triple/quadruple it

"Webinar-only: 4X version"

Jason's Example:

Regular: "We'll recoup up to $1,000 in ad spend if it doesn't work"

Webinar-only: "We'll recoup UNLIMITED ad spend, but only for webinar attendees"

Type 3: Extended Access

Regular offer: 30-day access

Webinar-only: Lifetime access

or 1-year → lifetime

Type 4: Quantity Increase

Regular: 1 coaching call

Webinar-only: 3 coaching calls

Type 5: Service Upgrade

Regular: Group coaching

Webinar-only: One-on-one coaching call

Where It Goes in Sequence

Jason's Recommended Order:

1\. All regular bonuses (\#1-7)

2\. Final pitch/offer slide

3\. WEBINAR-ONLY SPECIAL BONUS (here)

4\. Objection handling section

5\. Q\&A

6\. Final close

Why This Placement:

After they see full value stack

Before objections (reduces objections)

Creates buying momentum

Urgency is fresh for objection handling

The "Chop Point" Strategy

How to Structure for Replay:

During Live Webinar:

1\. Present bonus

2\. Say: "I hear your questions, I see them. We'll answer those shortly, but we're talking specifically

about this bonus right now. We just want to make sure you understand this bonus."

3\. Clarify bonus details

4\. Then say: "Even though I'm not gonna mention this webinar-only special bonus once I leave this

slide, know that at any point in time, when you decide to sign up, whether it's now or 5 minutes from

now, you will still get this bonus as long as it's done before I end this webinar."

5\. Move to objection handling

6\. At end: "Okay, now that the webinar part's done, I can just chop off the ending. So if there's anybody

signing up right now, let me know, otherwise I'm gonna end the webinar."

For Replay Editing:

Remove only the webinar-only special bonus slides

Keep all objection handling (needed for replay)

Result: Replay viewers don't see special bonus

Live attendees got exclusive offer

Jason's Advanced Move: "I'll say if we have time at the end, if I do a sign off, I may come back and

hang out with you just real quickly because I could just chop off the end. We'll see though, okay. And so

now they're anticipating that that's a possibility too."

The Live Engagement Technique

The Todd Story:

After announcing webinar ending:

"If there's anybody signing up right now, let me know"

Todd says: "I'm signing up"

Will watches like a hawk

"While Todd is still signing up... Todd, you done signing up yet?"

Takes Todd 15 minutes to sign up

Shows how inefficient customers are

Creates social proof (others see someone buying)

Creates pressure (don't want to miss it)

What Happens:

Real buyers announce themselves

Creates buying frenzy

Social proof compounds

Others don't want to be left out

Technical Issues Strategy: People will say:

"My browser's timing out"

"I don't understand how this happens"

Jason's response:

"Go to Firefox" \[if not working\]

"Try Chrome" \[if still not working\]

"Try Safari"

The Point: Buyers who want it will do ANYTHING to get in. That's how powerful the urgency is.

Results from This Technique

Jason's Metrics:

Increases conversion significantly

Creates future webinar attendance ("I better be on his next webinar 'cause what if he does that again")

Customers check your work ("He didn't lie to me, wow")

Builds trust for future offers

Why People Say They Buy: "I'm not buying because of the bonus, but because of your belief in me"

Jason's response: "Okay cool, the money still cashes the same to me."

How to Practice This

Jason's Warning: "The first few times I see people do it, it's not good, because they don't practice it like

we tell them. You gotta practice this."

Practice Routine:

1\. Stand in front of a mirror with slide deck

2\. Practice the pause

3\. Practice the dramatic reveal

4\. Practice the qualification

5\. Practice the ending

6\. Master the timing

Jason on His Delivery: "I don't get that dramatic, but there's drama to it."

The Timing Element:

When you hit the slide, pause

"You may wonder why I paused just there"

Creates anticipation

Makes it memorable

The pause IS part of the technique

SCARCITY INTEGRATION

Scarcity is Intertwined

Jason's Principle: "Scarcity is intertwined into pretty much every bonus that I offer"

How to Add Scarcity to Each Bonus:

Bonus \#1: "Only available when you buy Product Eclass from producteclass.com. It's $997 and there's no

bonuses if you buy it through the regular site. But through the webinar, it's $497 AND there's all the

bonuses."

FINAL CHECKLIST: BONUS AUDIT

Before Launch, Check Every Bonus:

Naming:

 Does it have a cool name?

 Is it memorable?

 Does it suggest benefit?

Value:

 Can you assign a dollar value?

 Is it believable?

 Is it more than they're paying?

Modality:

 Is it different from other bonuses?

 Does it use a different format?

 Does it appeal to different learning style?

Proof:

 Do you have a proof element?

 Can you show results?

 Is it believable?

Scarcity:

 Is there a reason it's exclusive?

 Is the scarcity real?

 Is it time-sensitive or quantity-limited?

Objection Handling:

 Does it remove a specific objection?

 Does it make the decision easier?

 Does it reduce risk?

Foreshadowing:

 Did you mention it earlier in content?

 Are they already wanting it?

 Is the reveal satisfying?

Presentation:

 Does it have 2-4 slides?

 Is it visually appealing?

 Is it easy to understand?

KEY PRINCIPLES TO ALWAYS REMEMBER

1\. Bonuses Are the Closer

The core offer gets them interested. The bonuses close the deal. Spend MORE time on bonuses than you

think you should.

2\. Your Best Stuff Should Be Free

Don't hold back. Give away valuable stuff as bonuses. Make them think, "If this is free, the paid stuff

must be INSANE."

3\. Proof or GTFO

Every bonus should have a proof element. If you can't show results or demonstrate value, it's probably not

a strong bonus.

4\. Multiple Modalities Matter

Training + training + training = overwhelming

Training + tool + templates + service = irresistible

5\. Foreshadow Everything

Mention bonus content in your teaching. Then reveal it's a bonus later. Makes them feel like you read

their mind.

6\. Scarcity Must Be Real

Don't fake scarcity. If you say "limited spots," mean it. If you say "webinar-only," actually remove it after

the webinar.

7\. The Number 7 is Magic

Aim for 7 numbered bonuses. You can add non-numbered bonuses on top, but 7 is the sweet spot.

8\. Webinar-Only is the Secret Weapon

Master this technique. Practice it. Perfect it. It's the most powerful conversion tool Jason uses.

9\. Bonuses Should Remove Objections

Every bonus should make it HARDER to say no. If a bonus doesn't remove an objection or add value, cut

it.

10\. Test and Rotate

Your bonuses should audition. If they don't perform, replace them. Keep the ones that convert, drop the

ones that don't.

Week 5: Integration

 Add foreshadowing in content sections

 Connect bonuses to main teaching

FINAL THOUGHTS

Jason spends more time on bonuses than any other part of his webinars. Why? Because bonuses are where

deals are won or lost.

Your core offer gets them interested. Your content builds trust. Your paradigm shifts their thinking. But

your BONUSES close the sale.

So don't treat bonuses as an afterthought. Don't just throw in some PDFs and call it a day. Craft them.

Name them. Present them. Make them irresistible.

And remember: If your bonuses don't make someone think, "I'd buy it just for the bonuses," they're not

strong enough yet.
