RISK LESSONS

Jason Fladlien's Guarantee & Objection Handling Masterclass

Complete Reference for AfterHarvest Implementation

# THE CORE PHILOSOPHY

"Show how it's riskier to say NO than YES - and be willing to spend HOURS handling objections. That's where all the money is."

Risk mitigation has TWO primary tools:

1\. Guarantees (risk removal)

2\. Objections (belief handling)

# PART 1: GUARANTEE STRUCTURES

## A. UNCONDITIONAL MONEY BACK GUARANTEE

The Standard Formula:

"When you sign up you're also protected by a 30 day no questions asked money back guarantee. If you're not satisfied, neither am I. For any reason or none at all, just contact us let us know. We'll return every penny of your investment. You're 100% protected. You only have the opportunity to gain when you sign up."

Key Language Patterns:

• "Every PENNY of your investment" - makes investment feel small

• "For any reason or NONE AT ALL" - removes need to justify

• "You only have the opportunity to GAIN" - reframe from risk to opportunity

Triple Guarantee Variation:

When you want to "groove it up and make it look even cooler":

• Guarantee \#1: Best support

• Guarantee \#2: You'll never have to \[specific pain point\]

• Guarantee \#3: Money back within 30 days, no reason needed

## B. CONDITIONAL MONEY BACK GUARANTEES

When to Use: Better conversion when you need skin in the game, or during high inflation periods (refund rates rise with inflation - do the math on conversion gain vs refund cost).

The "Fast Action Taker" Frame:

"WARNING: This course and bonuses are reserved for fast action takers only. Only sign up if you plan on getting your first product live within 30 days. \[Partner name\] makes his money off the fulfillment of you selling your products - otherwise we could never make such a deal. That's why we have a conditional guarantee. If you sign up and don't do just the bare minimum, you do not get to ask for your money back. However, if you just get a single product live in 30 days from when you sign up and you're not over the moon excited, we'll cheerfully give you your full investment back."

The "Boogeyman Close" to Sell the Conditional:

"Here's the real reality - I'm trying to TRICK YOU into your own success. I know if you just get going, this will be easier than you can ever think. But right now you're scared of the boogeyman. You think there's a pretend boogeyman under your bed that's gonna get you, and you're not gonna take action. So I'm gonna trick you into being successful."

## C. BETTER THAN MONEY BACK GUARANTEES

Jason's Personal Favorite: "These are my jam."

The Double Your Money Back Structure:

"Your investment is twice protected. We got your standard guarantee - for any reason or none at all, if you want your money back, you got it. 30 days. OR your double money back guarantee - just follow one of my \[product\] secrets and within 60 days, if you don't make double your investment, I'll give you DOUBLE your money back. Simple as that."

The Easiest DYMB Guarantee Ever Created:

Requirements to fulfill guarantee:

1\. Create a Google Doc

2\. Email you the Google Doc

3\. Add you as collaborator

4\. For 60 days straight:

• Morning: Write goal for the day

• Evening: Write what they did

• Two paragraphs per day

Result: "You're lucky if you get 1% compliance on that."

The Philosophy Behind This:

"Winners do it - so you're encouraging people to do the thing that winners will do. But most people aren't ready to win yet. That's why we keep selling them stuff to try to get them to win tomorrow if they won't win today."

The Sales Copy:

"It costs nothing. It's so simple. Everybody can see themselves doing it in the moment. And then you could say - just do this, if you do this the rest will take care of itself. I know you'll be successful. And if you're not for some strange reason, because lightning does strike twice occasionally, rarely but possibly, if you're the unluckiest SOB in the world, I'll still compensate you for your time. Double your money back. Wow."

# PART 2: HANDLING OBJECTIONS

## THE SIX UNIVERSAL OBJECTIONS

Every objection falls into one of these categories:

1\. The Money Objection

2\. The Time Objection

3\. The Confidence/Competence Objection

4\. The Alternatives Objection

5\. The Procrastination Objection

6\. Product-Specific Objections

## STAGES OF A PROSPECT (The Awareness Ladder)

Understanding where they are in their journey:

Stage 1: "No one can do it"

Stage 2: "You can do it. But that's it."

Stage 3: "You and some other people can do it"

Stage 4: "You and most others can do it"

Stage 5: "Okay, I'll try to do it"

Your job: Move them up the ladder with each piece of evidence.

## THE ANTIDOTE TO MOST RISKS

"TESTIMONIALS are the antidote to most risks."

Stack testimonials strategically:

• Different backgrounds

• Different starting points

• Different speeds of success

• Different levels of commitment

## SPECIFIC CLOSES & OBJECTION HANDLERS

### 1\. "DOES IT STILL WORK?"

The Close:

"Does it still work? Let me answer that with a question - does breathing still work? Does eating food still work? Does sleeping still work? Some things are fundamental principles that don't change. The tactics might evolve, but the strategy? That's timeless. And what I'm teaching you are the fundamental principles. So yes, it still works. It's always worked. And it will always work."

### 2\. "I'M SCARED"

The Fear of Success Reframe (Jason's favorite):

"Are you afraid you're gonna fail? Or are you afraid you're gonna succeed? Because here's the thing - you KNOW failure. You live with failure every single day. You understand what that's like. You may not like it, but you GET it. But success? You have NO CLUE what that's like. You don't know what it's gonna be like when you have \[desired result\]. You have no idea what that's gonna be. And THAT scares you more. So is it failure that really holds you back, or is it the fear of success?"

Why This Works: It's a confusion technique that gets them to reconsider an automatically-held belief.

The Passenger Seat Close:

"Fear's gonna be there. I'm not gonna lie to you and say the fear will disappear. But here's what we're gonna do - we're gonna put fear in the passenger seat. It can come along for the ride. You can even put a seatbelt on it if you want - I'll leave that up to you. But fear no longer gets to DRIVE the vehicle. Can you do that for me?"

### 3\. RIDE THE BIKE CLOSE

Jason's Personal Favorite - The Learning Machine:

"I understand - the first time's the hardest. Your first time trying to ride a bike probably didn't go so well. Thank God that younger you didn't give up. My guess is you didn't obsess over the failure. My guess is you got right back up, dusted yourself off, hopped back on that bike, tried it again. Even with scraped elbows, even with bruised knees."

Why This Works: "Milton Erickson close speaking to you as a child. Phonological ambiguity - who's the child? That close kills because they're like 'damn I've come a long way.'"

### 4\. THE THREE OPTION CLOSE

Jason: "This is a killer one."

The Structure:

"You've got three options:

1\. Do what you've always done with your \[business/situation\]

2\. Give up and do something completely different

3\. Double down with our additional advantages, resources, community, and support"

The Psychology: "Everybody wants to double down." Removes "do nothing" as an option.

### 5\. COMMITMENT & FENCE SITTING

The Hemorrhoids Line:

"Sitting on the fence is uncomfortable, and if you do it too long, it leads to hemorrhoids. I just want you off the fence now. Whether you land in yes territory or no territory, that's less important to me than you dying on the fence."

The Two Pains:

"We're all faced with two pains - the pain of regret or the pain of commitment. The pain of commitment weighs ounces. The pain of regret weighs tons. So you're gonna have to have a pain anyway. Which pain do you choose?"

# PART 3: APPLICATION TO AFTERHARVEST

## YOUR GUARANTEE STRUCTURE RECOMMENDATION

For Black Friday Campaigns:

Use a Conditional Better Than Money Back Guarantee

The Setup:

"You're protected two ways. First, standard 30-day money back guarantee - for any reason or none at all, you get every penny back. BUT - here's where it gets interesting. Follow our Black Friday campaign blueprint, send the emails we write for you, and if you don't generate at least 2x what you paid us within 60 days, we'll double your money back."

The Condition:

"Simple requirement: Create a Google Doc, share it with us, and for 60 days log: - Morning: Your goal for the day - Evening: What you actually did Two paragraphs per day. That's it."

Why This Works:

• Most won't do it (1% compliance)

• Those who do it will succeed

• It demonstrates commitment

• It removes risk completely

• It reframes from cost to profit

• The condition is so simple they can visualize doing it

## YOUR TOP 10 OBJECTIONS TO HANDLE

Based on your business model, here are the objections you'll face most:

1\. "Why would I give you 15% when I can do this myself?"

Response: "You COULD do this yourself. You could also cut your own hair, change your own oil, and file your own taxes. The question isn't CAN you do it - it's WILL you do it, and how much is it costing you every month that you HAVEN'T done it? We're not charging you for what we do - we're charging you for the results we get. And if we don't get results, you don't pay."

2\. "What if my list is too small for Black Friday?"

Response: "That's actually perfect. Here's why - the smaller your list, the MORE important it is that you maximize every dollar per subscriber. A small engaged list beats a large cold list every time. We've generated \[specific example\] from lists under 500 people. The question isn't the size of your list - it's whether you're leaving money on the table. And right now? You are."

3\. "I don't want to over-email my list"

Response: "I appreciate that you care about your list. That's exactly why this works. We're not spam artists. Your list is ALREADY being emailed by everyone else. Every day. And most of those emails? Garbage. What we do is create emails that your list actually WANTS to read. The risk isn't over-emailing. The risk is under-serving."

4\. "15% seems high"

Response: "Compared to what? The 100% you're keeping from revenue you're NOT generating right now? Here's the math - if we generate an extra $50K from your Black Friday campaign, you keep $42,500. You know what you're keeping right now from Black Friday revenue? $0. So is 85% of something better than 100% of nothing?"

## MASTER PATTERNS TO REMEMBER

1\. The Inflation/Refund Correlation

"When inflation rate goes up, so does refund rate. It's a math equation."

2\. The 1% Compliance Rule

"You're lucky if you get 1% compliance on \[easy action\]. Winners do it. Most people aren't ready to win yet."

3\. The Objection = Money Formula

"Be willing to spend HOURS handling objections. That's where all the money is."

4\. The Confusion Technique

"Gets somebody to get unstuck from their limiting belief. Through confusion I get them to reconsider a belief they automatically hold without thinking."

5\. The Riskier to Say No

"Show how it's riskier to say NO than YES. Focus on the cost of inaction, not just the benefit of action."

# FINAL WISDOM FROM JASON

"When it comes to risk, show how it's riskier to say NO than YES, and be willing to spend hours handling objections. That's where all the money is."

Translation for AfterHarvest:

• Spend MORE time on objection handling than pitch

• Show them the cost of inaction (another failed Black Friday)

• Make your guarantee so strong it's stupid NOT to try

• Handle every objection like it's a gift (because it is)

• Close in the intro, close in the content, close in the close

Jason Fladlien's Webinar Scarcity Masterclass

Complete Study Notes & Analysis

THE FOUNDATIONAL PRINCIPLE

"Scarcity is the greatest motivator of all time"

This isn't just a tactic—it's understanding human psychology at its deepest level. People are more

motivated by what they might lose than what they might gain. This is the core of why scarcity works so

powerfully in sales.

THE FIVE TYPES OF SCARCITY

1\. Price Increases

The offer costs more after a deadline

Simplest and most direct form

Works because of clear financial consequence

2\. Bonuses Disappear

Main offer stays, but extras go away

Powerful because people hate losing value

Can be stacked with other types

3\. Offer Closes Completely

The entire opportunity goes away

Strongest form of scarcity

Requires strongest justification

4\. Window of Opportunity Passes

Market conditions change

External factors make it impossible later

"The world will be different" positioning

5\. Combination Approach

Layering multiple scarcity types

Most sophisticated and effective

Example: "Price goes up AND bonuses disappear AND then it closes"

THE CRITICAL SCARCITY FRAMEWORK

THE THREE PILLARS THAT MAKE SCARCITY WORK

PILLAR \#1: THE "REASON WHY"

Why this matters: Without a legitimate reason, scarcity feels manipulative and fake. People's BS

detectors are highly tuned to artificial urgency.

Core Reasons That Work:

1\. Rewarding Fast Action Takers

"I want to reward people who take action quickly"

Positions scarcity as a benefit for decisive people

Creates an identity play (are you a fast action taker?)

2\. Maintaining Exclusivity

"Otherwise the offer isn't special"

"This needs to be exclusive to maintain value"

Appeals to status and uniqueness

3\. External Constraints ("It's Out of My Hands")

Physical limitations (seminar room capacity)

Partner restrictions

System/capacity limits

This is POWERFUL because you're not the "bad guy"—circumstances are

4\. Beta Testing/First Movers

"Looking for beta users to test this"

"Need to work with a small group first"

"Testing before full release"

Logical and believable

The Psychology: When you give a reason (even a simple one), compliance rates skyrocket. The famous

copy machine study showed people are 90%+ more likely to comply when you give ANY reason, even

"because I need to make copies."

PILLAR \#2: BELIEVABILITY

Why this matters: Scarcity means nothing if people don't believe it's real. You need to PROVE your

scarcity is legitimate.

How to Make It Believable:

1\. Show Visual Proof

Screenshots of number of sales

Live countdown timers

Seat/spot counters going down in real-time

Physical evidence builds trust

2\. Explain the Consequences

"Here's exactly what happens after the deadline..."

"The price WILL go to $X on this date"

"These bonuses WILL be removed"

Be specific about the change

3\. Historical Proof

"Last time we did this, here's what happened..."

Show screenshots from past events

Testimonials from people who missed it

AMZ Launchpad example: "Last time we sold out in 6 minutes"

4\. Transparency About the Mechanism

Explain HOW the scarcity works

RGT example: "We can only take 75 clients, here's why..."

Don't be mysterious—clarity builds trust

Key Insight: The more you can demonstrate that your scarcity is REAL and enforced, the more powerful

it becomes. False scarcity destroys trust forever.

PILLAR \#3: THE LANGUAGE OF SCARCITY

Why this matters: Certain words trigger psychological responses related to loss aversion and urgency.

Use them strategically.

The Power Words:

Limited - Implies finite quantity

Short time - Creates time pressure

While it lasts - Suggests depletion

First mover advantage - Status + logic combo

Miss out - Direct loss framing

Before it's gone - Urgency + loss

Exclusive - Status and scarcity combined

Deadline - Clear endpoint

Early Bird - Rewards timing

First come, first served - Fairness + urgency

Now - Immediate action required

Rare - Implies infrequency

How to Use Them:

Sprinkle throughout your close

Don't overuse (loses power)

Combine with your reason why

Make them factual, not hype

ADVANCED SCARCITY TACTICS BREAKDOWN

THE "CATCH" TECHNIQUE

What it is: Presenting an amazing offer, building excitement, then revealing there's a catch—but the

catch is actually SCARCITY that works in their favor if they act fast.

The Structure:

1\. Present incredible value proposition

2\. Show everything they get

3\. Build up the excitement

4\. "There is a catch to this super special deal though..."

5\. The catch = time-limited or quantity-limited

6\. "You have to act before \[deadline\]"

Why it works:

Pattern interrupt (people expect bad news)

Relief when the "catch" is just urgency

Makes them feel like they're getting away with something

Creates immediate decision pressure

Example from transcript: "Just one $497 investment gets you EVERYTHING... Just ONE authority

package you sell will cover your investment\! There is a catch though... The offer expires shortly."

THE "BAD NEWS" FRAME

What it is: Positioning scarcity as unfortunate news you have to deliver, making you seem reluctant to

limit the offer.

The Structure:

1\. Build incredible value

2\. "Here's the bad news..."

3\. "Not everyone will get this pricing"

4\. "It's reserved for fast action takers only"

5\. Frame yourself as the good guy who wants everyone to have it

Why it works:

Removes resistance (you're on their side)

Creates "us vs. them" dynamic

Fast action takers feel special

Uses negative framing to drive positive action

Example from transcript: "THE BAD NEWS: Not everyone will get the special $499 pricing nor all

these exclusive bonuses. They are reserved for fast action takers only... those like Jay Boyer, Mandy

Kendall, & Costas Peppas. That's the type of people I want to reward with our special Product eClass

discount."

THE FAST ACTION BONUS STACK

What it is: Adding extra bonuses that ONLY early buyers receive, creating tiers of value.

The Structure:

1\. Main offer with standard bonuses

2\. Additional bonus layer for acting during/immediately after webinar

3\. Escalating urgency (first 25 people, first hour, etc.)

4\. Clear cutoff point

Why it works:

Rewards immediate action

Creates FOMO for specific items

Segments serious buyers from browsers

Adds value without discounting

Example from transcript: "If you're one of the first 25 customers to sign up, you can come to Trey's live

workshop in November 6th, 7th & 8th in St. Louis, Missouri... Where Trey breaks down in person,

'hands-on' the ins and outs of the funnel you get access to in Ramp Engine."

Advanced move: Make the fast action bonus extremely valuable but time/quantity limited. This creates a

"bonus within the bonus" psychology.

THE PRE-ORDER/PRE-RELEASE PRICING

What it is: Offering special pricing before official launch, with clear price increase on launch day.

The Structure:

1\. "This product launches on \[date\]"

2\. "Retail price will be $X"

3\. "Pre-launch special: $Y"

4\. "Only time ever offered at this price"

5\. Show the value breakdown

Why it works:

Logical reason (pre-launch pricing is normal)

Clear deadline (launch date)

Significant savings

"Beta tester" psychology

Example from transcript: "Prosperity Algorithm: Retail Price $999.95, Pre-launch Special $99, Only

time ever offered at this price\!"

Key insight: The "only time ever" language is critical. It removes hope of future discounts and creates

now-or-never urgency.

THE DOWNSELL SCARCITY TACTIC

What it is: Using scarcity on a LOWER-priced offer to prevent replay watchers from gaming the system.

The Structure:

1\. Main webinar offer with standard close

2\. Lower-priced downsell exists BUT...

3\. Downsell is HIDDEN from replay viewers

4\. "Warning: We priced this so low we're not mentioning it on the webinar"

5\. "If you watch the replay, this section will be edited out"

Why it works:

Prevents people from waiting

Makes live attendees feel special

Creates fear of missing hidden opportunities

Protects main offer's value

Example from transcript: "Warning\! We have priced this so low we aren't going to mention it on the

webinar... Just in case anyone watches the replay and sees that they missed out on such a special low, low

investment. So... You have to go to the link that is appearing on the page now... If you watch the replay of

this webinar, you'll notice this section will have been edited it."

This is GENIUS because:

It acknowledges people might wait

Makes waiting explicitly costly

Creates multiple reasons to act now

Uses meta-awareness as a tactic

THE LIFETIME ACCESS DELETION POINT

What it is: Offering lifetime access to something, but then explicitly showing when that option ends.

The Structure:

1\. Present incredible lifetime value

2\. "Normal pricing: $X/month"

3\. "Today only: Lifetime access for one payment"

4\. Visual "END OF DELETE POINT" marker

5\. "After this, it's gone forever"

Why it works:

Lifetime vs. recurring is massive value difference

Visual marker makes it concrete

"Delete point" language is final

Simple math shows savings

Example from transcript: "LIFETIME\! One-Year Access to VA Placement... END OF DELETE

POINT"

Important note: The visual element matters. Showing where content will be deleted from recording

makes it tangible.

THE CAPACITY CONSTRAINT METHOD

What it is: Using genuine operational limits to create believable scarcity.

The Structure:

1\. Explain your actual limitation

2\. Show the numbers (X spots for Y demand)

3\. Historical proof of selling out

4\. "This isn't a sales tactic, it's reality"

Why it works:

Completely believable (provable constraint)

Not your "fault" (external limitation)

Creates competition among buyers

Urgency is logical, not manipulative

Examples from transcript:

Genius Webinars: "Spots are limited to 70 individuals who are ready to do webinars the most effective,

most profit pulling way imaginable... Full disclosure - I have no plans on doing another in person genius

webinar training in the future so this is the ONLY time I can say with 100% certainty that this opportunity

is available for you. So seize it."

AMZ Launchpad: "The bad news... only 300 spots available. The good news... only 300 spots available.

Last time we launched this was November 3rd, 2016. I gave the URL to sign up on the webinar at

1:15pm. By 1:21 we were sold out."

Key insight: When you can show you've enforced scarcity before, current scarcity becomes instantly

credible.

THE EXCLUSIVITY/WHITE GLOVE POSITIONING

What it is: Framing scarcity as quality control and exclusivity rather than artificial limits.

The Structure:

1\. "We can only work with X clients"

2\. "Here's specifically why we can't take more"

3\. "We only accept people who meet Y criteria"

4\. "This ensures quality for everyone"

Why it works:

Makes buyer feel special/qualified

Scarcity seems like a feature, not a bug

Creates aspirational positioning

"Applying" psychology (they're earning it)

Example from transcript:

RGT Marketing: "We've partnered with a company called RGT. We have the exclusive marketing rights

to this powerful service. We only allow Launchpad members in good standing to sign up for this. RGT

has the capacity to only work with 75 brands to get you in the top 3 spots."

Advanced element: The "good standing" requirement adds another layer—you have to qualify FIRST,

then you can access this limited opportunity.

THE "NEVER AGAIN" FRAMING

What it is: Explicitly stating this is the only time this will ever be available at this price/with these terms.

The Structure:

1\. Clear statement: "This is the only time..."

2\. Explain why (pre-launch, beta, special event, etc.)

3\. What changes after deadline

4\. No wiggle room in language

Why it works:

Removes future hope

Makes fence-sitting impossible

Forces decision now

Can reference later when you don't offer it again

Example language from transcript:

"Only time ever offered at this price\!"

"This is the ONLY time I can say with 100% certainty..."

"If you miss this, you might not ever see this offer ever made again"

Critical point: You MUST honor this. If you say "never again" and then offer it again, you destroy all

future credibility.

SCARCITY AS THE FRAME FOR THE ENTIRE WEBINAR

THREE EXAMPLES OF "SCARCITY AS FRAME"

These are masterclass examples where scarcity isn't just the close—it's THE CONCEPT the entire

webinar is built around.

EXAMPLE 1: THE PLAN (Beta Launch Framing)

The Setup: Position the entire webinar as a beta/pre-launch opportunity.

How it works:

Opening: "This is a beta launch"

Throughout: "Testing with limited group first"

Close: "That's why only X spots available"

Everything reinforces the scarcity reason

Why it's powerful: The scarcity makes sense from minute one. You're not adding urgency at the end—

you're explaining it from the beginning.

EXAMPLE 2: AUCTION WEBINAR (Contractual Obligation)

The Setup: Frame entire offer around an external constraint you "have to" honor.

How it works:

"I have a contractual obligation..."

"My hands are tied on this"

"The terms are set by \[external party\]"

Close becomes inevitable, not pushy

Why it's powerful: You're the good guy trying to help them navigate someone else's rules. The scarcity is

"out of your hands" which removes resistance.

EXAMPLE 3: PROSPERITY ALGORITHM (Pre-Release Price)

The Setup: Entire webinar is about pre-launch special before price increases.

How it works:

"Product officially launches \[date\]"

"Retail price is $X"

"Today pre-release: $Y"

Entire pitch is structured around this timeline

Why it's powerful: The scarcity is baked into the product launch itself. It's not arbitrary—it's part of the

natural product lifecycle.

THE PSYCHOLOGICAL PRINCIPLES UNDERLYING SCARCITY

LOSS AVERSION

What it is: People are more motivated to avoid losses than to acquire equivalent gains.

How scarcity leverages it: By creating urgency, you shift the frame from "what will I gain?" to "what

will I lose if I don't act?"

Example: "Miss out on $X in bonuses" is more powerful than "Get $X in bonuses"

SOCIAL PROOF THROUGH SCARCITY

What it is: When something is scarce, people assume it must be valuable (otherwise why would it be

limited?).

How to use it:

"First 25 spots filled in 6 minutes last time"

"700 people tried to register but only 300 got in"

"Sold out" becomes social proof

Example from transcript: "Joe's annual event is $10,000 and over 1000 people last year attempted to

pay that much to get in; 700 were turned away because even though they wanted in, they didn't qualify as

space was limited. Only 300 got to come."

REACTANCE THEORY

What it is: People want what they can't have. When freedom is threatened, they want it more.

How scarcity triggers it: Limiting availability makes people want it more, even if they were lukewarm

before.

Key: This is why artificial scarcity backfires—if they discover the "threat" wasn't real, they get angry at

being manipulated.

THE SCARCITY-VALUE LOOP

What it is: Scarcity increases perceived value, which justifies the scarcity, which increases perceived

value further.

How to use it:

1\. Show high value of offer

2\. Introduce scarcity

3\. Scarcity makes them value it more

4\. Higher value justifies the scarcity

5\. Loop continues

Example: "This is worth $31,250 in real value... which is why we can only offer it to 70 people at this

price."

COMMON SCARCITY MISTAKES TO AVOID

MISTAKE \#1: Scarcity Without Reason

The Error: Just saying "limited time" with no explanation

Why it fails: Feels manipulative, triggers BS detectors

Fix: Always pair scarcity with legitimate reason why

MISTAKE \#2: Unbelievable Limits

The Error: "Only 1000 spots\!" on a digital product

Why it fails: Obviously artificial

Fix: Use believable constraints or don't use quantity limits

MISTAKE \#3: Not Enforcing Your Scarcity

The Error: Offering the deal again after "deadline"

Why it fails: Destroys all future credibility

Fix: Honor your deadlines religiously (or build in flexibility from the start)

MISTAKE \#4: Too Much Scarcity

The Error: "Only 3 spots left\! Price increases in 10 minutes\! Bonuses disappear\! Never offered

again\!"

Why it fails: Overwhelming and unbelievable

Fix: Choose 2-3 scarcity elements maximum

MISTAKE \#5: Hiding the Deadline

The Error: Being vague about when scarcity ends

Why it fails: Reduces urgency (they'll "come back later")

Fix: Specific dates and times, countdown timers, spot counters

THE SCARCITY DECISION TREE

When planning your scarcity, ask:

QUESTION 1: What Type of Scarcity Fits Your Offer?

Physical product/event = quantity limits

Digital product = time limits or bonus expiration

Service = capacity constraints

High-ticket = exclusivity/qualification

QUESTION 2: What's Your Honest Reason Why?

Can't think of one = don't use that type of scarcity

Have multiple reasons = choose the strongest one

Reason feels weak = might not be ready for this scarcity

QUESTION 3: How Will You Prove It?

Screenshots, numbers, historical data?

Live counters or timers?

Transparent explanation?

Can you show the consequences of missing it?

QUESTION 4: What Happens After the Deadline?

Price increases = to what?

Offer closes = forever or reopens later?

Bonuses disappear = what exactly goes away?

Be specific about the change

QUESTION 5: Can You Enforce It?

Will you really close it?

Will you really increase the price?

Are you prepared to turn people away?

If "no" to any = rethink your scarcity

PRACTICAL LANGUAGE TEMPLATES

TEMPLATE 1: Fast Action Taker Reward

"I want to reward the people who take action quickly—the fast action takers like \[names\]. That's why

\[specific scarcity\]. After \[deadline\], \[specific consequence\]."

TEMPLATE 2: Exclusivity Maintenance

"For this to remain special and exclusive, we can only \[limitation\]. If we opened it to everyone, \[negative

outcome\]. That's why \[scarcity\]."

TEMPLATE 3: External Constraint

"Here's the reality: \[external factor\] means we can only \[limitation\]. This isn't a marketing tactic—it's

\[specific reason\]. That's why \[scarcity\]."

TEMPLATE 4: Beta/Testing Phase

"We're looking for \[X number\] of people to \[beta test/be founding members/first group\]. This is the only

time we'll \[offer at this level\] because \[specific reason\]. After \[deadline\], \[specific change\]."

TEMPLATE 5: Historical Proof

"Last time we did this, \[specific outcome\]. We had \[X people\] try to get in, but only \[Y number\] could.

This time we have \[Z spots/timeline\], and based on \[data\], I expect \[scarcity consequence\]."

THE ADVANCED SCARCITY STRATEGIES

STRATEGY 1: Stacking Scarcity Layers

How it works: Combine multiple types of scarcity that reinforce each other.

Example structure:

1\. "Limited to 300 people" (quantity)

2\. "Price increases by $500 after \[date\]" (time + price)

3\. "Fast action bonuses for first 25" (quantity + bonus)

4\. "Offer may close before deadline if we fill up" (uncertainty)

Why it's powerful: Each layer catches different psychological triggers. Some respond to quantity limits,

others to price increases, others to bonus loss.

From transcript example: "The offer expires shortly :) ... Not everyone will get the special $499 pricing

nor all these exclusive bonuses... If you're one of the first 25 customers..."

STRATEGY 2: The Countdown Uncertainty

How it works: You have a deadline, but you might close BEFORE it if you hit capacity.

Language: "We're open until \[date\] OR until we hit \[X number\]—whichever comes first."

Why it's powerful:

Creates double uncertainty

Can't wait until deadline (might close sooner)

Adds competition element

Shows genuine constraint

Example from transcript: AMZ Launchpad: "Last time... I gave the URL at 1:15pm. By 1:21 we were

sold out."

STRATEGY 3: The Replay Edit

How it works: Telling live attendees that certain offers will be removed from the replay.

Structure:

1\. Present special offer during live webinar

2\. "This will be edited out of the replay"

3\. "If you're watching the replay, you already missed this"

4\. Makes live attendance premium

Why it's powerful:

Rewards live attendance

Creates hierarchy of offers

Prevents people from waiting

Adds value to future live webinars

Example from transcript: "Warning\! We have priced this so low we aren't going to mention it on the

webinar... If you watch the replay of this webinar, you'll notice this section will have been edited it."

STRATEGY 4: The Qualification Scarcity

How it works: It's not just scarce—you have to QUALIFY for it.

Structure:

1\. Limited availability (scarcity)

2\. PLUS must meet criteria (qualification)

3\. Creates double barrier

Why it's powerful:

Makes people want it more (reactance)

Feels exclusive, not manipulative

Self-selects serious buyers

Can actually improve customer quality

Example from transcript: "We only allow Launchpad members in good standing to sign up for this."

"Spots are limited to 70 individuals who are ready to do webinars..."

STRATEGY 5: The Historical Scarcity Proof

How it works: Use past enforcement of scarcity to prove current scarcity is real.

Structure:

1\. "Last time we did this..."

2\. Show screenshots/numbers

3\. "It sold out in X minutes"

4\. "Many people missed it"

5\. "This time we have Y spots"

Why it's powerful:

Proves you enforce your limits

Creates FOMO based on real past events

Social proof + scarcity combined

Urgency is backed by evidence

Examples from transcript:

Launchpad: "Last time... sold out by 1:21"

Genius Network: "$10,000 event, 1000 tried to register, 700 turned away"

THE ETHICS OF SCARCITY

THE GOLDEN RULE: Never Fake Scarcity

Why this matters:

Once caught, trust is gone forever

Word spreads fast about fake scarcity

Short-term gains aren't worth long-term reputation damage

Your most valuable asset is credibility

What counts as fake:

Setting arbitrary limits you don't enforce

"Only 3 left\!" that resets daily

Deadlines that extend multiple times

Claiming capacity limits that don't exist

REAL SCARCITY OPTIONS

If you don't have natural scarcity:

1\. CREATE IT THROUGH PROCESS

"I can only take X new clients per month"

"We have to manually onboard each person"

"Our team can only handle Y customers at this level"

2\. USE TIME-BASED SCARCITY

Specific launch periods

Seasonal availability

"Open enrollment" periods

Legitimate beta testing phases

3\. BONUS-BASED SCARCITY

Main offer always available

Special bonuses are time-limited

Easier to enforce, more flexible

4\. PRICE ANCHORING SCARCITY

"Introductory price" that legitimately increases

Early adopter pricing

Founding member rates

Must actually increase the price

THE TRANSPARENCY APPROACH

When in doubt: Be more transparent than you think you need to be.

Examples:

"We're limiting this to 75 people because \[specific operational reason\]"

"This price increases on \[date\] because \[actual reason\]"

"We can't take more than X clients because \[team/capacity/quality reason\]"

Why this works:

Builds trust

Makes scarcity more believable

Differentiates you from manipulative marketers

Creates partnership feeling

PUTTING IT ALL TOGETHER: THE SCARCITY IMPLEMENTATION

CHECKLIST

BEFORE THE WEBINAR

 Decide on type of scarcity (price/quantity/bonus/combination)

 Develop genuine "reason why" for scarcity

 Determine exactly what changes after deadline

 Create proof elements (screenshots, counters, etc.)

 Plan specific language and timing for scarcity reveal

 Decide if scarcity will frame entire webinar or just close

 Set up technical elements (timers, counters, cut-off systems)

 Plan what happens to people who miss deadline (important\!)

DURING THE WEBINAR

 Establish value first (scarcity on low-value offer doesn't work)

 Introduce scarcity with clear "reason why"

 Show proof/believability elements

 Use specific language patterns

 Explain concrete consequences of missing deadline

 Give clear call-to-action

 Repeat deadline/limitation multiple times

 Address questions about scarcity directly

AFTER THE WEBINAR

 ENFORCE THE SCARCITY (most important)

 Follow up with reminders before deadline

 Use countdown language in follow-ups

 Show remaining spots/time in emails

 Close it when you said you would

 Don't reopen unless you built that in from the start

 Use enforcement as proof for next promotion

FINAL INSIGHTS: WHAT MAKES SCARCITY WORK AT THE DEEPEST

LEVEL

INSIGHT \#1: Scarcity Reframes the Decision

Without scarcity: "Should I buy this?"

With scarcity: "Can I afford to lose this opportunity?"

The question changes from gain-focused to loss-focused, which is more motivating.

INSIGHT \#2: Scarcity Forces Decision-Making

The real value: Not manipulation, but removing procrastination.

Many people genuinely want what you're offering but will delay indefinitely without urgency. Scarcity

helps them make the decision they already want to make.

INSIGHT \#3: Scarcity Must Serve the Customer

The reframe: Scarcity isn't about forcing sales—it's about:

Protecting your ability to deliver quality

Rewarding fast action takers

Creating exclusivity that benefits members

Maintaining value for early buyers

When scarcity genuinely serves these purposes, it's ethical and effective.

INSIGHT \#4: The Best Scarcity Is Operational, Not Artificial

The hierarchy:

1\. Best: Real operational limits (team capacity, physical seats, etc.)

2\. Good: Time-based with clear reason (launch period, seasonal, beta)

3\. Okay: Bonus-based (main offer available, extras limited)

4\. Dangerous: Arbitrary limits you don't enforce

INSIGHT \#5: Scarcity Is About Respect

The deepest principle: Scarcity shows respect for:

Your time (can't leave offer open forever)

Your resources (can only serve limited number well)

Their decisiveness (rewards people who act)

The value (exclusivity maintains worth)

When you frame it this way, scarcity becomes service, not manipulation.

THE ULTIMATE SCARCITY PRINCIPLE

From Jason's teaching: Scarcity is the greatest motivator of all time—but only when it's:

1\. Genuine (real limits you enforce)

2\. Justified (clear reason why)

3\. Provable (show the evidence)

4\. Valuable (worth acting on)

5\. Respectful (serves the customer)

Master these five elements, and scarcity becomes your most powerful tool for driving action.

RECOMMENDED REVIEW SCHEDULE

Before each webinar: Review "Reason Why" section + Ethics section

Weekly: Review one advanced strategy section

Monthly: Read through entire document to maintain mastery

After each launch: Note which scarcity elements worked best

KEY QUOTES TO REMEMBER

"Scarcity is the greatest motivator of all time."

"Without a reason why, it just feels manipulative."

"Show the proof it's scarce."

"The only thing that would stop you from signing up right now is you're scared. There is no actual risk,

the only risk is imagined on your part."

"This is the ONLY time I can say with 100% certainty that this opportunity is available for you. So seize

it."
